# Delivery specification

Scope: continuous integration (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`), the Cloudflare Pages deploy of the web app, the desktop release workflow (`.github/workflows/release.yml`), the nightly workflow (`.github/workflows/nightly.yml`), the platforms workflow (`.github/workflows/platforms.yml`), the distribution check `ui/scripts/check-dist.mjs`, the web app's response headers `ui/public/_headers`, the third-party notices and their generator `ui/scripts/notices.mjs`, the npm license check `ui/scripts/check-licenses.mjs`, the source of each distributed build (the commit stamp of every web deployment, Partsmith's own source archive of every web deployment and every release, made by `scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh`, the OCCT source archive of every web deployment and every release, made by `scripts/occt-source-archive.sh`, the archives of the Rust crates and npm packages each build is made from, made by `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, and the same archives with every run's artifacts that hold OCCT's compiled code), what `README.md` tells users and contributors, and the examples corpus (its layout, expected results and runners). Read with [technical design](../design/technical-design.md) sections 4 (build and deployment), 16 (testing) and 17 (guardrails in CI, where humans decide), [architecture.md](../design/architecture.md) section "Testing", ADRs [0003](../adr/0003-pin-occt-and-build-from-source.md), [0006](../adr/0006-protocol-framing-and-schema.md), [0009](../adr/0009-emscripten-c-abi-bridge.md), [0012](../adr/0012-dependency-log-m1.md), [0013](../adr/0013-oxc-tooling-and-theme-stylesheet.md), [0014](../adr/0014-desktop-host-and-dependencies.md), [0028](../adr/0028-single-license-apache-2.md), [0031](../adr/0031-self-hosted-geist-and-jetbrains-mono-for-code.md), [0033](../adr/0033-notices-and-npm-license-check-from-the-lock-files.md), [0034](../adr/0034-occt-linking-and-lgpl-obligations.md), [0038](../adr/0038-platforms-and-browser-engines-tested-weekly-outside-the-gate.md) and [0046](../adr/0046-examples-exported-to-every-format.md), and the specs whose checks CI runs: [cli.md](cli.md) (`schema`, `rebuild --json`, `export --json`), [protocol.md](protocol.md) 2.9 and 2.10, [ui.md](ui.md) 2.2 and 6, [python-api.md](python-api.md) 3.9, [features.md](features.md) (examples) and [desktop.md](desktop.md).

This spec is the definition of done for delivery. The checks of section 6 are its acceptance tests; implementers do not weaken them.

## 1. Purpose and scope

Every change is built and tested on Linux, in Chromium, before it merges, and every merge to `main` deploys the web app, so reviewers always try the current state (technical design section 4). The other platforms the project ships, Windows and macOS, and the other browser engines it supports, Firefox and WebKit (ui.md 1.1), are tested on every change too: CI builds and tests the crates whose code differs by platform and the examples on Windows and macOS, and runs the end-to-end tests tagged `@engines` in Firefox and WebKit (2.14). These jobs ran in the platforms workflow, outside the gate, until each had passed twice on `main`. They moved into CI in wave H (2.14, ADR 0038). The platforms workflow keeps the timing of the full rebuild on macOS, on demand and every week, outside the gate. A release waits for it (3.6). Nothing reaches users without a green build, and desktop releases wait for a human (technical design section 17).

### 1.1 In this milestone

- CI jobs: OCCT native and WebAssembly builds with caches; Rust format, lint and tests (including the desktop crate and the examples runner); `cargo-deny`; kernel and sketch tests and the engine's panic containment on WebAssembly; the WebAssembly core built with `scripts/build-wasm.sh` and its results compared with a native session's over the engine fixtures and the examples (engine.md test 154); UI lint, typecheck, unit tests and script tests, and the licenses of the npm packages that ship checked against the allow list of `deny.toml` (2.10); the web build with the distribution check; Playwright end-to-end tests against the real WebAssembly core; schema, generated-type and third-party notices drift; Python package tests including the examples' Python twins, and the wheel built and installed on CPython 3.12 and 3.13 (python-api.md test 76); a Linux desktop build; the tests of `partsmith-occt-sys`, `partsmith-engine` and `partsmith-desktop`, the examples runner and clippy for those crates on Windows (`windows-2025`) and macOS (`macos-15`), with OCCT built and cached on each as the release builds it; and the end-to-end tests tagged `@engines` in Firefox and WebKit (ui.md 6.8, 2.14).
- Third-party notices for the desktop app and the web app, generated from the lock files, committed, checked in CI and shipped in the desktop bundles, the web build and each release, the code the Windows installer adds included (2.9).
- The source of every distributed build (ADR 0034, option A, with the strictest reading of LGPL 2.1 sections 4 and 6 that the owner took on 2026-11-04): a commit stamp, Partsmith's own source archive, the OCCT source archive and the archives of the Rust crates and npm packages the web build is made from in every web deployment, all of which `deploy` requires, and Partsmith's own source archive, the OCCT source archive and the archives of the Rust crates and npm packages the desktop bundles are made from in every release (2.13). Every CI, platforms and release run that uploads an artifact holding OCCT's compiled code first uploads the same archives of that artifact's scope, kept 8 days, one day longer than the artifact (the owner's decisions of 2026-11-05 and the owner's rule of that date, 2.13). The release's crate archive also holds `tauri-cli` and the crates of its own lock file. Both npm archives also hold lightningcss's source and the crates under MPL-2.0 that native packages compile in, and every npm package they hold passes the license check of 2.10 (the owner's decisions of 2026-11-05).
- A nightly run of the sketch property tests at 4096 cases, which skips a night when `main` has not changed (2.11).
- A platforms workflow, on demand and every week, that gates no merge, deploy or release run (2.14): it times `bench50` from an empty cache in a release build on `macos-15`, the macOS arm64 runner that judges technical design section 14's full-rebuild gate (ADR 0054). Its Windows, macOS and engine jobs passed on `main` twice in a row, in platforms runs 14 and 15. The jobs moved into CI in wave H (2.14, The move). `full-rebuild` never moves.
- What `README.md` says, and the release checklist (2.12, 3.6).
- Cloudflare Pages: production on every push to `main`, a preview per pull request, through the Wrangler action, skipped cleanly when the secrets are absent (forks, unconfigured repositories).
- The 25 MiB per-file gate on `ui/dist`, with the measured sizes and the plan when the core outgrows it (3.5).
- The initial JavaScript limit of ui.md 5, 250 KB gzipped, checked on `ui/dist` by the same script (2.6).
- `ui/public/_headers` with the cross-origin isolation headers, the content security policy and two hardening headers (2.7).
- The desktop release workflow: on a version tag, bundles on Linux, Windows and macOS with the Tauri bundler and a draft GitHub Release that a human publishes.
- The Visual C++ runtime and the C++ standard library linked statically into every Windows build (ADR 0038 option (i), the owner's decision of 2026-11-04), and the Universal C Runtime taken from Windows by every Windows program (the owner's decision of 2026-11-05, ADR 0038's amendment of that day), with a check that the Windows app imports no DLL of the Visual C++ Redistributable and imports the Universal C Runtime of Windows, in CI and in each release, and the same check of the CLI in CI (3.6).
- The Windows installer compressed with zlib, with no code under the Common Public License, and the static Visual C++ runtime named in the desktop notices (the owner's decisions of 2026-11-05, 2.9 input 6, desktop.md 2.8).
- The examples corpus: one rule for every file under `examples/`, the expected-results format, the Rust runner with a documented way to regenerate expected files, the feature coverage check and the Python twin checks (section 2.8).
- Every action pinned to an exact version, and every job capped by `timeout-minutes` (2.4, 2.5, 2.11, 2.14).

### 1.2 Later (not in this milestone)

| Item | What it needs |
| --- | --- |
| Code signing (Windows, macOS) and notarization | Certificates and accounts, secrets (desktop.md 1.2), human decision on ownership. They plug into the `bundle` job's environment (3.6). |
| Auto-update | `tauri-plugin-updater`, the signing key secrets, `bundle.createUpdaterArtifacts` and a `latest.json` asset on the release (desktop.md 1.2). |
| Line and branch coverage with `cargo-llvm-cov` (technical design section 16, 90 percent target for core crates) | A per-crate threshold decision and a job; it doubles test time, so it waits until the core crates are complete. |
| The model corpus (500+ models), held-out corpus, naming metric and differential build123d tests (technical design sections 16 and 17) | The corpus itself, baseline storage and a build123d environment. |
| Performance regression gates and visual tests on fixed hardware | A self-hosted runner on the reference machine (ui.md 6.6 and 6.7, and the web column of engine.md section 5). |
| `cargo-semver-checks`, fuzzing (`cargo fuzz`) | Public API baselines after the first release; a nightly toolchain job. |
| Serving oversized core files from Cloudflare R2 | See 3.5 step 3. |
| Desktop end-to-end tests | A `tauri-driver` harness (desktop.md 1.2). |
| Pinning actions to commit SHAs | Tags are mutable; SHA pins with a comment naming the version are stronger. Needs a decision and an update tool (Dependabot or Renovate). |
| Published Python wheels and CLI downloads | A publishing job (PyPI trusted publishing for the wheel), notices for each artifact like those of 2.9 and a way to its source like that of 2.13 (both link OCCT statically, ADR 0034), and a human decision. |

### 2.2 Pinned actions and tools

Every `uses:` names an exact release tag. Actions already in `ci.yml` keep their version; the three that name only a major (`actions/checkout@v7`, `actions/cache@v6`, `actions/cache/restore@v6`) are pinned to the current release of that major. `mymindstorm/setup-emsdk` publishes major tags only, so `v16` is its exact release. `actions/cache/restore` and `actions/cache/save` are actions in folders of the `actions/cache` repository. They are released with it under the same tag and share its row.

| Action | Version | Used by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `actions/checkout` | `v7.0.1` | every job |
| `actions/cache`, `actions/cache/restore`, `actions/cache/save` | `v6.1.0` | OCCT caches, the OCCT source archive's cache (2.13), `tauri-cli` cache, the apt archives of the browser installs in `e2e` and `e2e-engines` and of the Tauri Linux packages in `rust`, `desktop` and `release.yml`'s `bundle` (3.2) |
| `actions/setup-node` | `v7.1.0` | jobs that run pnpm, and the `sources` jobs (2.13) |
| `pnpm/action-setup` | `v6.1.0` | jobs that run pnpm |
| `Swatinem/rust-cache` | `v2.9.2` | jobs that run cargo |
| `EmbarkStudios/cargo-deny-action` | `v2.1.1` | `deny` |
| `mymindstorm/setup-emsdk` | `v16` | `occt-wasm`, `kernel-wasm`, `core-wasm` |
| `actions/upload-artifact` | `v7.0.2` | `sources` of `ci.yml` and `release.yml`, `core-wasm`, `web`, `e2e`, `e2e-engines`, `bundle` |
| `actions/download-artifact` | `v8.0.2` | `web`, `e2e`, `deploy`, `draft`, `e2e-engines` |
| `actions/setup-python` | `v7.0.0` | `python` |
| `cloudflare/wrangler-action` | `v4.1.3` | `deploy` |
| `ilammy/msvc-dev-cmd` | `v1.13.0` | `bundle`, `occt-platforms` and `rust-platforms` on Windows |

| Tool | Version | Where it is pinned |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Rust | 1.97.0 | `rust-toolchain.toml` |
| Emscripten | 4.0.15 | `scripts/emsdk-version.sh` (and the `setup-emsdk` inputs) |
| OCCT | 7.9.3 | `scripts/occt-version.sh` |
| Node | 22 | `node-version` inputs (as `ci.yml`) |
| pnpm | 10.28.0 | `ui/package.json` `packageManager` |
| Playwright Chromium | from `@playwright/test` 1.64.0 (Chromium 156.0.8078.4) | `ui/pnpm-lock.yaml` |
| Playwright Firefox and WebKit | from `@playwright/test` 1.64.0 (Firefox 157.0 and WebKit 27.2, the `browsers.json` of `playwright-core` 1.64.0), installed only by `e2e-engines` (2.14) | `ui/pnpm-lock.yaml` |
| Python | 3.12 and 3.13 | `python-version` input of the `python` job, 3.13 first and 3.12 last, so 3.12 is the default `python` and `python3.13` is on `PATH` |
| maturin | 1.15.0 | `pip install maturin==1.15.0` |
| pytest | 9.1.1 | `pip install pytest==9.1.1` |
| `tauri-cli` | 2.12.2 | `cargo install tauri-cli --version 2.12.2 --locked` |
| Wrangler | 4.149.0 | `wranglerVersion` input |

Runners: `ubuntu-24.04` for every CI job but `occt-platforms` and `rust-platforms`, which run on `windows-2025` and `macos-15` (2.14); `ubuntu-24.04`, `windows-2025` and `macos-15` for release bundles; `macos-15` for the platforms workflow (2.14).

### 2.3 Secrets and variables

| Name | Kind | Used by | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` | repository secret | `deploy` | API token with only the permission "Account, Cloudflare Pages, Edit". |
| `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` | repository secret | `deploy` | The Cloudflare account that owns the Pages project. |
| `CLOUDFLARE_PAGES_PROJECT` | repository variable, optional | `deploy` | Pages project name; `partsmith` when unset. |
| `GITHUB_TOKEN` | automatic | `verify`, `draft` | Reads CI runs (`actions: read`) and creates the draft release (`contents: write`). |

No other secret exists in this milestone. Signing and updater secrets are later (1.2).

### 2.4 CI workflow (`ci.yml`)

Triggers `push` to `main` and `pull_request`; `concurrency` `ci-${{ github.ref }}` with `cancel-in-progress: true`; workflow `permissions: contents: read`; `env` `CARGO_TERM_COLOR: always`, `OCCT_ROOT: ${{ github.workspace }}/.occt/native` and `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG: "0"` (debug builds and tests without debug info, 3.2). Jobs:

| Job | Needs | What it runs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `occt-native` | | Restores or builds OCCT native into `.occt/native` (unchanged). It makes no OCCT source archive: the `sources` job makes it (2.13, The archive in every deployment). |
| `occt-wasm` | | Restores or builds OCCT for WebAssembly into `.occt/wasm` (unchanged). |
| `kernel-wasm` | `occt-wasm` | `cargo test -p partsmith-occt-sys --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten`, then `cargo test -p partsmith-sketch --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten` (the sketch suite under Node, sketch.md 3.12 and test 255) and `cargo test -p partsmith-engine --lib --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten dispatch::` (the engine's panic containment under Node, protocol.md test 84), each in a step of its own. |
| `rust` | `occt-native` | Frees disk space and installs the Tauri Linux packages in the step `Install the Tauri Linux packages`, between the steps of the apt cache of 3.2, then `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace`, and prints the free disk space after the tests, also when they fail. The workspace includes `partsmith-desktop` and the examples runner. |
| `deny` | | `cargo-deny` with `deny.toml` (unchanged action; the policy now covers the Tauri tree, 3.2). |
| `ui` | | In `ui/`: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm test:scripts`, `pnpm check:licenses` (2.10). |
| `schema` | `occt-native` | `pnpm -C ui install --frozen-lockfile` (step `id: install`), `cargo run -p partsmith-cli -- schema --write`, `pnpm -C ui generate:protocol`, the drift check of 3.4, then `pnpm -C ui check:notices` (2.9) in a step with `if: ${{ !cancelled() && steps.install.outcome == 'success' }}`, so that a failure of the schema steps or the drift check does not hide a notices failure, while the notices check, which reads `ui/node_modules`, never runs without the packages. The install comes first because it needs nothing the schema steps make. |
| `sources` | | Named `Source archives (web)`. Writes the web scope's source archives into `sources/` and uploads them as the artifact `sources-web`, kept 8 days, before any job of the run uploads an artifact that holds OCCT's compiled code (2.13, The source with every artifact): the OCCT source archive from its cache, which the job makes and saves on a miss, Partsmith's own source archive (`scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh`), and the parts of the web build's two dependency archives with their `SHA256SUMS`. It needs no job and starts at once. |
| `core-wasm` | `occt-native`, `occt-wasm`, `sources` | Restores both OCCT caches, sets up Emscripten 4.0.15, runs `scripts/build-wasm.sh` with `OCCT_ROOT_WASM32_UNKNOWN_EMSCRIPTEN=$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/.occt/wasm`, then `cargo test -p partsmith-wasm --test integration web_module::` (the Node round trips of `engine_info` and `list_uses` against the module just built) with `PARTSMITH_REQUIRE_WEB_MODULE=1`, so the test fails instead of skipping when Node or the module is missing, then `cargo test -p partsmith-wasm --test integration web_parity:: -- --ignored` (engine.md test 154: the module against a native session over the engine fixtures and the examples; ignored by default, and failing instead of skipping when it cannot run), and uploads `ui/public/core/` and `ui/public/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` as the artifact `core-wasm` (one `path` per line, so the artifact is rooted at their common folder `ui/public/` and holds `core/` and the web notices, which go wherever the core goes; retention 7 days, `if-no-files-found: error`). It needs `sources`. The run then holds `sources-web` before this upload (2.13). |
| `web` | `core-wasm`, `sources` | Downloads `core-wasm` into `ui/public/` (the notices file it writes is the checkout's, from the same commit), then in `ui/`: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm build`, the step `Write the source stamp`, which writes `dist/SOURCE.txt` (2.13), a step that downloads the artifact `sources-web` into `ui/dist` (`actions/download-artifact@v8.0.2` with `name: sources-web` and `path: ui/dist`), which adds `occt-{OCCT_VERSION}-source.tar.gz`, `partsmith-source.tar.gz` and `dependencies/` with the parts of the web build's two dependency archives and their `SHA256SUMS` (2.13, The source with every artifact), `pnpm check:dist`, which holds each of them to the 25 MiB limit with every other file, then checks that `dist/core/partsmith_core.wasm` and `dist/core/partsmith_core.mjs` exist (`check:dist` does not look for the core); uploads `ui/dist/` as the artifact `web-dist` (retention 7 days, `if-no-files-found: error`). It restores no cache and makes none of the archives itself. |
| `e2e` | `core-wasm` | Downloads `core-wasm` into `ui/public/`, then in `ui/`: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, the step `Install chromium and its system packages`, which runs `pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps chromium` with `timeout-minutes: 10` between the steps of the apt cache of 3.2, as `e2e-engines` installs its engine (2.14), `pnpm e2e` (with no `--project`, so Playwright runs its one default project, `chromium`, which runs every E-test, ui.md 6.8). On failure uploads `ui/playwright-report/` and `ui/test-results/` as the artifact `playwright-report` (retention 7 days: its traces can hold the files the page loaded, the core among them, whose source the run's `sources-web` holds, 2.13). An install that ends at its timeout is the mirror's. Open issue 16 records it. The job is rerun as 2.14 says for `e2e-engines`' rows. |
| `python` | `occt-native` | Python 3.12 and 3.13 (`actions/setup-python@v7.0.0` with `python-version` `3.13` and then `3.12`, one per line), a virtual environment made with 3.12, `pip install maturin==1.15.0 pytest==9.1.1`, `maturin develop --release` in `python/`, `pytest python/tests`, then `pytest python/tests -m budgets -s` in a step named `Measure the Python budgets` (python-api.md section 5: one line per row; a row over its budget warns and does not fail, on the runner as on any machine but a strict run on the reference machine, while a row that cannot be measured fails the job), then `pytest python/tests -m packaging` (python-api.md test 76: `maturin build --release` gives one abi3 wheel, which installs and imports on `python3.12` and `python3.13`). The wheel build uses the profile and features of `maturin develop --release`, so Cargo reuses the engine crates that step compiled; at most `pyo3` and `partsmith-py` compile again. |
| `desktop` | `occt-native` | The Tauri Linux packages, installed as in `rust` between the steps of the apt cache of 3.2, `pnpm -C ui install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm -C ui build` (no WebAssembly core: the desktop app does not load it), `tauri-cli` from the cache or `cargo install`, `cargo tauri build --bundles deb` in `desktop/src-tauri`, then the package check of section 6, test 12. |
| `occt-platforms` | | Named `OCCT (${{ matrix.runner }})`, on `windows-2025` and `macos-15`: restores or builds OCCT native with the steps and cache key of `release.yml`'s `bundle` job (2.14). |
| `rust-platforms` | `occt-platforms`, with `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}` | Named `Rust tests (${{ matrix.runner }})`, on the same runners: the tests of `partsmith-occt-sys`, `partsmith-engine` and `partsmith-desktop`, the examples runner and clippy for those crates, and on Windows the import checks of the app and the CLI (2.14, 3.6). |
| `e2e-engines` | `core-wasm` | Named `End-to-end tests (${{ matrix.browser }})`, on `ubuntu-24.04`: the tests tagged `@engines` in Firefox and in WebKit, against this run's `core-wasm` (2.14, ui.md 6.8). |
| `deploy` | the jobs of its `needs` (below) | The Pages deploy of 3.3, on `pull_request` and on pushes to `main`. |

Every job sets `timeout-minutes`, so a hung job ends long before GitHub's default of 360 minutes: 150 for `occt-native`, `occt-wasm` and `occt-platforms`, which build OCCT on a cache miss (the cold builds of 2026-10-13 took 56 and 88 minutes, and those of `occt-platforms` 48 minutes on Windows and 21 on macOS on 2026-10-26; with the static runtime, the Windows build took 1 h 10 min 21 s in platforms run 14, open issue 1 (e)), and 60 for every other job, nearly four times the longest run of any of them so far but three (`e2e`, 16 min 4 s in CI run 28). `rust` took 31 min 53 s in the CI run on wave H2's branch (42218307369), 26 min 26 s of it in its Tauri install, before the apt cache of 3.2. `e2e-engines`' WebKit row took 54 min 54 s in platforms run 3, before its browser install got its 10 minutes (2.14). `rust-platforms`' Windows row ran until its 60 minutes ended it in platforms run 1, when its tests hung (open issue 1 (g)). Since then its `crates` step has 20 minutes of its own, and `e2e-engines`' browser install has 10 (2.14). Since wave H2, `e2e`'s browser install has 10 too (3.2). The Tauri installs of `rust` and `desktop` have no step timeout: their jobs' 60 minutes bound them (3.2). Every job that needs OCCT restores its cache with `actions/cache/restore@v6.1.0` and `fail-on-cache-miss: true`, as `rust` and `kernel-wasm` do today. Every job that compiles with cargo uses `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2.9.2` after `rustup show active-toolchain || rustup toolchain install`. The `sources` jobs run only `cargo vendor` and `cargo metadata` and take the toolchain step alone (2.13). Every job that runs pnpm sets up pnpm with `package_json_file: ui/package.json` and Node 22 with the pnpm cache keyed on `ui/pnpm-lock.yaml`, as `ui` does today.

`occt-platforms` and `rust-platforms` run on `windows-2025` and `macos-15`, and `e2e-engines` runs the engine subset in Firefox and WebKit. Every other job of `ci.yml` runs on Linux, and `e2e` runs every E-test in Chromium. The three came from the platforms workflow in wave H, once each had passed its two latest runs on `main`. They joined `deploy.needs` (2.14, The move).

The `deploy` job, exactly in structure:

```yaml
deploy:
  name: Deploy to Cloudflare Pages
  needs: [kernel-wasm, rust, deny, ui, schema, web, e2e, python, desktop, occt-platforms, rust-platforms, e2e-engines]
  if: github.event_name == 'pull_request' || github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
  runs-on: ubuntu-24.04
  timeout-minutes: 60
  environment:
    name: ${{ github.event_name == 'pull_request' && 'preview' || 'production' }}
    url: ${{ steps.pages.outputs.pages-deployment-alias-url || steps.pages.outputs.deployment-url }}
  env:
    CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN }}
    CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID }}
  steps:
    - id: secrets
      run: |
        if [ -n "$CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN" ] && [ -n "$CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID" ]; then
          echo "available=true" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
        else
          echo "available=false" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          echo "::notice title=Deploy skipped::Cloudflare secrets are not available to this run (a fork, or the repository is not configured), so nothing was deployed."
        fi
    - if: steps.secrets.outputs.available == 'true'
      uses: actions/checkout@v7.0.1
    - if: steps.secrets.outputs.available == 'true'
      uses: actions/download-artifact@v8.0.2
      with:
        name: web-dist
        path: ui/dist
    - if: steps.secrets.outputs.available == 'true'
      name: Check the source stamp
      run: |
        if ! grep -qxF "Commit: $GITHUB_SHA" ui/dist/SOURCE.txt 2>/dev/null; then
          echo "::error title=Source stamp missing::ui/dist/SOURCE.txt does not name the commit $GITHUB_SHA, so nothing was deployed. The web job writes it (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13)."
          exit 1
        fi
    - if: steps.secrets.outputs.available == 'true'
      name: Check the OCCT source archive
      run: |
        source scripts/occt-version.sh
        archive="ui/dist/occt-$OCCT_VERSION-source.tar.gz"
        if ! scripts/check-source-archive.sh "$archive" "$OCCT_COMMIT"; then
          echo "::error title=OCCT source missing::$archive is missing or does not hold the source of the OCCT commit $OCCT_COMMIT, so nothing was deployed. The sources job writes it, and the web job puts it into the build (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13)."
          exit 1
        fi
    - if: steps.secrets.outputs.available == 'true'
      name: Check the dependency source archives
      run: |
        if ! (cd ui/dist/dependencies &&
          grep -q '  rust-crates-web\.tar\.gz\.part-00$' SHA256SUMS &&
          grep -q '  npm-packages-web\.tar\.part-00$' SHA256SUMS &&
          sha256sum --check --strict --quiet SHA256SUMS &&
          for part in *.part-*; do cut -c 67- SHA256SUMS | grep -qxF -- "$part" || exit 1; done &&
          cat rust-crates-web.tar.gz.part-* | gzip -t &&
          cat npm-packages-web.tar.part-* | tar -t >/dev/null) >/dev/null 2>&1; then
          echo "::error title=Dependency source missing::ui/dist/dependencies does not hold every part of rust-crates-web.tar.gz and npm-packages-web.tar with the SHA-256 that its SHA256SUMS lists, so nothing was deployed. The sources job writes them, and the web job puts them into the build (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13)."
          exit 1
        fi
    - if: steps.secrets.outputs.available == 'true'
      name: Check the Partsmith source archive
      run: |
        archive=ui/dist/partsmith-source.tar.gz
        if ! scripts/check-source-archive.sh "$archive" "$GITHUB_SHA"; then
          echo "::error title=Partsmith source missing::$archive is missing or does not hold the source of the commit $GITHUB_SHA, so nothing was deployed. The sources job writes it, and the web job puts it into the build (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13)."
          exit 1
        fi
    - if: steps.secrets.outputs.available == 'true'
      id: pages
      uses: cloudflare/wrangler-action@v4.1.3
      with:
        apiToken: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN }}
        accountId: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID }}
        wranglerVersion: "4.149.0"
        command: >-
          pages deploy ui/dist
          --project-name=${{ vars.CLOUDFLARE_PAGES_PROJECT || 'partsmith' }}
          --branch=${{ github.event_name == 'pull_request' && github.head_ref || 'main' }}
          --commit-hash=${{ github.event.pull_request.head.sha || github.sha }}
    - if: steps.secrets.outputs.available == 'true'
      run: echo "Deployed to ${{ steps.pages.outputs.pages-deployment-alias-url || steps.pages.outputs.deployment-url }}" >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
```

### 2.5 Release workflow (`release.yml`)

Triggers: `push` of tags matching `v[0-9]+.[0-9]+.[0-9]+`, and `workflow_dispatch` (a dry run that uploads the bundles with their source artifact, without a release). Workflow `permissions: contents: read`; `concurrency` `release-${{ github.ref }}` with `cancel-in-progress: false`. `timeout-minutes` is 150 for `bundle`, above the cold release budget of section 5 (120 minutes, OCCT built on the runner), so a slow first build is not cut off while a hung job still ends long before GitHub's default of 360 minutes; and 60 for `verify`, `sources` and `draft`.

| Job | Needs | Runs on | What it runs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `verify` | | `ubuntu-24.04` | For a tag only (`permissions: contents: read, actions: read`), with `shell: bash`, whose `pipefail` makes a failing `cargo metadata` fail the step instead of reading as an empty version: the tag without `v` is compared separately with the version of `partsmith-desktop` (`cargo metadata --no-deps --format-version 1`) and with the version of `ui/package.json`, and each wrong version is reported once with the message of section 4 (a wrong version both files share gives one message); and `gh run list --workflow ci.yml --commit "$GITHUB_SHA" --status success --limit 1 --json databaseId --jq length` prints `1`. |
| `sources` | `verify` | `ubuntu-24.04` | Named `Source archives (desktop)`, for a tag and for a dry run, with `if: ${{ !cancelled() && (needs.verify.result == 'success' \|\| needs.verify.result == 'skipped') }}`. It is `ci.yml`'s `sources` with the desktop scope and without the archive cache. It makes the OCCT source archive from the pinned tag in every run (2.13, The source with every artifact). It uploads the artifact `sources-desktop`, kept 8 days: the OCCT source archive, Partsmith's own source archive and the parts of the desktop bundles' two dependency archives. A lock file whose tarballs the script cannot download or check, or whose crates it cannot vendor, then fails the dry run and not the tag. |
| `bundle` | `verify`, `sources` | matrix of 3.6 | It has `if: ${{ !cancelled() && needs.sources.result == 'success' }}`. It runs in a dry run, where `verify` is skipped. It never runs before the run has uploaded `sources-desktop` (2.13). On Linux, the Tauri packages, after a restore of the apt archives that `ci.yml` saved on `main` (3.2). OCCT native from its cache or built, on Linux with `ninja-build` installed only for that build, the UI build, `tauri-cli`, `cargo tauri build --bundles <matrix bundles>`, on Windows the step `Check the imports of the app` (3.6, The C runtime on Windows), copies the bundle files into one flat `bundles/` folder, and uploads it as the artifact `bundle-<runner.os>`, kept 7 days (3.6, step 6). `fail-fast: false`. |
| `draft` | `bundle`, `sources` | `ubuntu-24.04` | For a tag only (`permissions: contents: write`): checks out the tagged commit, downloads every `bundle-*` artifact into `release/` (merged, so `release/` holds the flat bundle files), downloads `sources-desktop` into `release/` (`actions/download-artifact@v8.0.2` with `name: sources-desktop` and `path: release`), which adds `occt-{OCCT_VERSION}-source.tar.gz`, `partsmith-source.tar.gz` and the parts of the desktop bundles' two dependency archives (2.13), copies `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` into `release/` (2.9), writes `release/SHA256SUMS` (`sha256sum` of each file, sorted by name, so it covers the notices, both source archives and every part too), and runs `gh release create "$GITHUB_REF_NAME" --draft --verify-tag --title "Partsmith $VERSION" --generate-notes release/*`. It makes none of the archives again. It needs neither the toolchain nor Node. |

The `bundle` job bundles without running a test. The tests of its Windows and macOS builds are CI's (2.14), whose `occt-platforms` and `rust-platforms` jobs take the steps of 3.6 steps 1 and 2 (the same `uses` and `run` texts and the same OCCT cache key, test 61; `occt-platforms` sets up the compiler only on a cache miss, where it builds), so both workflows build the same OCCT, either restores what the other saved on `main`, and a fix the platform jobs need applies to the bundles too. A release waits for a passing platforms run on its commit (3.6, checklist item 2).

### 2.6 Distribution check (`ui/scripts/check-dist.mjs`)

An ES module with no dependencies beyond Node's standard library and the notices generator, from which it takes one constant, usable as a library and as a command:

```js
/** Cloudflare Pages' per-file limit: 25 MiB. */
export const PAGES_FILE_LIMIT = 26_214_400

/** The initial JavaScript limit of ui.md section 5: 250 KB gzipped, with 1 KB = 1,000 bytes. */
export const INITIAL_JAVASCRIPT_LIMIT = 250_000

/** The exact text of `_headers` (2.7). */
export const EXPECTED_HEADERS =
  '/*\n' +
  '  Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin\n' +
  '  Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy: require-corp\n' +
  "  Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'; style-src 'self'; font-src 'self'; img-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'; worker-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'\n" +
  '  X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff\n' +
  '  Referrer-Policy: no-referrer\n'

/** The first line of the web app's notices (2.9), defined once in the generator. */
export { WEB_NOTICES_HEADING } from './notices.mjs'

/**
 * Every regular file under `directory` (recursively, not following symbolic links) larger than `limit` bytes,
 * as `{ path, size }` with `path` relative to `directory` using `/`, sorted by path.
 */
export async function oversizedFiles(directory, limit = PAGES_FILE_LIMIT)

/**
 * The initial JavaScript of `directory` (below) as `{ files, unresolved }`. `files` lists each initial script once as
 * `{ path, size, gzippedSize }`, sorted by path, with `path` as `oversizedFiles` writes it, `size` its length in bytes
 * and `gzippedSize` the length of `gzipSync(contents, { level: 9 })` from `node:zlib`. `unresolved` lists each distinct
 * reference of `index.html` that is not a file of the build, in order of first appearance.
 */
export async function initialJavaScript(directory)

/**
 * The initial JavaScript problems of `directory` as messages (section 4): one per unresolved reference, in order,
 * then the limit message when the `gzippedSize` values of its files sum to more than `limit` bytes.
 */
export async function initialJavaScriptProblems(directory, limit = INITIAL_JAVASCRIPT_LIMIT)

/**
 * Every problem that keeps `directory` from deploying, as messages (section 4): oversized files by path, then
 * `_headers`, then the notices, then `initialJavaScriptProblems(directory)`; only the missing-directory message when
 * `directory` does not exist; empty when it is ready.
 */
export async function checkDist(directory)
```

Command: `node scripts/check-dist.mjs [directory]` (default `dist`, relative to the working directory). `ui/package.json` gains `"check:dist": "node scripts/check-dist.mjs dist"` and `"test:scripts": "node --test \"scripts/**/*.test.mjs\""`.

- Exit 0 and two lines on standard output, `{directory} is ready for Cloudflare Pages: {n} files, the largest is {path} ({size} bytes).` and `Initial JavaScript: {total} bytes gzipped, {percent} percent of the limit of 250000 bytes.`, when `checkDist` returns no problems. `{total}` is the sum of the `gzippedSize` values of `initialJavaScript(directory).files` (0 when there are none) and `{percent}` is `(total * 100 / INITIAL_JAVASCRIPT_LIMIT).toFixed(1)`.
- Exit 1 with every problem on standard error, one per line, when it returns any.
- Exit 2 with `usage: node scripts/check-dist.mjs [directory]` when given more than one argument.

`build-wasm.sh` keeps its informational size line and warning; the gate is this script.

**Notices.** The build must hold the web app's notices (2.9): a regular file `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` directly in `directory` (not a symbolic link), whose first line, the text before the first LF, equals `WEB_NOTICES_HEADING`. Vite copies it from `ui/public`. A missing file gives the missing-notices message of section 4, and any other first line, including the desktop heading, the wrong-notices message. The check reads only the first line; whether the content is current is the job of `check:notices` in CI (2.9).

**Initial JavaScript** is the JavaScript a browser downloads and runs before the app is usable (ui.md 3.1 and 5). In `directory` it is:

1. Every file that the regular file `index.html` names in the `src` of a `<script>` tag or in the `href` of a `<link>` tag whose `rel` is `modulepreload`. Vite writes a `modulepreload` link for every chunk the entry script imports statically, directly or through other chunks, so these tags cover the whole static import graph; `vite.config.ts` keeps Vite's default `build.modulePreload` for that reason (test 32). Tags are read as Vite writes them: lowercase tag and attribute names, attribute values in double quotes, attributes in any order. An inline script (a `<script>` without `src`) is part of `index.html` and does not count, and neither its text nor the text of an HTML comment is read for tags. Without a regular file `index.html`, nothing is read.
2. The core worker script: every regular file whose path starts with `assets/core.worker-`, ends with `.js` and contains no further `/`. That is the name Vite gives `src/core/core.worker.ts` (its default `assets/[name]-[hash].js`). The app starts the worker during startup (ui.md 3.1), before it is usable, so its script counts; a change that renames the worker entry or its output name changes this pattern and test 32 too.

A reference of `index.html` is a file of the build when, with one leading `/` or `./` removed, it equals the `path` of a regular file of `directory` as `oversizedFiles` writes paths. Any other reference is unresolved: a URL with a scheme or starting with `//`, a missing file, a folder, a symbolic link, or a path with a query or a fragment. Each file counts once, even when it is named twice or is both referenced and the worker script.

Not initial: the core module under `core/` (`partsmith_core.mjs` and `partsmith_core.wasm`), which the worker loads and ui.md 5 excludes (3.5 gates its size); source maps; stylesheets; and every other `.js` file, such as a chunk Vite splits off for a dynamic `import()`.

The measure is the sum of each file's gzipped length, each file compressed on its own (it is a separate response) with `gzipSync` at level 9, zlib's highest, the same as `gzip -9`. 1 KB is 1,000 bytes, as in the sizes Vite prints after a build, so the limit is 250,000 bytes, and a total of exactly 250,000 bytes passes. Pages compresses responses itself, with its own encodings and levels, so the measure is a fixed and reproducible stand-in for the transfer size, not the number of bytes served.

**Measured (2026-10-13, a `vite build` of the current tree):** `index.html` names one script, `assets/index-*.js` (756,011 bytes, 232,509 gzipped), and no `modulepreload` link, because the app is one chunk; `assets/core.worker-*.js` is 7,286 bytes, 2,839 gzipped. The initial JavaScript is 235,348 bytes gzipped, 94.1 percent of the limit and 14,652 bytes under it (open issue 8). The gzip column of Vite's build report comes from another compressor and reads about 1 percent higher (235.11 kB for `assets/index-*.js`); the check's number is the one the limit applies to.

### 2.7 `ui/public/_headers`

Exactly `EXPECTED_HEADERS` (2.6): the line `/*`, then five headers, one per line, each indented by two spaces (409 bytes, LF line endings, trailing newline; its longest line, 253 characters, is far under Pages' limit of 2,000). Vite copies it to `ui/dist/_headers`, and Pages applies it to every path. Pages sends `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` by default, and a header that `_headers` names replaces the default one; Pages' other defaults, such as `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, stay as they are (ADR 0049).

| Header | Value | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` | `same-origin` | With the next one, cross-origin isolation, which `SharedArrayBuffer` and WebAssembly threads need (technical design section 4). |
| `Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy` | `require-corp` | The same. Fonts are same-origin files of the build (ui.md 2.16.7, ADR 0031), so they need no CORP header. |
| `Content-Security-Policy` | the policy below | What the page and its worker may load and run. |
| `X-Content-Type-Options` | `nosniff` | A file is used only as the type it is served as, so no file of the build runs as a script or a stylesheet it is not. |
| `Referrer-Policy` | `no-referrer` | The app requests nothing from another origin and links nowhere, so no request needs to say where it came from. |

**The content security policy** is the desktop's (desktop.md 2.8), adapted to a page that runs the core in a module worker and fetches it over the network instead of IPC (ADR 0049):

```text
default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'; style-src 'self'; font-src 'self'; img-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'; worker-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'
```

Against the desktop's, directive by directive: `script-src` adds `'wasm-unsafe-eval'`, without which the worker's `WebAssembly.instantiateStreaming` of `core/partsmith_core.wasm` is refused and the core fails to load with the fatal panel of ui.md 4.4 (measured in Chromium on 2026-10-30), while JavaScript's `eval`, `new Function` and inline scripts stay refused; `worker-src 'self'` replaces `'none'`, since the core runs in a same-origin module worker (`assets/core.worker-*.js`, ui.md 2.3); `connect-src 'self'` replaces the desktop's IPC sources, since the worker fetches the core's `.wasm` from the same origin; and `img-src` leaves out `data:`, which nothing of the web app loads (the build's CSS and JavaScript hold no `data:` URL, 2026-10-30). The other seven directives are the desktop's. No source names a host or a scheme: the app loads only its own files, fonts included (ADR 0031). The policy covers the worker as well: it comes with the worker script's response too, and the worker's fetches and its WebAssembly compile follow it. React writes element styles through the CSSOM (`element.style`), which `style-src` does not govern, so the runtime positions of ui.md 3.12 need no `'unsafe-inline'`, while a `style` attribute in markup or a `<style>` element is refused, as the root AGENTS.md's rule against inline styles already keeps out. A download (an object URL and a click on an `<a download>`, ui.md 2.15) is a navigation, which no fetch directive governs. Chrome 97, Firefox 102 and Safari 16 added `'wasm-unsafe-eval'`, so every browser and webview at the minimum versions of ui.md 1.1 knows each source the policy uses. Cloudflare features that inject scripts into the served pages stay off for the Pages project and its zone: Web Analytics' automatic setup (a beacon script from `static.cloudflareinsights.com`), Rocket Loader and Zaraz inject scripts from another host or inline, which `script-src 'self'` refuses, and Email Obfuscation has no address to rewrite in a page that shows none; open issue 19's check on the production host looks for each. The visual tests run with Playwright's `bypassCSP` (ui.md 6.6), because Playwright applies a screenshot's `stylePath` as a `<style>` element, which `style-src 'self'` refuses; E-161 checks the policy in the page and the core worker, and every other E-test ends by checking it in the page, in Chromium (ui.md 6.3 `test`). The policy changes only in `ui/public/_headers`, `EXPECTED_HEADERS` (2.6) and test 14's written-out copy together, which the Vite plugin below and test 65 read from the file, with this section, and with a new ADR that supersedes decision 1 of ADR 0049; each change is checked against the desktop's policy (desktop.md 2.8, `csp` and `devCsp`), from which this one is adapted.

**Local servers.** `vite.config.ts` sends the headers of `_headers` on every response of `pnpm preview` and `pnpm dev`, 304 Not Modified included, from its plugin `webHeaders` (`partsmith:web-headers`). The plugin reads `public/_headers` when the configuration loads, by the rule test 65 reads it with (after the line `/*`, each line is two spaces, a name, `: ` and a value), so the file is the one place that holds the values. Its middleware runs before the ones with which Vite serves files: these answer a revalidation (an `If-None-Match` equal to the file's `ETag`) with a bare 304 before they apply `server.headers` or `preview.headers`, and on the first platforms run WebKit found the core failed right after `page.reload()` in four tests, most likely because the revalidated worker script came back without the isolation headers (ui.md open issue 41). `pnpm preview` sends all five, so every E-test that opens the app runs under the production policy (ui.md 6.5). `pnpm dev` sends the same headers but `Content-Security-Policy` (ADR 0049, option (h)), for two reasons. Its page holds an inline script (the Fast Refresh preamble of `@vitejs/plugin-react`) and Vite inserts stylesheets as `<style>` elements, so under the production policy the dev page never renders (measured 2026-10-30). And the same server serves the desktop's dev window: `cargo tauri dev` loads `http://localhost:5173` directly, since Tauri 2.12.3 proxies the dev server only on mobile and so sends `devCsp` with none of the dev page's responses on the desktop (desktop.md 2.8), and a policy of the dev server would be the only one there, where a `connect-src` without `ipc:` and `http://ipc.localhost` refuses Tauri's IPC. So the test pages that `ui/e2e/support/test-pages.ts` serves from a dev server (those of `viewport.spec.ts`, P-12 and the engine page) run without a policy. `vite.config.ts` also turns Vite's CORS middleware off (`server.cors: false`, which `preview.cors` takes over), since the app requests nothing from another origin and the middleware's `Vary: Origin` on every response is the likely reason WebKit fetched the preloaded font a second time (ui.md 2.16.7). `server.headers` and `preview.headers` stay unset, so the plugin is the one source of the headers in local runs (test 65). So local runs and Playwright behave like production.

### 2.8 Examples corpus

This section settles the layout that features.md open issue 3 and cli.md open issue 3 left to it.

**The rule.** Every `.ptsm` file under `examples/` is an example. Next to `examples/<path>/<stem>.ptsm`:

- `<stem>.expected.json` holds its expected results (required).
- `<stem>.py` is its Python twin (required exactly when the example qualifies, see below; forbidden otherwise).
- `assets/` holds the files its features import, named by content hash (cli.md 2.3), shared by the examples in that folder. An asset is committed once, as written, and never rewritten: its name is the BLAKE3 hash of its bytes (engine.md 3.14), so other bytes for the same model (a STEP header names the OCCT version) are a new asset under their own hash, never a change to the old one. Runner test 4 holds every asset an example imports to its name, since `partsmith rebuild` stops with `asset_mismatch` on bytes that do not hash to it, and runner test 9 requires every file in an `assets/` folder to be imported by an example of that folder, so every committed asset is held to its name.

Allowed locations: `examples/<type>/<stem>.ptsm`, where `<type>` is a feature type registered by `Registry::with_builtins()` and `<stem>` matches `[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*` (the existing examples, which `crates/partsmith-recompute/tests/feature_*.rs` load by path, all follow it and keep their names); `examples/references/<stem>.ptsm` for scenarios of [the reference stability corpus](../design/reference-stability-corpus.md), which span several feature types, each the scenario's document after its edit with the references its setup picked (`references/line-redrawn` is R06 (a), `references/slot-inserted` is R17 (a) and `references/notch-replaces-line` is R66, [the reference stability study](../design/reference-stability-and-errors.md) section 10.3 item 11; `references/moved-face-sketch` is R32 (a) and `references/sketch-after-cut` is R33, its section 10.4 item 6, which adds R38, a scenario about one feature type, as `linear_pattern/leaning-edge`; its section 10.5 item 8 adds R43 (b) as `hole/deleted-point`, the hole alone, since the error of the scenario's chamfer would forbid the twin), with `<stem>` matching the same pattern; and `examples/bench/<name>/model.ptsm` for benchmark documents (`examples/bench/fifty-features/model.ptsm`, engine.md section 5), with `<name>` matching the same pattern. No other files exist under `examples/` outside `assets/` folders. Examples do not set the rollback marker (rollback is covered by engine.md, recompute.md and python-api.md tests), so every feature of an example is built.

**Expected results.** `<stem>.expected.json` is the `partsmith rebuild --checks --json` report of the example (cli.md 3.3) with two members removed: the top-level `path` (it depends on where the command runs) and every finding's `triangles` (triangle ranges depend on the display tessellation; the finding's `faces`, `location`, `value` and `limit` stay). Everything else stays in the report's order: `printer`, `features` (each with `id`, `type`, `status`, `messages`), `parameters`, `bodies` (volume, area, center of mass, bounding box and face, edge and vertex counts, or `empty`) and `checks` (summary and per-body reports). The file is pretty JSON with two-space indentation and one trailing newline, as the CLI prints it.

**Qualifying for a Python twin.** An example qualifies when it has no `import` feature (a script cannot carry asset bytes, features/import.md) and no feature in its expected results has status `error` (a feature function that fails raises `FeatureError`, python-api.md 3.5). These are the conditions of python-api.md 3.9's first form.

**Rust runner (`crates/partsmith-cli/tests/examples.rs`).** One integration test module of the CLI's one test binary, with the export check in `tests/examples/export.rs` and the coverage checks in `tests/examples/coverage.rs` as its submodules, run by `cargo test --workspace` (and alone by `cargo test -p partsmith-cli --test integration examples::`). It finds the repository root from `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` and runs the built binary (`CARGO_BIN_EXE_partsmith`) with the repository root as working directory. Its tests:

1. `examples_follow_the_layout`: every file under `examples/` outside `assets/` is an allowed `.ptsm`, `.expected.json` or `.py` path; every `.expected.json` and `.py` has its `.ptsm`; no example has a `rollback` member.
2. `examples_are_canonical`: `partsmith fmt --check` over every example exits 0.
3. `every_feature_type_has_an_example`: for every type in `Registry::with_builtins().types()` (through `partsmith_engine::Session::new(SessionOptions::headless()).registry()`), some example under `examples/<type>/` has a feature record of that type.
4. `examples_rebuild_as_expected`: for each example, in parallel over `std::thread::available_parallelism()` workers, runs `partsmith rebuild --checks --json examples/<path>/<stem>.ptsm`, accepts exit status 0 or 1 (1 means a feature failed, which the expected results record), removes `path` and the findings' `triangles`, and compares the result with `<stem>.expected.json` by the rules below. It also checks that `<stem>.py` exists exactly when the example qualifies.
5. `examples_export_to_every_format`: exports every example that builds to STL, 3MF, OBJ and STEP, compares each report with the file asked for and written, and imports each STEP file back and compares its volume with the expected results (**Export examples** and **STEP round trip**, below).
6. `every_sketch_record_form_has_an_example`: every record form of a sketch constraint and of a sketch entity appears in an example (**Corpus coverage**, below).
7. `every_option_and_reference_kind_has_an_example`: every option value and plane or axis reference kind of every feature type's manifest is held by a feature record of that type in an example under `examples/<type>/` (**Corpus coverage**).
8. `every_check_has_findings_on_every_technology`: every print check has a finding in an example on each printer technology (**Corpus coverage**).
9. `every_asset_is_imported`: every file in an `assets/` folder is imported by an example of that folder (**Corpus coverage**).
10. The comparison rules' own tests (section 6, tests 20 to 25), the export check's own tests (section 6, tests 68 to 70 and 107) and the coverage checks' own tests (section 6, tests 74 to 78), on literal JSON, files the tests write, the engine's registry, schemas and printer profiles, in tests 70 and 107 the built binary, and in test 107 a headless engine session.

Each test collects every problem before failing and fails once, listing the problems sorted by path (runner tests 6 to 8, whose problems name no file, by their text), one per line (section 4).

**Comparison.** Two JSON values match when they have the same type; objects have the same set of keys and matching values; arrays have the same length and matching elements; strings, booleans and `null` are equal; two numbers written as integers (no fraction or exponent) are equal; any other two numbers `actual` and `expected` satisfy `|actual - expected| <= max(1e-6 * |expected|, 1e-6)`. The tolerance is python-api.md's 1e-6 relative for volumes and areas with an absolute floor of `LINEAR_TOLERANCE` (1e-6) for values near zero; it absorbs the floating-point differences between platforms and compilers in values that vary smoothly with the geometry (volumes, areas, positions, measured values). It cannot absorb a difference in a count, a face id, a message or an order, so whatever a mesh decides must not depend on the platform: the triangulation's arithmetic is the same everywhere (kernel.md 3.7 item 2), regions whose areas this comparison cannot tell apart are ordered by their triangles, not by which area is larger (print.md 3.7 item 7), and a wall's thickness is measured only between faces that face each other (print.md 3.9.6), since a ray into an acute edge measured where a centroid fell, which at a cone's rim the platform's math decides (ADR 0041). A real change still shows. A mismatch is reported at its JSON Pointer.

**Export examples.** Technical design section 16 asks the examples to cover each export path. An example *builds* when its expected results have no feature with status `error` and at least one body without `"empty": true` whose record in the example's `.ptsm`, the body with the same `id`, has neither `"visible": false` nor a `role` other than `part`. These are the bodies `partsmith export` takes by default in every format (engine.md 3.13 item 1, which adds visible modifier bodies for 3MF alone), and it refuses a failed feature without `--allow-errors` (cli.md 3.4 item 3), so an example that does not build exits with 1 (`features_failed` or `nothing_to_export`). On 2026-10-28, 232 of the 256 examples build; of the other 24, 7 have a failing feature and 17 have no body with geometry. Runner test 5 runs `partsmith export --json examples/<path>/<stem>.ptsm -o <output>` for every example that builds and for each of the formats `stl`, `3mf`, `obj` and `step`, in parallel over `std::thread::available_parallelism()` workers, where `<output>` is a file with the format as its extension, one per example and format, in a temporary directory that the test creates and removes. Each run is checked in this order, and its checks stop at the first step that fails: the run starts, exits with 0 and prints JSON, each failure reported as runner test 4 reports a rebuild's, with `partsmith export to <format>` in place of `partsmith rebuild`; a file can be read at `<output>`; and the report without its `warnings` member matches `{"files": [{"path": "<output>", "media_type": "model/<format>", "bytes": <size>}]}` by the comparison rules above, with `<output>` written as it was passed (cli.md 2.2 and 3.4 item 8) and `<size>` the length in bytes of the file at `<output>` (engine.md 3.13 item 5), each mismatch a problem at its JSON Pointer (section 4); and, for `step` alone, the file at `<output>` imports back with the volume of the example's expected results (**STEP round trip**, below). An example's problems come in the format order `stl`, `3mf`, `obj`, `step`. The report's `warnings` are not compared: they come from the mesh at the document's `standard` tolerance, the mesh the print checks of the same document analyze (engine.md 3.12 item 4 and 3.13 items 2 and 6), so the example's expected `mesh_validity` findings already hold what they report (runner test 4), and io.md's tests hold their words; `extrude/union-touching-itself`, whose two parts touch along an edge, and `extrude/corners-touching-resin` (print.md test 127), whose two cubes share only one edge, warn `non_manifold_edges` in each mesh format on purpose. An example whose expected results are missing, cannot be read or are not JSON, or whose `.ptsm` cannot be read or is not JSON, is not exported, since runner tests 2 and 4 report it, and nothing is blessed, since the check stores no results. The other export paths are tested elsewhere: `py` by the twins (python-api.md 3.9's first form exports every example that has a twin through the engine's `export_python`, which `partsmith export --format py` also calls, cli.md 3.4 item 5), and the export options (`--ascii`, `--per-body`, `--body`, `--allow-errors`, `--chord` and `--angle`) by cli.md tests 34 to 49. The bytes of the mesh files are io.md's tests' and, for two 3MF exports, engine.md test 160's.

**STEP round trip.** Technical design section 16 asks a STEP export and import to keep the volume within 1e-6 relative. Runner test 5 checks it for every example that builds, on top of cli.md test 41 and engine.md test 89, which check it on `box.ptsm` ([ADR 0046](../adr/0046-examples-exported-to-every-format.md), option (c), the owner's choice of 2026-11-04). Once an export to `step` has passed the checks above, the test imports the bytes that the report check read for `<size>` in a headless engine session: `new_document` in millimeters with no printer, `import_step` with those bytes and no name, and `measure` of the one body that the import made (`MeasureTarget::Body`). The `volume` of that measurement is the imported volume. The expected volume is the sum of the `volume` members of the bodies of the expected results that make the example build: no `"empty": true`, and a record in the `.ptsm` that is neither hidden nor of a role other than `part`. These are the bodies the export wrote, since STEP takes no modifier bodies (engine.md 3.13 item 1). Both volumes are in mm³ whatever the example's units: the expected results and `measure` report mm³ (engine.md 3.11), and the inch examples are compared as they are. The imported volume must satisfy the rule above for numbers that are not both integers, `|actual - expected| <= max(1e-6 * |expected|, 1e-6)`. For a volume of 1 mm³ or more that is technical design section 16's 1e-6 relative. The smallest expected volume of an example that builds is 62.8 mm³ (`circular_pattern/count-one`). A `new_document`, `import_step` or `measure` that fails is one problem with the message of the engine's error. An imported volume that does not match is one problem with both volumes (section 4). Only the volume is compared, the value technical design section 16 names. The check takes the expected volume and the import as arguments. Runner test 5 computes the expected volume from the expected results and the `.ptsm`, the values the rule of building reads. It passes the import of the session of the worker that ran the export. Tests 69 and 107 give literal volumes and, where they say so, an import that stands in for the session's. Each of the workers that run the exports has a headless session of its own (`Session::new(SessionOptions::headless())`). It imports the file of an export to `step` right after it has run that export and checked its report. The kernel reads one STEP file at a time in a process (kernel.md 3.10 item 4). `import_step` reads each file twice: once for the body's name (engine.md 3.14), and once when the import feature builds (features/import.md section 3.3 step 2). The workers take turns at those reads while the exports of the other workers run in their own processes. Each session closes each document it made, whatever `import_step` and `measure` give. A close that fails is not a problem. The imports run in the test's own process: a panic or crash there fails runner test 5 without naming the example. Open issue 18 has how close the corpus's STEP files come back, and open issue 4 the time the imports add.

**Corpus coverage.** Technical design section 16 asks the example suite to cover every feature and option: each sketch tool and constraint, each feature mode, each printability check, FDM and resin. Runner test 3 covers the feature types; runner tests 6 to 9 cover what the files can show of the rest, and the drawing tools' output is ui.md's U-247 ([ADR 0051](../adr/0051-corpus-coverage-checks.md)). Tests 6 to 9 read the files under `examples/` as runner tests 1 to 4 find them and run no binary. Tests 6 to 8 count only examples outside `examples/bench/`, whose one document is a benchmark, not documentation, and in tests 6 to 9 an example whose `.ptsm` (or, in test 8, expected results) cannot be read or is not JSON counts for nothing, since runner tests 2 and 4 report it. The schemas they read are the texts `partsmith schema` prints, `partsmith_engine::ptsm_schema()` and `partsmith_engine::protocol_schema()`, and the registry is the one runner test 3 reads, so a new record form, option, reference kind, check or technology is checked as soon as the code has it.

- **Runner test 6, sketch record forms.** Every alternative of `/$defs/SketchConstraint/oneOf` and of `/$defs/SketchEntity/oneOf` in the `.ptsm` schema, a record form, is the form of a constraint or an entity of a `sketch` feature record of an example. A record has the form whose `properties.type.const` is its `type`, whose `required` keys it all has, and whose `properties` hold every key it has: `{"id": "c-1", "type": "horizontal", "points": ["e-1.start", "e-1.end"]}` has the points form of `horizontal`, not its line form, and `distance` has three forms (`points`; `point` and `line`; `line`). A form that no record has is the problem `Sketch {constraint or entity} {type} ({keys}) has no example outside examples/bench/. Add one under examples/sketch/.`, with `{keys}` the form's `required` keys other than `id` and `type`, in the schema's order, joined by `, ` (`Sketch constraint horizontal (points) has no example ...`).
- **Runner test 7, option values and reference kinds.** For every registered type (as runner test 3 reads them) and every field hint of its manifest (`Session::registry()`, `Registry::get(type)` and its `manifest()`, features.md section 2): each option `value` of a hint with `options`, and each reference kind of a hint whose `selection` is `plane` or `axis`, is held by a feature record of that type in an example under `examples/<type>/`, as runner test 3 counts the examples of a type; a record of that type in another folder, such as a sketch in `examples/extrude/` or a pattern in `examples/references/`, counts for nothing here. A record's value is found by following the hint's path from the record, one key per `.`-separated part: for an option it is the string there, for a reference kind the `kind` member of the object there. The kinds a plane or axis field accepts are the `properties.kind.const` of the alternatives of the field's schema in the `.ptsm` schema, reached from `/$defs/feature.<type>` through `properties` by each key of the path, following each `$ref`, and through an `anyOf` of a schema and `{"type": "null"}` (an optional field) to that schema: `plane` of `box` accepts `origin`, `datum` and `face` (`PlaneRef`), `plane` of `sketch` the same three (`SketchPlane`), and `second_direction.axis` of `linear_pattern` accepts `origin`, `sketch_line`, `edge` and `face` (`AxisRef`). Canonical form never writes a default (features.md section 3.2), so the runner lists the nine (type, field) pairs whose field has one, with its value: `op` `join` of `mirror`, `linear_pattern` and `circular_pattern`, `hole_type.kind` `simple` of `hole`, and the `plane` kind `origin` of `box`, `cylinder`, `sphere`, `cone` and `torus` (each feature spec's params; test 76 holds this list to the registry and the `.ptsm` schema). A record that leaves out the first key of one of these paths holds its default; a record that leaves out any other path holds nothing there (`second_direction.axis` of a pattern without a second direction). A value that no record holds is the problem `Feature type {type}: {field} {value} has no example outside examples/bench/. Add one under examples/{type}/.`, with `{field}` the hint's path for an option and the path followed by `.kind` for a reference kind (`Feature type linear_pattern: second_direction.axis.kind edge has no example ...`).
- **Runner test 8, print checks on each technology.** For every check of `/$defs/CheckKind/oneOf` and every technology of `/$defs/Technology/oneOf` in the protocol schema (each alternative's `const`), the expected results of an example on a printer of that technology hold a finding of that check: an entry of `checks.bodies[*].report.checks` with that `check` and at least one finding in `findings`, of any severity, an info included (an enclosed hollow on FDM is only ever an info, print.md section 3.9.8). An example's printer is the expected results' `printer`, and its technology the `technology` of the profile of `Session::printer_profiles()` with that `id`, as the profile serializes; a printer that names no listed profile counts for no technology. A check and technology without a finding is the problem `No example outside examples/bench/ on a printer of technology {technology} has a finding of {check}. Add one.`
- **Runner test 9, assets.** Every file under an `assets/` folder of an example folder (`examples/<folder>/assets/`, where `<folder>` is a feature type, `references` or `bench/<name>` as runner test 1 allows them) has its path below `assets/` as the `asset` of an `import` feature record of an example in `<folder>`. Only `partsmith rebuild` of an example that imports a file reads its bytes (runner test 4, engine.md section 3.14), so without this a committed asset that no example imports is never held to its name. A file that no example there imports is the problem `examples/{folder}/assets/{name}: no example in examples/{folder}/ imports it. Import it there or delete it.`

The problems of runner tests 6 to 8 are sorted by their text, those of runner test 9 by the file's path. None of the four has exceptions: a value that the corpus cannot hold would be a defect of the check, fixed in this section, and an exception, were one ever needed, would be listed here with its reason.

**Coverage examples.** Measured on 2026-10-30, before the examples below were added: runner test 6 found no example with the points forms of `horizontal` and `vertical` or the line forms of `horizontal_distance` and `vertical_distance` (every constraint type and entity type was in one outside the benchmark); runner test 7 found `second_direction.axis` of `linear_pattern` only as `origin` (every option value was written or a default, `sphere` and `cone` standing on the origin plane only by default); runner test 8 found no `mesh_validity`, `first_layer_contact`, `islands` or `small_features` finding on `generic-resin` (FDM had all eight); runner test 9 found both committed assets imported. Seven examples close these gaps, each with its Python twin and blessed expected results and each held by a test of its spec, so a bless that moves one of their values fails there: `sketch/point-and-line-forms` (features/sketch.md test 51); `linear_pattern/second-sketch-line-direction`, `linear_pattern/second-edge-direction` and `linear_pattern/second-face-direction` (features/linear_pattern.md tests 22 to 24); and the resin examples `boolean/island-resin`, `extrude/wedge-on-edge-resin` and `extrude/corners-touching-resin` (print.md tests 125 to 127). With them, scratch versions of the four checks passed on the corpus.

**Regenerating expected results.** After an intended change, run `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 cargo test -p partsmith-cli --test integration examples::`. With `PARTSMITH_BLESS` set to `1`, `examples_rebuild_as_expected` writes `<stem>.expected.json` for every example whose file is missing or does not match, prints `blessed examples/<path>/<stem>.expected.json` for each, and passes; missing Python twins are listed but do not fail (the Python bless below adds them). Layout, canonical form, coverage (runner tests 3 and 6 to 9) and the export check (runner test 5) still fail. Runner tests 5 and 8 bless nothing: they read the expected results as they find them (test 5 to decide which examples build and for the volume each STEP file must keep, test 8 for their findings), which runner test 4 may be rewriting in the same run, so after a bless run, run `cargo test -p partsmith-cli --test integration examples::` again without `PARTSMITH_BLESS` before committing. When the environment variable `CI` is set, a set `PARTSMITH_BLESS` fails the run with `PARTSMITH_BLESS is set in CI. Bless expected results locally and commit them.` Without `PARTSMITH_BLESS`, a missing expected file fails with `examples/<path>/<stem>.expected.json is missing. Run: PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 cargo test -p partsmith-cli --test integration examples::`. Blessed files are reviewed in the pull request like code; a kernel upgrade that changes them needs the sign-off of technical design section 16. The engine's golden 3MF files use the same variable and the same refusal in CI (engine.md section 3.20, engine tests 160 and 161), and are blessed only with their own filter, `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 cargo test -p partsmith-engine --test integration export_and_import::t160`, so bless the examples with their filter too: a bless of the whole workspace would also rewrite a golden that differs.

**Python twin checks (`python/tests/test_examples.py`).** Run by `pytest python/tests` in the `python` job. They find the repository root from the test file's location. For each example with a twin:

1. `run_script(<stem>.py)` returns exactly one document, and its `to_text()` equals the example's `.ptsm` text byte for byte, so the twin builds the identical model.
2. python-api.md 3.9's second form holds for the twin: `D1 = run_code(twin)`, `D2 = run_code(D1.to_python())`, `D2.to_text() == D1.to_text()`.
3. python-api.md 3.9's first form holds for the example's `.ptsm` text.
4. The twin keeps up with code generation: it has no more generic calls than the generated script, `Document.open(<stem>.ptsm).to_python()`, where a generic call is a line that starts, after its indentation and an optional `<variable> = ` (python-api.md 3.8 rule 7), with `feature(`. So a twin written before a feature's options got a dedicated form (python-api.md 3.5, [ADR 0048](../adr/0048-python-keywords-gui-round-trip-and-python-budgets.md)) fails until it is regenerated, while a hand-edited twin that writes dedicated forms the generator cannot, such as `Rectangle`, passes.

A twin may be the generated script or a hand-edited, more readable one, as long as checks 1 and 4 hold. With `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1` (refused under `CI` as above), for every qualifying example (by its `.expected.json`) whose twin is missing or fails check 1 or check 4, the test writes the generated script, `Document.open(<stem>.ptsm).to_python()`, to `<stem>.py`, prints `blessed examples/<path>/<stem>.py`, and passes. This is how twins are regenerated, never by hand: the 8 twins that wrote an extrude with two sides, a chamfer with two distances or a reversed linear pattern in the generic form (`chamfer/edge-order`, `chamfer/two-distances`, `chamfer/two-distances-flipped`, `extrude/two-sides`, `extrude/two-sides-one-zero`, `extrude/two-sides-reverse`, `linear_pattern/reverse` and `linear_pattern/second-reverse`) fail check 4 once the hooks write those options, and a bless rewrites them; `hole/deleted-point`, whose generated script keeps its one generic call, does not change.

**Documents the UI writes (`python/tests/test_gui_documents.py`).** Technical design sections 10 and 16 ask for a round trip from the GUI through Python to `.ptsm`. The examples and the engine fixtures are written by hand, with sequential ids, unnamed features and references written out in tests, so they never hold what only the UI writes. `ui/e2e/fixtures/gui/` holds three documents that the real UI made and saved, through the steps of ui.md's E-160, which writes them with `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1` (only one that is missing or differs from the download) and checks on every e2e run that the UI still writes them: the facts of each row, and the download's JSON structure and values, generated ids, references and numbers aside (ui.md 6.2 and 6.5). They are `extrude-sides.ptsm`, `picks.ptsm` and `starting-values.ptsm`, with random ids, starting values, a parameter added in the Parameters panel, a reversed two-sided extrude, a sketch on a picked face (with its fallback) holding a point with a through-all hole at it, a two-distance chamfer, a reversed linear pattern into a new body, edge references and an axis direction copied from picks, a split into the new body its command starts with, and renamed features, bodies and parameters. The test finds the repository root from its own location, as `test_examples.py` does, and is parametrized over these three names, a constant list, so a missing file fails with the message of section 4. For each document:

1. every feature of the document, opened with `Document.open`, has status `ok` (otherwise the message of section 4, one line per other feature);
2. python-api.md 3.9's first form holds for its text (steps 1 to 5: `export_python`, `run_code` makes one document whose `to_text()` equals the file byte for byte and whose `to_python()` equals the script, the same feature statuses, and per body the same volume, area, bounding box and element counts, its face count among them), checked as `test_examples.py` checks its check 3;
3. the generated script has no generic call (check 4 above), so every option the UI wrote has its dedicated form (python-api.md 3.5); otherwise the message of section 4. Check 3 holds only while the keywords of [ADR 0048](../adr/0048-python-keywords-gui-round-trip-and-python-budgets.md)'s decision 1 exist, and goes with them (its option (C)).

The documents are not examples: they have no expected results or twins, and the examples runner does not read them.

**Adding an example.** Write the `.ptsm` (canonical: `partsmith fmt`), run `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 cargo test -p partsmith-cli --test integration examples::`, then `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 pytest python/tests/test_examples.py` when it qualifies, review the new files, and commit them together.

### 2.9 Third-party notices

Every artifact Partsmith distributes carries the notices of the third-party software in it (ADR 0033): the license texts of every Rust crate and npm package it is built from, the two self-hosted fonts and the other libraries linked into it, and, for OCCT, its LGPL 2.1 text and exception, the notices of the code it includes from other projects, and a statement of how it is linked, where its source is and how to relink it. OCCT is linked statically (ADR 0003), and LGPL 2.1 section 6 is to be met with published source (ADR 0034, option A): the public repository, the source stamp, Partsmith's own source archive and the OCCT source archive of every web deployment, and Partsmith's own source archive and the OCCT source archive of every release (2.13), which the statements below name.

**Files.** Two generated files, committed, in UTF-8 with LF line endings and one final newline:

| File | Artifact | Ships |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` (repository root) | The desktop app for Linux (`x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`), Windows (`x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`) and macOS (`aarch64-apple-darwin`), root crate `partsmith-desktop` | As a resource of every bundle (desktop.md 2.8), and as an asset of every release next to `SHA256SUMS` (2.5) |
| `ui/public/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` | The web app, whose core is built for `wasm32-unknown-emscripten` from root crate `partsmith-wasm` | At `/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` of every deployment: Vite copies it into `ui/dist`, and `check:dist` requires it (2.6) |

The desktop app embeds `ui/dist`, so it also carries the web file among its assets, where nothing shows it.

**In the app** (open issue 11). The palette command Third-party notices (ui.md 3.5) opens the web file in a new tab on the web. On the desktop it names where the bundle installed the desktop file. The macOS About panel and the Windows executable's legal copyright name OCCT and the file through `bundle.copyright` (desktop.md 2.8).

**Commands.** `ui/scripts/notices.mjs` is an ES module that uses only Node's standard library and the exports of `check-licenses.mjs` (2.10). Like `check-dist.mjs`, it runs its command only when it is the script Node started, so importing it has no side effect. `ui/package.json` gains `"notices": "node scripts/notices.mjs write"` and `"check:notices": "node scripts/notices.mjs check"`, both run in `ui/`:

- `write` writes both files and prints `Wrote THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt and ui/public/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt.`
- `check` generates both texts in memory and compares each with its committed file byte for byte. It exits 0 and prints `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt and ui/public/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt are up to date.` when both match, and exits 1 with the out-of-date message of section 4 for each file that differs or is missing, in the order of the table above.
- Any other argument list exits 2 with `usage: node scripts/notices.mjs write|check`.
- A failing input (section 4) exits 1 with its message, and `write` then writes nothing.

Both commands need `cargo` and `pnpm` on `PATH` and `ui/node_modules` installed (`pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`). They need no OCCT install: every text they read is in the repository or in a package folder. The module finds the repository root from its own location and runs every command through one function, `run(command, args, directory)`, which resolves to the command's standard output and which the tests replace; the command passes `runCommand` of `check-licenses.mjs`.

```js
/** The first line of each notices file. */
export const DESKTOP_NOTICES_HEADING = 'Third-party notices for the Partsmith desktop app'
export const WEB_NOTICES_HEADING = 'Third-party notices for the Partsmith web app'

/** The desktop triples with the platform names the notices use, in this order. */
export const DESKTOP_TARGETS = [
  { triple: 'x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu', platform: 'Linux' },
  { triple: 'x86_64-pc-windows-msvc', platform: 'Windows' },
  { triple: 'aarch64-apple-darwin', platform: 'macOS' },
]

/**
```
