Each deploy refreshes that hash. A deploy after Pages dropped it (a long pause, or a failed `upsert-hashes`) uploads the 17.5 MB again. Wrangler's source does not say how long Pages keeps a hash. Wrangler gives the archive the content type `application/gzip`, from the `mime` table it bundles.

**The archive check.** `scripts/check-source-archive.sh <archive> <commit>` is the one check of a source archive that `git archive` wrote: the step `Write the OCCT source archive` of the `sources` jobs and the steps `Check the OCCT source archive` and `Check the Partsmith source archive` of `deploy` run it, each with its own error. It runs with `set -euo pipefail` and needs only `bash`, `git` and `gzip`. It exits 0 and prints nothing when `<archive>` is a whole gzip file (`gzip -t` reads all of it) whose tar names the commit `<commit>`. Otherwise it exits 1 and prints nothing, a missing file included. With other than two arguments, it exits 2 with `usage: scripts/check-source-archive.sh <archive> <commit>` on standard error. `git get-tar-commit-id` reads the commit from the first header of the tar, where `git archive` stores it (The OCCT source archive, Bytes), and needs no repository. A cut-off archive still names the right commit there (measured on 2026-11-04 with the first 1,000,000 bytes of the 7.9.3 archive). `gzip -t` is there for that case. `git get-tar-commit-id` exits after that header. The `gzip -dc` that feeds it then writes into a closed pipe. Where SIGPIPE has its default action, that `gzip` ends silently. Where SIGPIPE is ignored, it prints `gzip: stdout: Broken pipe` and exits 1 (measured here with `trap '' PIPE`). The script sends the standard error of `gzip` and `git` to `/dev/null` and compares only what `git` prints. Until the review of 2026-11-05, each of the three steps held these two commands itself (ADR 0034's amendment of 2026-11-05, choice 6).

**Partsmith's source archive** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-05, option (b) of section 8's entry on Partsmith's own source, ADR 0034's amendment of that date). Every deployment, previews included, holds the regular file `partsmith-source.tar.gz` at its root, next to `SOURCE.txt`. Pages serves it at `/partsmith-source.tar.gz`. Every release carries it as an asset next to `SHA256SUMS`, which covers it (the owner's rule of 2026-11-05, ADR 0034's amendment of that date, choice 3). It is `git archive` of the commit the run built: for a deployment the `Commit:` of its `/SOURCE.txt` (for a preview, the merge commit GitHub made), and for a release the tagged commit. The fourth line of `/SOURCE.txt` links GitHub's archive of the same commit. This file stays with the deployment when GitHub drops a preview's merge commit. Its bytes do not change when GitHub changes how it makes its archives.

`scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh <directory>` writes `<directory>/partsmith-source.tar.gz`. Like `scripts/occt-source-archive.sh`, it runs with `set -euo pipefail`.

- **Commit.** The repository is `{root}`, the real path of the folder above the script's own folder. The script finds it with `CDPATH` empty and the output of `cd` discarded: an exported `CDPATH` would otherwise send `cd` to a folder of its list and make `cd` print that folder into `{root}`. The script archives the commit that `HEAD` names there, `{commit}`, the full id that `git -C {root} rev-parse --verify 'HEAD^{commit}'` prints. It never reads the work tree: uncommitted changes and untracked files stay out. In CI the checkout's `HEAD` is the run's `GITHUB_SHA`. When `git -C {root} rev-parse --show-toplevel` fails or prints another folder than `{root}`, the script exits 1 with the not-a-clone message of section 4 and writes nothing. It sends that command's standard error to `/dev/null`, as `scripts/occt-source-archive.sh` does for its tag check. The message is then the one line on standard error. That covers a tree extracted from an archive, which has no `.git`, and a tree inside another clone, whose `HEAD` is not Partsmith's.
- **Bytes.** `git -C {root} -c core.autocrlf=false -c core.eol=lf -c tar.umask=0002 -c core.attributesFile=/dev/null archive --format=tar --prefix=partsmith-{commit}/ {commit}`, run with `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1` in its environment and piped to `gzip -9 -n`, with the four `-c` options of the OCCT archive (The OCCT source archive, Bytes). Without `core.eol=lf`, a `core.eol=crlf` in the user's configuration (or `native` on Windows, the default) would give CRLF line endings to a file whose attributes set `text=auto` without `eol`, even with `core.autocrlf=false`. `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1` keeps Git from reading the system attributes file (`$(prefix)/etc/gitattributes`), which could also leave files out with `export-ignore`. The folder `partsmith-{commit}/` is the one GitHub's archive of a commit has. `git archive` of a commit gives every entry the commit's committer time and stores `{commit}` in the archive's pax header, where `git get-tar-commit-id` reads it. The repository's `.gitattributes` sets `* text=auto eol=lf` and names no `export-ignore` or `export-subst`. The archive then holds every file of the commit, the text files with LF line endings. `git archive` reads only the commit and its tree, which a shallow clone holds. The checkout of a CI job, at depth 1, gives the bytes of a full clone. The same commit gives the same bytes on every run with the same `git` and `gzip`, whatever the Git configuration and the global and system attributes files say. The clone's own `.git/info/attributes` still applies. CI's checkout has none.
- **Output.** As the OCCT script: it writes a temporary file in `<directory>` and renames it when `gzip` has finished. An `EXIT` trap removes it on a failure. A failure leaves `<directory>` as it was. A `partsmith-source.tar.gz` already there is replaced only by a whole new archive. Then it prints `Wrote {directory}/partsmith-source.tar.gz from commit {commit}: {bytes} bytes, SHA-256 {sha256}.` (`{directory}` as given) and exits 0. `{sha256}` comes from `sha256sum`, or from `shasum -a 256` where `sha256sum` is missing, as on macOS without GNU coreutils. The script then needs `git`, `gzip` and either of the two, and `wc`, `tr`, `cut` and `mv`, which every Linux and macOS has. The log of each run names the bytes it made.
- **Failures.** With other than one argument, it exits 2 with `usage: scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh <directory>` on standard error. Outside a clone it exits 1 (Commit, above). Any other failure, such as a missing `<directory>` or a failing `git archive` or `gzip`, exits with a status other than 0: `pipefail` makes the failure of `git archive` fail the pipeline.
- **In every deployment.** The step `Write the Partsmith source archive` of the `sources` jobs (The source with every artifact, below) runs `scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh sources` in the repository root. `web` downloads `sources-web` into `ui/dist` before `pnpm check:dist`, which holds the archive to the 25 MiB limit like every other file (3.5). `/SOURCE.txt` names it on its fifth line (The source stamp, above). The web statement of 2.9 names it with its script. `deploy`'s step `Check the Partsmith source archive` (2.4) comes after `Check the dependency source archives` and before Wrangler. Unless `scripts/check-source-archive.sh ui/dist/partsmith-source.tar.gz "$GITHUB_SHA"` passes, that is unless the file is a whole gzip file whose tar names the run's commit (The archive check, above), it prints the Partsmith-source error of section 4 and fails the job. The check makes no archive again.
- **In every release.** `release.yml`'s `sources` job writes the archive of the tagged commit into `sources-desktop`. `draft` downloads it into `release/` before `Write SHA256SUMS` (2.5). The desktop statement of 2.9 names it with its script.
- **Measured** (2026-11-05, git 2.43.0 and gzip 1.12, 4 cores). The archive of `0ae7337` holds 2,722 entries, 33,546,240 bytes as tar, and is 12,122,642 bytes (11.6 MiB) with SHA-256 `8a86cd6b7fe2240b1124bc1c7632e36782d745b6972509557b12b0f67040ba48`. That of `99e2111`, wave G's work in progress, is 12,312,807 bytes. Two runs of each wrote the same bytes, in 2.2 s each after a first run of 5.3 s with a cold disk cache. A merge commit made with `git merge --no-ff` and fetched alone with `git fetch --depth 1`, as the checkout of a pull request's run fetches `refs/pull/{number}/merge`, gave the same bytes as in the full clone. The largest folders of the tar are `ui/e2e` (6.9 MB), `ui/src` (4.7 MB) and `docs/spec` (4.0 MB).
- **Size and upload.** At 12,122,642 bytes the archive is 14,091,758 bytes under the 25 MiB limit. A repository whose archive grows over the limit fails `check:dist` in `web`. Like the core, it then needs an ADR (3.5), such as parts like those of the dependency archives. Wrangler uploads a file only when Pages lacks its hash (The archive in every deployment, Size and upload). This archive changes with every commit. Every deployment uploads it. `web-dist` grows by its 12 MB.

**The dependency source archives** (ADR 0034 question 2, answered on 2026-11-04 with the strictest reading of LGPL 2.1 section 6(a): the complete work that uses the Library, from the same place as the build). Every release and every web deployment carries the source of every Rust crate and npm package that the app's executables and the web build are compiled from. The npm archives also carry the source of the code under MPL-2.0 in the native packages they hold (Native packages, below). The desktop crate archive also carries `tauri-cli` and the crates of its own lock file (The crates of tauri-cli, below). `node scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs <scope> <directory>` writes it from the committed `Cargo.lock` and `ui/pnpm-lock.yaml`, with the pins the script holds for those two additions. The script is an ES module that uses only Node's standard library and the exports of `ui/scripts/notices.mjs` and `ui/scripts/check-licenses.mjs`. It runs its command only when it is the script Node started. oxlint and oxfmt refuse a path that holds `..`. The `lint` and `format` scripts of `ui/package.json` reach the script from the repository root with the configuration files of `ui/`. `lint` is exactly `oxlint --deny-warnings && oxfmt --check && cd .. && oxlint --deny-warnings -c ui/.oxlintrc.json scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs && oxfmt --check -c ui/.oxfmtrc.json scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, and `format` is exactly `oxfmt && cd .. && oxfmt -c ui/.oxfmtrc.json scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`. `pnpm lint` in `ui/` then lints the script and checks its format, and `pnpm format` formats it. `<scope>` is `web` or `desktop`:

| Scope | Rust crates | npm packages | Where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `web` | the crates the web notices list (2.9 input 1: what `partsmith-wasm` builds for `wasm32-unknown-emscripten`) | those pnpm installs on Linux x86_64 with glibc, the platform of the `web` job, and the source of their code under MPL-2.0 | `/dependencies/` of every web deployment |
| `desktop` | the crates the desktop notices list (the union over the three triples of `DESKTOP_TARGETS`), `tauri-cli` and the crates of its own lock file | those pnpm installs on any of the three bundle runners: Linux x86_64 with glibc, Windows x86_64 and macOS arm64, and the source of their code under MPL-2.0 | the assets of every release |

The script writes two archives, `rust-crates-{scope}.tar.gz` and `npm-packages-{scope}.tar`, into `<directory>` as parts (Parts, below).

- **Rust crates.** The archive holds one folder, `rust-crates-{scope}/`, with `config.toml` and `vendor/`. `vendor/` is what `cargo vendor --locked --versioned-dirs` writes for the repository's `Cargo.lock`: a folder `{name}-{version}` for each registry package of the lock file (482 on 2026-11-04, 481 since 2026-11-06). For `desktop` the same command also vendors the lock file of `tauri-cli` (`--sync`, The crates of tauri-cli, below). A crate of the scope keeps its whole folder, apart from the files that `EXCLUDED_LICENSE_FILES` of 2.9 names for it (Files left out, below). For `desktop`, so does every package of `tauri-cli`'s lock file with a `source`, as `lockCrates` reads it, which is crates.io for each (The crates of tauri-cli, below). Every other crate becomes a stub: its `Cargo.toml` as vendored, its `.cargo-checksum.json` with `files` empty and `package` kept, and an empty file at the path of each of its targets that `cargo metadata --format-version 1 --locked --all-features` lists, relative to its folder. A crate that output does not list keeps only the two files: `cxxbridge-cmd`, which `cxx` names only under `cfg(any())`. Cargo resolves the whole lock file before it builds anything. It reads the manifest and the targets of every package then, also of one no build compiles. Without the stubs it stops at the first crate it misses with `no matching package named`. Cargo checks only the files `files` lists. No build compiles a stub. The crate set comes from `scopeCrates` of `ui/scripts/notices.mjs`, which computes 2.9 input 1 for the scope's triples. The archive and the notices then list the same crates. `config.toml` is exactly:

  ```toml
  # Set CARGO_HOME to this folder to build Partsmith from these crates without the network (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).
  [source.crates-io]
  replace-with = "vendored-sources"

  [source.vendored-sources]
  directory = "rust-crates-{scope}/vendor"

  [net]
  offline = true
  ```

  Cargo resolves a relative `directory` in `$CARGO_HOME/config.toml` against the folder that holds `CARGO_HOME`. The path therefore starts with the folder's own name. With `CARGO_HOME` set to the full path of the folder, `cargo build`, `scripts/build-wasm.sh` and `cargo tauri build` take every crate from `vendor/` (environment variables cannot name a source replacement, and `cargo --config <file>` does not reach the cargo that a script runs). A relative `CARGO_HOME` would be read against the working folder of each cargo. `cargo tauri build` runs in `desktop/src-tauri`. The text is `vendorConfig('rust-crates-{scope}', 'Partsmith')` (The script's library, below). The crates archive of a native package's whole source has the same text with its own folder and the package's name in place of `Partsmith` (Source of the native packages, below).
- **Crates built for the build machine** (`web` only). The web core is built for `wasm32-unknown-emscripten` on Linux. Its build scripts and procedural macros compile for the Linux machine. The graph of 2.9 input 1 for `wasm32-unknown-emscripten` holds them today, but a crate that only the Linux target reaches would be missing from it. For `web`, the script also runs the command of 2.9 input 1 for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`. Among the crates that `partsmith-wasm` reaches there through normal and build edges, it takes every procedural macro crate (a target whose `kind` holds `proc-macro`) and every crate at the end of a build edge, and then every crate these reach through normal and build edges. When one of these registry crates is not in the web crate set, the script exits 1 with the build-machine message of section 4 before it writes anything. On 2026-11-04 there are 24 of them, all in the set. `ci.yml`'s `sources` runs the script in every CI run. A `Cargo.lock` change that brings in such a crate fails there, and with it `core-wasm`, `web` and `e2e` (The source with every artifact, First in the run). A crate behind an edge the wasm build never takes can fail the check too. That failure is safe. Either case is fixed by a change to 2.9 input 1, which then lists the crate in the web notices as well.
- **npm packages.** The archive holds one folder, `npm-packages-{scope}/`, with the tarball the npm registry serves for each package of the scope, at `{name}/-/{base}-{version}.tgz`. That is the path of its URL under `https://registry.npmjs.org/`. `{base}` is the name without its `@scope/`. The packages are those of the `packages` section of `ui/pnpm-lock.yaml` whose `os`, `cpu` and `libc` lists, where the entry has them, each allow one of the scope's platforms: (`linux`, `x64`, `glibc`) for `web`, and those and (`win32`, `x64`) and (`darwin`, `arm64`) for `desktop`. A list allows a value it holds, and every value but one it holds after a `!`. A platform whose `libc` is null, Windows and macOS, passes every `libc` list. That is the set pnpm installs on those platforms. The lock file records no `libc` list on 2026-11-04. The musl builds of rolldown, oxlint, oxfmt, lightningcss and Tailwind's oxide are then in the Linux set. pnpm fetches them on glibc too. The script downloads each tarball and checks its bytes against the `integrity` of its entry (`sha512-` or `sha1-` followed by the base64 of that hash) before it writes anything. A download that fails with a network error, or with an answer of 408, 429 or 500 and above, is tried again after 2 s and once more after 10 s (`DOWNLOAD_RETRY_DELAYS`). pnpm and cargo retry their downloads too. Any other answer, such as 404, and a tarball whose integrity differs fail at the first attempt. An entry whose `resolution` holds no `integrity` (a tarball URL, a folder or a Git repository; the lock file has none on 2026-11-04) fails the script. Served over HTTP, the folder is a registry for pnpm: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --registry <its URL>` finds each tarball at the path it asks for. pnpm also asks for `/pnpm`, its own metadata, and goes on when the answer is 404.
- **Licenses of the npm packages** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-05, ADR 0033's amendment of that date). Once every tarball of the scope is downloaded and checked, the script reads the allow list of `deny.toml` in the repository root with `allowedLicenses` of 2.10 and holds each package's license to it with `licenseAllowed`. The license is the `license` string of the tarball's `package.json`: the entry whose path is one folder followed by `package.json` (npm writes `package/`, and the tarballs of `@types` packages a folder named after the package), the first of them in the tarball's order when there are more. `tarballLicense` reads the tarball in Node (`zlib`'s gunzip, then the tar headers: the ustar name with its prefix, or the pax `path` or GNU long name before it) and runs no command. A tarball without such an entry, a `package.json` that is not valid JSON, and one with no `license` string (an object, or an older `licenses` list) count as `Unknown`, which no list allows. When a package fails, the script exits 1 with one line per failing package, in the lock file's order, each the message of 2.10 (section 4) with `{license}` as read here, and writes nothing. Measured on 2026-11-05 over the 160 tarballs of the desktop scope, which hold the web scope's 147: each has a `license` string. With MIT-0, Python-2.0 and BlueOak-1.0.0 in the list (2.10), all pass. Without those three, four fail: `@csstools/color-helpers` 6.1.2 and `@csstools/css-syntax-patches-for-csstree` 1.1.15 (MIT-0), `argparse` 2.0.1 (Python-2.0) and `lru-cache` 11.5.3 (BlueOak-1.0.0). The crates are not checked here. Those of `Cargo.lock` pass `cargo deny` (`deny`, 2.4). Those of the lock files of lightningcss and `tauri-cli` are the source of build tools, given out as published (the owner's rule, ADR 0033's amendment of 2026-11-05; Native packages, checked, below).
- **Native packages** (the owner's rule of 2026-11-05, ADR 0034's amendment of that date). A package is native when it holds prebuilt native code: its lock entry has an `os`, `cpu` or `libc` list (it is built for some platforms only), or its tarball holds a regular file whose name ends in `.node`, `.wasm`, `.so`, `.dylib`, `.dll` or `.exe`, in any letter case, or whose first bytes are those of prebuilt code (`PREBUILT_MAGIC_NUMBERS`, in hexadecimal): an ELF file (`7f454c46`), a Mach-O file (`feedface`, `feedfacf`, `cefaedfe` or `cffaedfe`) or a universal one (`cafebabe` or `cafebabf`), a PE file (`4d5a`, `MZ`), a WebAssembly module (`0061736d`) or an `ar` archive of objects (`213c617263683e0a`, `!<arch>` and LF). The first bytes find an executable whose name tells nothing, such as one with no extension (the owner's rule of 2026-11-05, ADR 0034's amendment of that date). A Java class file starts like a universal Mach-O file and counts too. That is safe. On 2026-11-05 these are the platform builds of rolldown, oxlint, oxfmt, Tailwind's oxide and lightningcss, fsevents in the desktop scope, and `playwright-core` 1.64.0, whose `lib/webp_codec.wasm` comes with no list. `NATIVE_PACKAGES` records each at the version the lock file holds, with the source it is built from and what that source asks for (Native packages, checked, below). A package belongs to an entry when its name is the entry's `name` or starts with `name` followed by `-`, and its version is the entry's `version`. Before it runs any `cargo` or `git` command or sends any request, the script exits 1 with the native-package message of section 4 for the first package of the scope, in the lock file's order, that has an `os`, `cpu` or `libc` list and belongs to no entry. Once every tarball is downloaded and checked, it exits 1 with the same message for the first package, in the lock file's order, whose tarball holds a file with one of those endings and that belongs to no entry. It reads the file names and first bytes in Node, as `tarballLicense` reads the license (Licenses of the npm packages, above). On 2026-11-05 no tarball of either scope holds a file that its first bytes alone make prebuilt. A new native package, or a new version of one, then stops `ci.yml`'s `sources` job, and with it `core-wasm`, `web` and `e2e`, until someone checks its source and records the result here and in the table. That failure is safe, as the build-machine check's is.
- **Source of the native packages.** For each entry that a native package of the scope belongs to (Native packages, above), each once and in the table's order, the npm archive holds what the entry asks for in a second folder, `npm-sources-{scope}/`. When no entry asks for anything, there is no such folder. `{stem}` is an entry's name without its leading `@` and with `/` written as `-` (`oxfmt-binding`, `lightningcss`), as `npm pack` names the tarball of a scoped package.
  - An entry with a `commit` (lightningcss) gives the whole tree of its `tag`. The script clones it with `git clone --quiet --depth 1 --branch {tag} {source} {clone}`, where `{clone}` is `{temporary folder}/clones/{stem}-{version}`, and checks that `git -C {clone} rev-parse "{tag}^{commit}"` prints the entry's `commit`. A failed clone is tried again after each of `DOWNLOAD_RETRY_DELAYS`, as a download is, with the partial clone removed first. After the third attempt the script exits 1 with the command message of section 4 for that attempt. When the tag names another commit, it exits 1 with the tag-commit message of section 4 and writes nothing. It writes `{stem}-{version}-source.tar.gz` with `git -C {clone} -c core.autocrlf=false -c tar.umask=0002 -c core.attributesFile=/dev/null archive --format=tar --prefix={stem}-{version}/ {tag}` and `gzip -9 -n`, three of the four options of the OCCT archive (Bytes, above). It does not set `core.eol=lf`. The tags of 1.32.0 and 1.33.0 hold no `.gitattributes`. With `core.autocrlf=false`, Git then treats no file as text. `core.eol` does not reach the bytes (measured on 2026-11-05 with `core.eol=crlf`).
  - The entries with a `commit` and the same `name` give one `{stem}-crates.tar.gz`, the folder `{stem}-crates/` with `config.toml` (`vendorConfig('{stem}-crates', '{name}')`) and `vendor/`. First the script reads `{clone}/Cargo.lock` of each of them with `lockCrates` (The script's library, below). A package there whose `source` is neither absent nor crates.io's, `registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index`, exits 1 with the tool-lock source message of section 4 and writes nothing. Such a source is a `git+` source or another registry. That `config.toml` replaces only crates.io. An offline build would not find such a package. `vendor/` is what `cargo vendor --locked --versioned-dirs --manifest-path {clone}/{manifest} {vendor}` writes for the first of them, with `--sync {clone}/{manifest}` for each further one in the table's order, sent through `run` in the repository root. It holds every registry crate of their lock files whole, those of every platform and the development ones included. `cargo vendor` checks each one against its lock file's checksum. With `CARGO_HOME` set to the full path of that folder, `cargo build --locked --release -p lightningcss_node` in `lightningcss-{version}/` builds the shared library that the native packages ship as `lightningcss.{platform}.node`, without the network.
  - Each crate of an entry's `crates` gives `{crate}-{version}.crate`, downloaded from `{crateRegistry}{crate}/{crate}-{version}.crate` with the retries of the npm downloads and checked against the entry's `sha256`, the checksum that the lock file of the entry's source records. `CRATE_REGISTRY` is crates.io's download host, the `dl` of its index's `config.json`. Its storage serves a crate at `{name}/{name}-{version}.crate`. A failed download exits 1 with the crate download message of section 4, and other bytes with the crate checksum message. A crate that two entries name is written once.
  - An entry with `notices` gives `{stem}-{version}-notices.txt`, a copy of the committed file `ui/scripts/notices/native/{stem}-{version}.txt` (the owner's rule of 2026-11-05, ADR 0034's amendment of that date). That file holds the license notices of the crates that the entry's native packages compile in, which their tarballs do not carry. `ui/scripts/native-notices.mjs` writes it from the entry's source at the commit that `notices` pins (The notices of the native packages, below). When the file is missing, the script exits 1 with the native-notices message of section 4 and writes nothing.
- **The crates of tauri-cli** (`desktop` only, the owner's decision of 2026-11-05). `TAURI_CLI` holds the version of 2.2, `WINDOWS_INSTALLER.tauriCli` of 2.9 itself (test 112), and the SHA-256 of its `.crate`, which the crates.io index records for that version. Before it runs any `cargo` command or sends any request, the script compares the real path of Node's `os.tmpdir()` with the real path of the repository root. When the first is the root or a folder inside it, the script exits 1 with the temporary-folder message of section 4. Inside the repository, Cargo would take the extracted crate for a member of Partsmith's workspace and refuse it (measured on 2026-11-05). The script downloads `{crateRegistry}tauri-cli/tauri-cli-{version}.crate` as above, checks it against that SHA-256, and extracts it with GNU tar into its temporary folder as `tauri-cli-{version}/`, which holds the crate's own `Cargo.lock`. It reads that lock file with `lockCrates`. A package there whose `source` is neither absent nor crates.io's exits 1 with the tool-lock source message of section 4 and writes nothing (none on 2026-11-05: 859 of its 860 packages come from crates.io, and the 860th is `tauri-cli` itself, with no source). The desktop vendor command is then `cargo vendor --locked --versioned-dirs --sync {temporary folder}/tauri-cli-{version}/Cargo.toml {vendor}`, which adds every crate of that lock file. The folder `{name}-{version}` of each package with a source stays whole (Rust crates, above). After the stubs and the files left out (below), the folder `tauri-cli-{version}/` moves to `vendor/tauri-cli-{version}/` and the script writes its `.cargo-checksum.json`: `{"files":{...},"package":"{sha256}"}` with the SHA-256 of every regular file of the folder by path in byte order, in the compact form that `cargo vendor` writes. Every file of the crate stays, the eleven `.gitignore` files of its templates among them. With `CARGO_HOME` set to the full path of `rust-crates-desktop`, `cargo install tauri-cli --version {version} --locked` finds the crate in `vendor/`, builds it without the network and installs `cargo-tauri` in `rust-crates-desktop/bin/`. Cargo looks for subcommands in `$CARGO_HOME/bin`. `cargo tauri build` then finds it. No toolchain comes with the archive, as the owner decided.
- **Files left out** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-05). For each crate that stays whole and that `EXCLUDED_LICENSE_FILES` of 2.9 names, the script removes the entry's `path` from the crate's folder (a file, or for a path that ends in `/`, that folder and every file below it) and from `files` of its `.cargo-checksum.json`, whose `package` stays. Cargo checks only the files that `files` lists. On 2026-11-05 this leaves out `vendor/dbus/` of `libdbus-sys` 0.2.7 in the desktop archive: 304 files of libdbus under `AFL-2.1 OR GPL-2.0-or-later`, 1,108,114 bytes compressed, which only the crate's `vendored` feature compiles and no Partsmith build enables. The notices leave out the same files (2.9 input 3).
- **Bytes.** GNU tar packs each folder, with `LC_ALL=C` and `--sort=name --mtime=@0 --owner=0 --group=0 --numeric-owner --format=pax --pax-option=exthdr.name=%d/PaxHeaders/%f,delete=atime,delete=ctime --mode=u+rwX,go+rX,go-w`. These fix the order, the times, the owners and the modes of the entries. The crates' tar goes through `gzip -9 -n`. The packages' tar stays as it is: its tarballs are gzip files already, and gzip took 0.5 percent off it. It packs `npm-packages-{scope}` and then `npm-sources-{scope}` in one run. The files of `npm-sources-{scope}` are gzip files too, apart from the notices files: the source archives of `git archive` and `gzip -9 -n`, the crates folders packed with the options above and `gzip -9 -n`, and the `.crate` files as crates.io serves them. The same lock files and pins with the same cargo, git, tar and gzip give the same bytes. The script checks that `tar --version` names GNU tar.
- **Parts.** Each archive is written as parts of 25,165,824 bytes (24 MiB), the last one shorter, named `{archive}.part-00`, `{archive}.part-01` and so on, with two digits from 00. A part stays 1 MiB under Pages' limit (3.5). The script writes parts for an archive of one part too. The names then never depend on its size. `cat` of the parts in name order gives the archive.
- **Output.** The script works in a temporary folder outside `<directory>` and removes it on exit. It moves the parts into `<directory>` only once both archives are complete. A failure writes nothing there. It then prints one line per archive, the crates first, `Wrote {archive} to {directory}: {bytes} bytes, SHA-256 {sha256}, last part {archive}.part-{NN}.`, and exits 0.
- **Failures** (section 4). Other arguments than a scope and a directory exit 2 with the usage line. A missing `<directory>`, a `tar` that is not GNU tar, a failing command (`cargo vendor`, `cargo metadata`, `git`, `tar` or `gzip`), a lock entry without `integrity`, a native package that `NATIVE_PACKAGES` does not record, a failed download of a tarball or a crate (after its third attempt when it is tried again), a tarball whose integrity differs, a crate whose SHA-256 differs, a clone that fails three times, a tag that names another commit, a package of a tool's lock file from a source other than crates.io, a missing notices file of a native package, an npm package under a license outside the list and, for `desktop`, a temporary folder inside the repository each exit 1 with their message. A part that is already in `<directory>` exits 1 with the error of Node's file system (`EEXIST`). That file stays as it was.
- **Needs.** Node 22, the cargo of `rust-toolchain.toml` with the network (or with every crate it vendors in Cargo's cache), the network to `registry.npmjs.org`, `static.crates.io` and `github.com` (the sources of the native packages), git, GNU tar and gzip. For `desktop`, `TMPDIR` names a folder outside the repository, or is unset.

**The script's library.** The command and the tests use these exports. `run(command, args, directory)` is the `run` of 2.9: it resolves to the command's standard output. Every `cargo` command goes through it, in the repository root. `git`, `tar`, `gzip` and `tar --version` run directly. `fetch(url)` is Node's `fetch` in the command.

```js
/**
 * The two scopes: the root crate and the triples whose crates each archive holds (2.9 input 1), and the platforms whose
 * npm packages it holds, as `{ os, cpu, libc }` with `libc` null where pnpm checks none.
 */
export const SCOPES = {
  web: {
    rootCrate: 'partsmith-wasm',
    triples: ['wasm32-unknown-emscripten'],
    platforms: [{ os: 'linux', cpu: 'x64', libc: 'glibc' }],
  },
  desktop: {
    rootCrate: 'partsmith-desktop',
    triples: DESKTOP_TARGETS.map((target) => target.triple),
    platforms: [
      { os: 'linux', cpu: 'x64', libc: 'glibc' },
      { os: 'win32', cpu: 'x64', libc: null },
      { os: 'darwin', cpu: 'arm64', libc: null },
    ],
  },
}

/** The size of every part but the last: 24 MiB. */
export const PART_SIZE = 25165824

/** The registry the command downloads the tarballs from. */
export const NPM_REGISTRY = 'https://registry.npmjs.org/'

/**
 * How long a download waits before its second and its third attempt, in milliseconds. Only a network error and an
 * answer of 408, 429 or 500 and above are tried again.
 */
export const DOWNLOAD_RETRY_DELAYS = [2000, 10000]

/** crates.io's download host, the `dl` of its index's `config.json`. Its storage serves a crate at `{name}/{name}-{version}.crate`. */
export const CRATE_REGISTRY = 'https://static.crates.io/crates/'

/** The source that Cargo lock files write for a crate from crates.io, the one source the archives' config.toml replaces. */
export const CRATES_IO_SOURCE = 'registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index'

/**
 * The tauri-cli of 2.2, `WINDOWS_INSTALLER.tauriCli` of 2.9, and the SHA-256 of its `.crate`, which the crates.io index
 * records for that version. The desktop crate archive holds it and the crates of its own lock file.
 */
export const TAURI_CLI = {
  version: WINDOWS_INSTALLER.tauriCli,
  sha256: '7a08bf51957aad7b46276ba4242b2edeb80b6afae2b42b9547f9b9ae485db465',
}

/**
 * The npm packages of prebuilt native code, each at the version whose source was checked (Native packages, checked).
 * A package belongs to an entry when its name is `name` or starts with `name` followed by `-`, and its version is
 * `version`. `source` and `tag` name what it is built from. `commit` is the commit of `tag` when the archives hold the
 * tag's whole tree, with the crates of the lock file of `manifest`. `crates` are the crates under MPL-2.0 that it
 * compiles in, as `{ name, version, sha256 }` with the checksum that the lock file of its source records. `notices` is
 * there when its tarballs lack the notices of the crates it compiles in, which `ui/scripts/notices/native/` holds, as
 * `{ crate, commit }`: the crate of its source that builds it and the commit of `tag` that `ui/scripts/native-notices.mjs`
 * makes the file from.
 */
export const NATIVE_PACKAGES = [
  {
    name: '@oxfmt/binding',
    version: '0.72.0',
    source: 'https://github.com/oxc-project/oxc',
    tag: 'oxfmt_v0.72.0',
    crates: [],
    notices: { crate: 'oxfmt', commit: '2bd08ebe8f36fcf1954a675ffdeb4c6d0129f609' },
  },
  {
    name: '@oxlint/binding',
    version: '1.87.0',
    source: 'https://github.com/oxc-project/oxc',
    tag: 'oxlint_v1.87.0',
    crates: [],
    notices: { crate: 'oxlint', commit: '2bd08ebe8f36fcf1954a675ffdeb4c6d0129f609' },
  },
  {
    name: '@rolldown/binding',
    version: '1.2.13',
    source: 'https://github.com/rolldown/rolldown',
    tag: 'v1.2.13',
    crates: [],
    notices: {
      crate: 'rolldown_binding',
      commit: 'f7de35f45bcd4cb8e4b6d8d11e74fbfb20b03127',
    },
  },
  {
    name: '@tailwindcss/oxide',
    version: '4.3.3',
    source: 'https://github.com/tailwindlabs/tailwindcss',
    tag: 'v4.3.3',
    crates: [
      {
        name: 'bexpand',
        version: '1.2.0',
        sha256: '045d7d9db8390cf2c59f39f3bd138f1962ef616b096d1b9f5651c7acba19e5a7',
      },
    ],
    notices: { crate: 'tailwind-oxide', commit: 'c2b24dd15fed1c59dd521bd86082f520c9f5ad0d' },
  },
  {
    name: 'fsevents',
    version: '2.3.3',
    source: 'https://github.com/fsevents/fsevents',
    tag: 'v2.3.3',
    crates: [],
  },
  {
    name: 'lightningcss',
    version: '1.32.0',
    source: 'https://github.com/parcel-bundler/lightningcss',
    tag: 'v1.32.0',
    commit: '7f8a861bdee476fe90c89a8badeb3fd33a99c51a',
    manifest: 'node/Cargo.toml',
    crates: [],
  },
  {
    name: 'lightningcss',
    version: '1.33.0',
    source: 'https://github.com/parcel-bundler/lightningcss',
    tag: 'v1.33.0',
    commit: 'c6a0c3cebf3395635e61075d2c81a96a710d4910',
    manifest: 'node/Cargo.toml',
    crates: [],
  },
  {
    name: 'playwright-core',
    version: '1.64.0',
    source: 'https://github.com/microsoft/playwright',
    tag: 'v1.64.0',
    crates: [],
  },
]

/**
 * Every entry of the `packages` section of a pnpm lock file's text (lockfileVersion 9.0), in the file's order, as
 * `{ key, name, version, integrity, os, cpu, libc }`, with null for a field the entry lacks.
 */
export function lockPackages(lockText)

/** The entries of `packages` whose `os`, `cpu` and `libc` lists allow one of `platforms`, in their order. */
export function platformPackages(packages, platforms)

/** The path of a package's tarball under the registry: `{name}/-/{base}-{version}.tgz`. */
export function tarballPath(name, version)

/** null when `bytes` have the hash `integrity` names; otherwise the integrity of `bytes` with the same algorithm. */
export function integrityMismatch(bytes, integrity)

/**
 * Writes `file` into `directory` as parts of `partSize` bytes named `{base name of file}.part-{NN}`, the last one
 * shorter, and resolves to their names in order.
 */
export async function writeParts(file, directory, partSize = PART_SIZE)

/**
 * The exact text of `{folder}/config.toml` for the crates that build `subject` without the network: the text of 2.13
 * with `{folder}/vendor` as the directory and `subject` in its first line.
 */
export function vendorConfig(folder, subject)

/** The exact text of `rust-crates-{scope}/config.toml`: `vendorConfig('rust-crates-{scope}', 'Partsmith')`. */
export function cargoConfig(scope)

/** The names of the two archives of `scope`, crates first: `[rust-crates-{scope}.tar.gz, npm-packages-{scope}.tar]`. */
export function archiveNames(scope)

/**
 * The entries of `nativePackages` that the packages of `packages` with an `os`, `cpu` or `libc` list belong to, each
 * once, in the table's order. Throws an `Error` with the native-package message of section 4 for the first such
 * package, in the order of `packages`, that belongs to none.
 */
export function nativeEntries(packages, nativePackages)

/**
 * The license of the npm tarball at `file`: the `license` string of its `package.json`, or `Unknown` (2.13). It reads
 * the tarball in Node (`zlib`'s gunzip, then the tar headers: the ustar name with its prefix, or the pax `path` or GNU
 * long name before it) and runs no command.
 */
export async function tarballLicense(file)

/**
 * Every `[[package]]` of a Cargo lock file's text, in the file's order, as `{ name, version, source }`, with `source`
 * null for a package that has none (a member of the lock file's workspace).
 */
export function lockCrates(lockText)

/**
 * Clones the tag of the table entry `entry` into the new folder `clone` with the shallow `git clone` and the retries
 * of Source of the native packages, and checks that the tag names `commit`. Rejects with the tag-commit message of
 * section 4 when it names another.
 */
export async function cloneCheckedTag(entry, commit, clone, retryDelays)

/** The committed notices file of a table entry with `notices`, relative to the repository root. */
export function nativeNoticesFile(entry)

/**
 * Writes the parts of both archives of `scope` for the repository at `root` into `directory`, downloading each tarball
 * from `registry` and each crate from `crateRegistry` with `fetch` and sending every `cargo` command through `run`. A
 * download that fails with a network error or a retried status, and a failed `git clone`, wait each of `retryDelays`
 * in turn and try again.
 * `nativePackages` and `tauriCli` take the place of NATIVE_PACKAGES and TAURI_CLI. Resolves to one
 * `{ archive, bytes, sha256, lastPart }` per archive, crates first, where `bytes` is the archive's size, `sha256` its
 * SHA-256 in hexadecimal and `lastPart` the name of its last part. Rejects with the message of section 4 and leaves
 * `directory` as it was on any failure.
 */
export async function writeDependencySources(
  root,
  scope,
  directory,
  {
    registry,
    crateRegistry,
    fetch,
    run,
    retryDelays = DOWNLOAD_RETRY_DELAYS,
    nativePackages = NATIVE_PACKAGES,
    tauriCli = TAURI_CLI,
  },
)

/** The line the command prints for one result of `writeDependencySources` written into `directory` (Output, above). */
export function summaryLine({ archive, bytes, sha256, lastPart }, directory)
```

The crate set of a scope is `scopeCrates(root, run, SCOPES[scope].triples, SCOPES[scope].rootCrate)` of `ui/scripts/notices.mjs` (2.9). For `web` the library then runs the command of input 1 for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` (Crates built for the build machine, above), and for both scopes `cargo vendor --locked --versioned-dirs <vendor folder>` (for `desktop` with `--sync` and the manifest of `tauri-cli` before the folder) and `cargo metadata --format-version 1 --locked --all-features`, whose targets the stubs take. The library works in this order: it checks `<directory>` and GNU tar, and for `desktop` the temporary folder; it reads the scope's packages from the lock file and requires an integrity for each and an entry of `nativePackages` for each one with an `os`, `cpu` or `libc` list; it computes the crate set and, for `web`, makes the build-machine check; for `desktop` it downloads, checks and extracts `tauri-cli` and checks the sources of its lock file; it vendors the crates, makes the stubs and leaves out the excluded files; for `desktop` it moves `tauri-cli` in and writes its checksum file; it downloads and checks the tarballs, then the prebuilt files they hold, then their licenses; it writes `npm-sources-{scope}`; and it packs both archives and writes their parts. Nothing reaches `<directory>` before the parts. The notices and the archives share one copy of each step of input 1: `cargoMetadata` runs the command of input 1 (without `--filter-platform` when it gets no triple), `reachableIds` follows the normal and build edges, and `workspacePackage` finds the root crate, all three exports of `ui/scripts/notices.mjs`. They also share `checkedRun` of `check-licenses.mjs`, which gives a failed command its message. The command passes `NPM_REGISTRY`, `CRATE_REGISTRY`, Node's `fetch` and `runCommand` of `check-licenses.mjs`, and leaves `retryDelays`, `nativePackages` and `tauriCli` at their defaults.

**In every deployment.** The step `Write the dependency source archives` of `ci.yml`'s `sources` job (The source with every artifact, below) comes after `Write the Partsmith source archive` and before the upload, after a step that runs `rustup show active-toolchain || rustup toolchain install`. It runs in the repository root, sets no `shell` and runs these lines:

```sh
mkdir sources/dependencies
node scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs web sources/dependencies
cd sources/dependencies
export LC_ALL=C
sha256sum -- * > SHA256SUMS
```

`export LC_ALL=C` comes on a line of its own, as in `draft`'s step `Write SHA256SUMS`: the shell sorts the glob in its own locale, and a prefix assignment would reach only `sha256sum`. `web` downloads `sources-web` into `ui/dist`. Every deployment, previews included, then holds `/dependencies/SHA256SUMS` and the parts it lists, in byte order of their names, in the line format of `sha256sum`. `check:dist` holds each part to the 25 MiB limit like every other file (3.5). `deploy`'s step `Check the dependency source archives` (2.4) comes after `Check the OCCT source archive` and before Wrangler. The step prints the dependency-source error of section 4 and fails the job unless all of these hold: `ui/dist/dependencies/SHA256SUMS` lists `rust-crates-web.tar.gz.part-00` and `npm-packages-web.tar.part-00`; every file it lists has the SHA-256 it names (`sha256sum --check --strict`); it lists every file of the folder whose name holds `.part-`; the joined parts of the crates' archive are a whole gzip file (`gzip -t`); and `tar -t` reads the joined parts of the packages' archive to its end. GNU tar reads a tar cut between two entries, or inside an entry's header, as whole (measured with GNU tar 1.35). Only `SHA256SUMS` then shows the missing part. The step checks what the build holds and does not make the archives again, which would need the network.

**In every release.** `release.yml`'s `sources` job (The source with every artifact, below) installs the toolchain of `rust-toolchain.toml` and Node 22. Its step `Write the dependency source archives` runs `node scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs desktop sources` in the repository root, with no `shell` and no `env`, after `Write the Partsmith source archive` and before the upload of `sources-desktop`. The archives come from the lock files of the commit the run checked out: in a tag run the tagged commit, which the `bundle` jobs build from. `draft` downloads them with the rest of `sources-desktop` into `release/` before `Write SHA256SUMS`, which then covers every part. The dry run before each tag (3.6) runs the same job on its own commit. A lock file whose tarballs the script cannot download or check, or whose crates it cannot vendor, then fails the dry run and not the tag. Tests 91 and 92 run the script's logic on fixtures, and test 97 runs it for real by hand. A failed `draft` is run again with `gh run rerun <id> --failed` while the run's `bundle-*` and `sources-desktop` artifacts last (7 and 8 days, 3.6).

**Scope, measured** (2026-11-04, the lock files of commit `dc2fdb8`, cargo 1.97.0, GNU tar 1.35 and gzip 1.12). Each artifact's archives hold the least that is still complete: what its build compiles or installs, and the stubs Cargo needs to resolve. Since 2026-11-05 they also hold the whole lock files of `tauri-cli` and lightningcss (The crates of tauri-cli; Source of the native packages). The whole lock file is the simple superset. The table gives both:

A deployment then holds 88,479,008 bytes of dependency archives in 5 parts, and a release 183,310,159 bytes in 8 parts. With the whole lock files a deployment would hold about 504 MB in 21 parts, nearly all of it builds for other platforms. Leaving the musl builds out of the Linux set would save 27,099,553 bytes. pnpm installs without them (a 404 for an optional package only skips it). However, the lock file does not mark them. A rule by name would be a guess. The set pnpm fetches is the one an offline install shows complete. `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs` wrote the archives of the table. Two runs of each scope wrote the same parts, and so did a third run of the web scope with an empty Cargo cache, which downloaded every crate. The web scope took 15.3 s with every crate in Cargo's cache and 24.5 s with an empty one, and the desktop scope 34.0 s with a warm cache. The rows since 2026-11-05 are what the script wrote on 2026-11-05 from the same lock files, at `0ae7337`, with the contents of that date and git 2.43.0. `rust-crates-web.tar.gz` stayed the same bytes. Two runs of each scope wrote the same parts. With every crate in Cargo's cache, the web scope took 53 s and the desktop scope 152 s. The desktop scope's temporary folder held about 2.8 GB at its peak. The rows since 2026-11-06 are what the script wrote on 2026-11-06, after the `zip` crate stopped building `zopfli` (io.md open issue 5). `zopfli` 0.8.3 left `Cargo.lock` and both crate archives. `bumpalo` 3.20.3 became a stub in both, and `log` 0.4.34 a stub in the web archive. The desktop archive still holds `zopfli` 0.8.1 whole, as a crate of `tauri-cli`'s lock file. Both npm archives kept their bytes. The whole `Cargo.lock` lost the 188,720 bytes of `zopfli` 0.8.3's folder. Its row is `cargo vendor --locked --versioned-dirs` of the lock file in `rust-crates-web/` with the scope's `config.toml`, packed with the script's tar options and `gzip -9 -n`. That `config.toml` has a first comment line of 127 bytes, which the one of 2026-11-04 did not have. A deployment then holds 145,421,225 bytes of dependency archives in 7 parts, and a release 355,506,449 bytes in 15 parts.

**What the archives gain, measured** (2026-11-05, the lock files of `0ae7337`, cargo 1.97.0, git 2.43.0, GNU tar 1.35 and gzip 1.12). These parts were measured one by one, before the script wrote them, and the notices files when they were made. The table above gives the archives that the script then wrote, with their SHA-256s (test 114).

| Part | Scope | Contents | Uncompressed (bytes) | Compressed (bytes) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `lightningcss-1.32.0-source.tar.gz` | both | the tree of the tag `v1.32.0`, 260 entries, SHA-256 `f5b3bf8932458a69fd446f0d3bfe6a82bedad18d20a0d8eb66fa7783ef1f5a7b` | 11,284,480 as tar | 7,556,568 |
| `lightningcss-1.33.0-source.tar.gz` | both | the tree of the tag `v1.33.0`, 266 entries, SHA-256 `e33c8f58de66dcdf8735f88596431c17509a1e08e4331f816a173d99cb68b3aa` | 11,345,920 as tar | 7,566,545 |
| `lightningcss-crates.tar.gz` | both | 243 crates: the 201 registry crates of the lock file of `v1.32.0` and the 233 of `v1.33.0`, most of them shared | 344,407,961 | 40,526,686 |
| `bexpand-1.2.0.crate` | both | the crate as crates.io serves it | | 13,447 |
| `tauri-cli-2.12.2` and the crates of its lock file | desktop | the crate and the 771 crates of its lock file that the desktop archive did not hold whole (741 outside `Cargo.lock`, 30 there as stubs); 88 more it holds whole already | | 116,194,489 |
| `vendor/dbus/` of `libdbus-sys` 0.2.7 | desktop | 304 files, left out | | 1,108,114 less |
| `rolldown-binding-1.2.13-notices.txt` | both | 336 crates, 46 of them rolldown's own, and 160 texts, stored as is | 526,721 | |
| `oxlint-binding-1.87.0-notices.txt` | both | 265 crates, 26 of them oxc's own, and 136 texts, stored as is | 418,131 | |
| `oxfmt-binding-0.72.0-notices.txt` | both | 218 crates, 29 of them oxc's own, and 113 texts, stored as is | 344,249 | |

When the script first wrote them, `npm-packages-web.tar` grew from 83,374,080 bytes to 140,523,520 in 6 parts, and a deployment holds 145,628,448 bytes of dependency archives in 7 parts. `npm-packages-desktop.tar` grew to 190,965,760 bytes in 8 parts, and `rust-crates-desktop.tar.gz` to 164,702,423 in 7 parts. A release then holds 355,668,183 bytes in 15 parts. Every part stays at 24 MiB or less (Parts, above). One vendor folder serves both lightningcss versions (`--sync`): two folders would hold 36,585,912 and 39,406,486 bytes compressed. Vendoring the two lock files took 16.1 s with a cold Cargo cache. Vendoring `tauri-cli`'s lock file took 45 s, and packing and compressing its 898,989,499 bytes of crates 53 s. The `.crate` of `tauri-cli` 2.12.2 is 2,016,863 bytes with SHA-256 `7a08bf51957aad7b46276ba4242b2edeb80b6afae2b42b9547f9b9ae485db465`, the `cksum` of the crates.io index. It holds 276 files and a `Cargo.lock` of 860 packages, 859 from crates.io. The four notices files of `ui/scripts/notices/native/` (Source of the native packages, above) hold 1,475,740 bytes. A crate from crates.io that ships no license text and takes no standard one got the files at the root of its repository's default branch on 2026-11-05: 47 crates in rolldown's file, 16 in oxlint's, 14 in oxfmt's and 6 in Tailwind's, from 19 repositories. `ui/scripts/notices/repositories/` holds copies of those files, 25 files of 103,178 bytes (The notices of the native packages, below). The crates that oxc publishes take oxc's `LICENSE` and `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSE`, the notices of the code oxc ports from other projects, which the tarballs of `oxlint` and `oxfmt` lack. The tag `v1.33.0` of lightningcss names `c6a0c3c`, the commit after the one that npm records as `gitHead` for 1.33.0 (`1d680fa`). They differ only in `rust-toolchain.toml` (1.92.0 to 1.96.0). The owner's decision names the tag. For 1.32.0 the tag and `gitHead` are the same commit.

**Native packages, checked** (2026-11-05, `NATIVE_PACKAGES`). Each source's lock file was read at its tag with `cargo metadata --locked --all-features` over a checkout of its manifests, following the normal and build edges from the crate that builds the binding, on every platform:

- `@rolldown/binding` 1.2.13 (tag `v1.2.13`, commit `f7de35f45bcd4cb8e4b6d8d11e74fbfb20b03127`): 406 packages, 353 from crates.io. `rolldown_binding` compiles in no crate under MPL-2.0 or a copyleft license alone. `self_cell` 1.3.0 (`Apache-2.0 OR GPL-2.0-only`) and `r-efi` 6.0.0 (`MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR LGPL-2.1-or-later`, for UEFI targets only) each offer a permissive license. `colored` 2.2.0 and 3.1.1 (MPL-2.0) are in the lock file, outside the binding's graph.
- `@oxlint/binding` 1.87.0 and `@oxfmt/binding` 0.72.0 (tags `oxlint_v1.87.0` and `oxfmt_v0.72.0`, both commit `2bd08ebe8f36fcf1954a675ffdeb4c6d0129f609`): 437 packages, 371 from crates.io, with the same three findings as rolldown for the crates `oxlint` and `oxfmt`.
- `@tailwindcss/oxide` 4.3.3 (tag `v4.3.3`, commit `c2b24dd15fed1c59dd521bd86082f520c9f5ad0d`): 105 packages, 101 from crates.io. `tailwind-oxide` compiles in `bexpand` 1.2.0, under MPL-2.0 alone, through `tailwindcss-oxide` on every platform. MPL-2.0 section 3.2(a) asks for its Source Code Form with the executable. The archives carry `bexpand-1.2.0.crate` (the owner's rule, ADR 0034's amendment of 2026-11-05). The lock file records its SHA-256, `045d7d9db8390cf2c59f39f3bd138f1962ef616b096d1b9f5651c7acba19e5a7`.
- `fsevents` 2.3.3 (desktop only, macOS; tag `v2.3.3`, commit `2db891e51aa0f2975c5eaaf6aa30f13d720a830a`): its `fsevents.node` is built from one C file of its own, `src/fsevents.c` (MIT), against macOS frameworks, with no third-party code.
- `playwright-core` 1.64.0 (both scopes, with no `os`, `cpu` or `libc` list; tag `v1.64.0`, commit `606ecd8b34bac100db32561b6278ffd76e5eb3cd`): its `lib/webp_codec.wasm` is libwebp (BSD-3-Clause) built with Emscripten by `utils/libwebp-wasm/build.sh` of its repository. The tarball carries the notices of both next to it, in `lib/webp_codec.LICENSE`. No source is owed.
- `lightningcss` 1.32.0 and 1.33.0 (MPL-2.0): the whole source. Its Node module compiles in, from its own tag, `lightningcss`, `lightningcss-napi`, `lightningcss-derive`, `parcel_selectors`, `static-self` and `static-self-derive`, and from crates.io `cssparser` (0.33.0 or 0.37.0), `cssparser-color` (0.1.0 or 0.5.0), `cssparser-macros` (0.6.1 or 0.7.0), `dtoa-short` 0.3.5 and `serde-detach` 0.0.1, all under MPL-2.0, besides permissive crates.

The tarballs of `@rolldown/binding-*`, `@oxlint/binding-*` and `@oxfmt/binding-*` hold only `README.md`, `package.json` and the `.node` file, with no license file (measured on 2026-11-05 in `ui/node_modules/.pnpm`). The MIT notices of rolldown, oxlint and oxfmt themselves are in their wrapper packages `rolldown`, `oxlint` and `oxfmt`, in the same archive. The tarballs of `@tailwindcss/oxide-*` carry Tailwind's `LICENSE`. No binding tarball carries the notices of the crates compiled into its `.node` file, under MIT, Apache-2.0 and other permissive licenses. The archives carry them for those four entries (`notices`, Source of the native packages, above), by the owner's rule (ADR 0034's amendment of 2026-11-05). lightningcss's tarballs carry its `LICENSE`. The crates it compiles in come with their own files in `lightningcss-crates.tar.gz`.

**The notices of the native packages** (`ui/scripts/native-notices.mjs`, the owner's rule of 2026-11-05, ADR 0034's amendment of that date). `node scripts/native-notices.mjs` in `ui/`, with no argument, writes `ui/scripts/notices/native/{stem}-{version}.txt` for each entry of `NATIVE_PACKAGES` with `notices`, one at a time in the table's order. It prints `Wrote {file} from {source} at {tag}.` for each and exits 0. With any argument it exits 2 with `usage: node scripts/native-notices.mjs` on standard error. An entry's `notices` is `{ crate, commit }`: the crate of the source's workspace that builds the binding, and the commit of `tag`. For each entry the script:

1. Clones `tag` into a new temporary folder with `cloneCheckedTag` of the archive script: the clone and the retries of Source of the native packages (above), and the tag-commit message of section 4 when the tag names another commit than `notices.commit`. The folder is removed at the end.
2. Runs `cargo metadata --format-version 1 --locked --all-features --manifest-path {clone}/Cargo.toml` through `run` in the repository root (`cargoMetadata` of 2.9 with a manifest path). The toolchain of Partsmith's `rust-toolchain.toml` then reads the source's lock file. The crates are every package that `notices.crate` reaches through normal and build edges (`reachableIds`) on every platform, that crate and the other crates of the source's workspace included, sorted by name and version (`compareNameAndVersion`).
3. Gives each crate the entry line of 2.9: `{name} {version}, {license}, {url}`, then `, standard text` or `, text from its repository` when its texts come from there, then `, texts` and ` [{n}]` for each of its texts. `{license}` is `see texts` for a crate without one. `{url}` is `{source}/tree/{tag}` for a crate of the source's workspace and `https://crates.io/crates/{name}/{version}` for a crate from crates.io. A crate from another source exits 1 with the native-crate source message of section 4 before any text is read.
4. Finds each crate's texts as 2.9 input 3 finds and normalizes them, with `EXCLUDED_LICENSE_FILES`. A crate without a license file takes the standard text that its license allows alone, with `/` read as `OR` (`MIT/Apache-2.0` takes the Apache-2.0 text). Otherwise it takes the texts of its repository. For a crate of the source's workspace these are the regular files at the root of the clone whose names start like a license file's. For a crate from crates.io they are every regular file in `ui/scripts/notices/repositories/{host}/{owner}/{name}/`, in byte order of names. The folder comes from the host and the first two path segments of the `repository` URL its manifest names, without a final `.git` (`repositoryFolder`). Each such folder holds committed copies of the license files at the root of that repository. Every crate and version from the repository shares them. A missing folder, a crate whose manifest names no such URL, and a workspace crate whose clone holds no license file at its root each exit 1 with their message of section 4.
5. Writes the file with `renderCrateNotices` of `ui/scripts/notices.mjs`: the entry lines, then each distinct text once as `[{n}]`, a blank line and the text, numbered in the order the lines first name them, with blank lines between the blocks and one final LF. It is UTF-8 with LF line endings. Its license texts are copied verbatim and keep their em dashes, as the copies of 2.9 do (ADR 0033, way 1, which the owner's rule of 2026-11-05 extends to `ui/scripts/notices/native/` and `ui/scripts/notices/repositories/`).

The same commits, lock files and copies give the same bytes. A failure leaves the files of the earlier entries written and its own file as it was. The command needs Node 22, git, the cargo of `rust-toolchain.toml`, and the network to the sources and to crates.io (or every crate in Cargo's cache). CI does not run it. Test 115 holds the committed files to this layout in every CI run. Test 114 runs the command whenever `NATIVE_PACKAGES` changes. The script takes 2.9's text lookup from `ui/scripts/notices.mjs`, whose generator uses the same exports: `folderTexts`, `standardLicense`, `standardText`, `copiedTexts`, `rootLicenseTexts`, `excludedPaths`, `readInOrder` and `renderCrateNotices`. The script's library:

```js
/**
 * The folder under ui/scripts/notices/repositories/ of the repository at `url`: `{host}/{owner}/{name}`, from the
 * host and the first two segments of its path, without a final `.git`. Null when `url` is null or has no such path.
 */
export function repositoryFolder(url)

/**
 * The text of the notices file of the table entry `entry` from `clone`, its source at the tag (2.13): every crate that
 * `entry.notices.crate` reaches through normal and build edges, on every platform, in the `cargo metadata` output of
 * the source's workspace, sent through `run` in `root`. Its own crates are included.
 */
export async function nativeNotices(root, run, clone, entry)

/**
 * Writes the notices file of the table entry `entry` into the repository at `root` (`nativeNoticesFile`), from a
 * shallow clone of its tag in a temporary folder, which must name `entry.notices.commit`, and resolves to the file's
 * path relative to `root`. Every `cargo` command goes through `run`. A failed clone waits each of `retryDelays` in
 * turn and is tried again. On a failure the file stays as it was.
 */
export async function writeNativeNotices(root, entry, { run, retryDelays = DOWNLOAD_RETRY_DELAYS })
```

**Measured** (2026-11-05, cargo 1.97.0 and git 2.43.0, every crate in Cargo's cache). The command wrote the four files of `ui/scripts/notices/native/` in 25 s, byte for byte the files that the walk by hand had made. The copies of `ui/scripts/notices/repositories/` are the root license files of the default branch of each of the 19 repositories on 2026-11-05, the texts that walk had read. `napi-rs/json-escape-simd` now leads to `napi-rs/escape-simd`. Both folders hold the same three files.

The crates of the lock files of lightningcss and `tauri-cli` are given out as published, and the license check does not hold them to `deny.toml` (the owner's rule, ADR 0033's amendment of 2026-11-05). Measured: none is under a copyleft license alone. Those of lightningcss all pass `licenseAllowed` with `/` read as `OR`. Of the 859 of `tauri-cli`, five are outside the list: `webpki-roots` 0.26.11 and 1.0.5 and `webpki-root-certs` 1.0.5 (CDLA-Permissive-2.0), `unicode-id-start` 1.3.1 (`(MIT OR Apache-2.0) AND Unicode-DFS-2016`), and `sublime_fuzzy` 0.7.0, whose manifest names only its license file, the Apache License 2.0. Another 77 write `/` for `OR` (`MIT/Apache-2.0`), which `licenseAllowed` does not read. Test 114 measures these licenses again in each change of `NATIVE_PACKAGES` or `TAURI_CLI`.

**Built from the archives alone** (test 97, run here on 2026-11-04 with the archives that `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs` wrote, inside a network namespace that holds only loopback). In a tree of `git archive` at `dc2fdb8` with the two web archives extracted next to it and nothing else, `CARGO_HOME=<sources>/rust-crates-web scripts/build-wasm.sh` built the web core in a new target folder. It compiled 51 registry crates, all of them whole in the archive. `pnpm -C ui install --frozen-lockfile --store-dir <new store> --registry http://127.0.0.1:48733/` against `npm-packages-web` served by `python3 -m http.server` installed from the folder alone, with 148 requests and one 404, for `/pnpm`. `pnpm -C ui build` and `check:dist` passed. In a second tree with the desktop archives, the install from `npm-packages-desktop` asked for the same 147 tarballs and `pnpm -C ui build` passed. With `tauri-cli` 2.12.2 installed beforehand, `CARGO_HOME=<sources>/rust-crates-desktop cargo tauri build --debug --bundles deb,rpm` in `desktop/src-tauri` wrote one `.deb` and one `.rpm`. It compiled 288 registry crates, all of them whole in the archive. Each archive holds crates its build here does not compile: the web one `adler2`, `miniz_oxide` and `rand_core`, which features in the notices' graph (`--all-features`) reach, and the desktop one 74, the crates of Windows and macOS among them. No build from the desktop archives ran on Windows or macOS (open issue 21).

On 2026-11-05 the two new builds ran here the same way, in a network namespace with only loopback, from folders laid out as the archives lay them out (test 114 runs them from the archives the script writes). With `CARGO_HOME` a folder of the `config.toml` of 2.13 and a `vendor/` of the 859 crates of `tauri-cli`'s lock file and `tauri-cli-2.12.2/` with the `.cargo-checksum.json` of The crates of tauri-cli, `cargo install tauri-cli --version 2.12.2 --locked --debug` compiled 622 crates in 2 min 12 s and installed a `cargo-tauri` whose `--version` printed `tauri-cli 2.12.2`. The release profile that the statement's command uses was not timed. With `CARGO_HOME` the folder of the union of both lightningcss lock files, `cargo build --locked -p lightningcss_node` built `liblightningcss_node.so` in `lightningcss-1.32.0/` and `lightningcss-1.33.0/`, extracted from the two source archives of the table above, in 71 s and 65 s (debug, one target folder). Both builds used Rust 1.97.0: the tags pin 1.92.0 and 1.96.0, which this container does not have and could not download inside the namespace.

Test 114 ran here on 2026-11-05 from the archives that the script wrote, joined and extracted, inside a network namespace that holds only loopback. With `CARGO_HOME` the full path of `rust-crates-desktop`, `cargo install tauri-cli --version 2.12.2 --locked` compiled 622 crates in release in 6 min 7 s. It installed `rust-crates-desktop/bin/cargo-tauri`, whose `--version` printed `tauri-cli 2.12.2`. `cargo tauri --version` found it there. With the same `CARGO_HOME`, `cargo check --locked -p partsmith-desktop` checked the desktop crates in 1 min 43 s. It compiled `libdbus-sys` 0.2.7 without `vendor/dbus/`, against the system's libdbus through pkg-config. The full `cargo tauri build` of test 97 was not repeated here for the disk its target folder needs. For Partsmith's own build, the desktop crate archive changed since test 97 only in the folder that `libdbus-sys` now lacks. In `lightningcss-1.32.0/` and `lightningcss-1.33.0/`, extracted from `npm-sources-web/`, with `CARGO_HOME` the full path of `lightningcss-crates`, `cargo build --locked --release -p lightningcss_node` built `liblightningcss_node.so` in 3 min 43 s and 3 min 39 s. It used Rust 1.92.0 and 1.96.0, the toolchains that the tags pin, installed beforehand. `bexpand-1.2.0.crate` extracted into `bexpand-1.2.0/` with its `LICENSE`. The script's runs reported no license problem.

**Size and upload.** Wrangler uploads a part only when Pages lacks its hash (Size and upload, above). The parts change only when a lock file, the script, its pins, the crate set of `ui/scripts/notices.mjs`, a file of `ui/scripts/notices/native/`, cargo, git, tar or gzip changes, and not with the commit otherwise. A deployment after such a change uploads up to 146 MB more. `web-dist` and `sources-web` each hold the same 146 MB, kept 7 and 8 days (The source with every artifact, below). The npm tarballs are the packages as the registry serves them: the native packages of rolldown, oxlint, oxfmt, lightningcss and Tailwind's oxide hold prebuilt binaries of build tools, whose source is in their own repositories. The archives give out every crate and package as published, development packages included. Every npm package passes the license check (Licenses of the npm packages, above). lightningcss's binaries are under MPL-2.0, and so is `bexpand` in Tailwind's oxide: `npm-sources-{scope}` holds their source (Source of the native packages, above). rolldown, oxlint, oxfmt, fsevents and `playwright-core` are under MIT or Apache-2.0 and compile in no code under MPL-2.0 or a copyleft license alone. Their notices are owed, not their source. `npm-sources-{scope}` holds the notices of the crates compiled into the bindings of rolldown, oxlint, oxfmt and Tailwind's oxide, which their tarballs lack (Native packages, checked, above).

**The source with every artifact** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-05 on CI's artifacts, option (b) widened, ADR 0034's amendment of that date). The repository is public. Any signed-in GitHub user can download a run's artifacts (ADR 0034, CI's public artifacts). Every run of `ci.yml` and `release.yml` that uploads an artifact holding OCCT's compiled code first uploads, as an artifact of its own, OCCT's source archive, Partsmith's source archive and the dependency source archives of that artifact's scope. GitHub counts each artifact's days from its own upload. The source artifact is kept 8 days, one more than the artifacts it goes with (Kept a day longer, below):

| Workflow | Artifacts with OCCT's compiled code | Scope | Source artifact | Its files |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ci.yml` | `core-wasm`, `web-dist`, `playwright-report` after a failed `e2e`, and `playwright-report-{browser}` after a failed row of `e2e-engines` (their traces can hold the core) | `web` | `sources-web` | `occt-{OCCT_VERSION}-source.tar.gz`, `partsmith-source.tar.gz`, and `dependencies/` with the parts of `rust-crates-web.tar.gz` and `npm-packages-web.tar` and their `SHA256SUMS`: the source files of a deployment, at their paths in it |
| `release.yml`, tag runs and dry runs | `bundle-Linux`, `bundle-Windows` and `bundle-macOS` | `desktop` | `sources-desktop` | `occt-{OCCT_VERSION}-source.tar.gz`, `partsmith-source.tar.gz`, and the parts of `rust-crates-desktop.tar.gz` and `npm-packages-desktop.tar`: the source assets of a release |

`web-dist` also holds the files of `sources-web`. `web` builds the deployment with them (below). `platforms.yml` uploads no artifact since its web jobs moved into `ci.yml` in wave H (2.14, The move).

- **First in the run** (the owner's rule of 2026-11-05, ADR 0034's amendment of that date, choice 1). `ci.yml` and `release.yml` each have a job `sources` that writes the source artifact and uploads it. Every job that uploads an artifact of the table needs `sources`, directly or through the jobs it needs. In `ci.yml`, `core-wasm` needs `occt-native`, `occt-wasm` and `sources`, and `web`, `e2e` and `e2e-engines` need `core-wasm`. In `release.yml`, `bundle` needs `verify` and `sources`, with `if: ${{ !cancelled() && needs.sources.result == 'success' }}`. None of these jobs, and no job between it and `sources`, has an `if` that runs it after a failed job it needs. A failed `sources` job skips them all. No run holds OCCT's compiled code without its source. `ci.yml`'s `sources` needs nothing and starts at once, beside `occt-native` and `occt-wasm`. `release.yml`'s needs `verify` and has the `if` that `bundle` had: it runs in a tag run whose `verify` passed and in a dry run, where `verify` is skipped. The cost: whatever fails `sources` now skips these jobs too. That is a failure of one of the archive steps: a native npm package or version that `NATIVE_PACKAGES` does not record (any Dependabot bump of rolldown, oxlint, oxfmt, lightningcss or Tailwind's oxide), a license outside the list of 2.10, a crate built for the build machine outside the web crate set, an OCCT tag moved upstream or a damaged cache entry, or an outage of the npm registry, crates.io or GitHub that outlasts the retries. In `ci.yml` it skips `core-wasm`, `web`, `e2e`, `e2e-engines` and `deploy`. In `release.yml` it skips every `bundle` row, and in a tag run `draft` too. Before wave G such a failure failed only `web` in CI, which skipped `deploy`, `draft` in a tag run and `dependency-sources` in a dry run. GitHub reports a job that a failed job it needs skipped as skipped. Branch protection counts a skipped required check as passed. Branch protection requires `Source archives (web)` itself (section 8).
- **The job**, exactly in structure, in `ci.yml`, with the `run` texts of The archive in every deployment (Copied into the build) and In every deployment, above:

  ```yaml
  sources:
    name: Source archives (web)
    runs-on: ubuntu-24.04
    timeout-minutes: 60
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7.0.1
      - id: occt-source
        uses: actions/cache@v6.1.0
        with:
          path: .occt/source
          key: occt-source-${{ hashFiles('scripts/occt-source-archive.sh', 'scripts/occt-version.sh') }}
      - if: steps.occt-source.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'
        run: |
          mkdir -p .occt/source
          scripts/occt-source-archive.sh .occt/source
      - run: rustup show active-toolchain || rustup toolchain install
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7.1.0
        with:
          node-version: 22
      - name: Write the OCCT source archive
        env:
          OCCT_SOURCE_SHA256: bb71bf7473f9a278648f3ff96e28bd50afe49bf8d6e53ab55e883b54d0220155
        run: |
          {the lines of Copied into the build}
      - name: Write the Partsmith source archive
        run: scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh sources
      - name: Write the dependency source archives
        run: |
          {the five lines of In every deployment}
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v7.0.2
        with:
          name: sources-web
          path: sources/
          retention-days: 8
          if-no-files-found: error
          compression-level: 0
  ```

  `release.yml`'s has `name: Source archives (desktop)`, `needs: verify` and `if: ${{ !cancelled() && (needs.verify.result == 'success' || needs.verify.result == 'skipped') }}`. Its step `Write the dependency source archives` runs `node scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs desktop sources`. Its artifact is `sources-desktop`. It has no cache step, and its archive step has no `if` (The archive in every deployment, Made in the `sources` jobs). It has eight steps. Its other steps are the same. The steps write the folder `sources/` in the repository root. The artifact's root is that folder. The cache step and the step after it are those of The archive in every deployment (Made in the `sources` jobs): the cache step restores the entry, on a miss the step after it makes the archive, and the post step saves it when the job passes. The job compiles nothing and has no Rust cache. `cargo vendor` and `cargo metadata` need only the toolchain (2.4).
- **Reused by the build and the release** (the owner's rule of 2026-11-05, choice 2). `web` downloads `sources-web` into `ui/dist` and makes none of the archives itself (2.4). `draft` downloads `sources-desktop` into `release/` (2.5). Each archive is made once per run. The deployment and the release carry the bytes of their run's source artifact. A run whose `web` job failed still holds `sources-web` beside `core-wasm`. `web-dist` keeps the archives: the artifact deployed stays the one `check:dist` checked (3.3).
- **Upload.** `compression-level: 0` stores the files in the artifact's zip as they are. They are gzip files, tar files of gzip files and `SHA256SUMS`. Measured here with Info-ZIP's `zip` 3.0 on the 175,444,768 bytes of a whole `sources/` of the web scope (2026-11-05): level 6, the action's default, took 5.6 s and saved 1.3 percent, and level 0 took 0.6 s. The documentation of `actions/upload-artifact` recommends level 0 for files that do not compress.
- **Kept a day longer** (the owner's rule of 2026-11-05, ADR 0034's amendment of that date, choice 4). Each `sources-{scope}` upload sets `retention-days: 8`, and every other upload of the three files `retention-days: 7` (test 59). GitHub counts an artifact's days from its own upload. The source artifact is uploaded first. In a cold CI run, `core-wasm` uploads about 90 minutes after `sources-web` (`occt-wasm` builds for 88 minutes). In a cold release run, the `bundle-*` artifacts upload up to about 2.5 hours after `sources-desktop`. With 7 days for both, the run would hold OCCT's compiled code without its source for that long at the end of the window. A rerun of failed jobs uploads their artifacts again, with 7 days from the new upload, while the source artifact keeps its first upload. A job that uploads OCCT's compiled code (`core-wasm`, `web`, `e2e`, a row of `e2e-engines` or of `bundle`) is rerun with `gh run rerun <id> --failed` only within 12 hours of the end of the run's `sources` job, which `gh run view <id>` shows. Later, the whole run is rerun with `gh run rerun <id>`, which uploads the source artifact again too. 12 hours leave room inside the source artifact's extra day for a rerun that builds OCCT cold (about 2.5 hours in a release run).
- **Size and time per run.** `sources-web` holds about 175.4 MB: 17,457,358 bytes of OCCT's archive, about 12.3 MB of Partsmith's and 145,628,448 bytes of dependency archives in 7 parts. `sources-desktop` holds about 385.4 MB: the same two archives and 355,668,183 bytes in 15 parts. Each CI run uploads about 187 MB more than before: 175 MB in `sources-web` and the 12 MB of Partsmith's archive in `web-dist`, which already held the OCCT and dependency archives. Each platforms run uploaded about 175 MB more until wave H, when its web jobs moved into `ci.yml` (2.14, The move). Each release run, dry runs included, uploads about 385 MB more. CI run 41926888431 uploaded the 113,924,270 bytes of `web-dist` in 4.2 s, about 27 MB/s. At that rate the two uploads take about 7 s and 15 s. In `ci.yml`, `sources` starts at once and runs beside `occt-native` and `occt-wasm`. `core-wasm` waits for all of them. It takes about 1.5 to 2 minutes: the steps it takes over from `web` (the toolchain step and the dependency script took 23 s on the runner in that run, before the native sources, and the script took 53 s here with them), 2.2 s for Partsmith's archive, the upload and the job's setup. A warm `occt-native` or `occt-wasm` takes about a minute (3.1). `core-wasm` then starts about 0.5 to 1 minute later than before. A warm CI run takes about 0.5 to 1 minute more wall time than the 16 min 58 s of run 41785469932. It takes about 1 minute more runner time. `web` drops its toolchain step and the dependency script and downloads about 175 MB. `sources` adds its own setup. In `release.yml`, the bundles start after `sources`, which takes about 4 to 5 minutes (the desktop scope took 152 s here with a warm Cargo cache, and the runner's is cold), and about 20 s more for the OCCT clone and archive it makes in every run. `draft` drops about as long: the OCCT clone, the toolchain, Node and the dependency script, for a download of about 385 MB. A tag run then takes about as long as before. A dry run takes about 4 to 5 minutes more wall time than before, when its archives job ran beside the bundles. Open issue 21 (g) records the first runs.

### 2.14 Platform jobs (`ci.yml`) and the platforms workflow (`platforms.yml`)

Builds and tests what the Linux jobs of 2.4 do not (ADR 0038): the crates whose code differs by platform and the examples runner on Windows and macOS, the two platforms besides Linux that the desktop bundles ship for (3.6), and the end-to-end tests tagged `@engines` in Firefox and WebKit, the two browser engines besides Chromium that the web app supports (ui.md 1.1), WebKit also being the webview of two of the three desktop platforms (desktop.md 1). Three jobs do it: `occt-platforms`, `rust-platforms` and `e2e-engines`. They are jobs of `ci.yml` and in `deploy.needs`. They gate every merge and deploy like every other CI job (2.4). Until wave H they were jobs of `platforms.yml`, outside the gate. Each moved into `ci.yml` once it had passed in the two latest runs of `platforms.yml` on `main` (promotion and The move, below). `platforms.yml` keeps one measurement: it times the full rebuild of technical design section 14 on the macOS arm64 runner, the one place that gate is judged (the full-rebuild gate, below, ADR 0054), after a copy of the `macos-15` row of `occt-platforms`.
