Code compiled with `/MT` calls the Universal C Runtime without `__declspec(dllimport)`, and with `ucrt.lib` the linker resolves those calls through the import library's stubs. The owner's decision of 2026-11-05 leaves `scripts/build-occt.sh` as wave F made it, and no OCCT cache is built again.
2. `.cargo/config.toml` gives the target `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` the rustflags `["-C", "target-feature=+crt-static", "-C", "link-arg=/NODEFAULTLIB:libucrt.lib", "-C", "link-arg=/DEFAULTLIB:ucrt.lib"]`, and the same six as its `rustdocflags`. With `crt-static`, Rust links `libcmt` (the `#[link(name = "libcmt")]` of the `libc` crate that `std` uses), and `libcmt.lib` names `libvcruntime.lib` and `libucrt.lib` among its default libraries. `/NODEFAULTLIB:libucrt.lib` keeps the linker from reading `libucrt.lib` for any default library list, `libcmt.lib`'s among them, which every `/MT` object of OCCT, the shim and `cxx.cc` reaches through `LIBCMT`. `/DEFAULTLIB:ucrt.lib` adds the import library of Windows' Universal C Runtime in its place. The Visual C++ runtime and `libcpmt` stay static. No build uses the debug runtime: `cc` compiles with `/MT`, never `/MTd`, OCCT's runtime is `MultiThreaded` and Rust links `libcmt`. The debug pair `libucrtd.lib` and `ucrtd.lib` is not named. Every crate of the workspace links this way, with its build script and proc macros, since no Windows build passes `--target`: the app, the CLI, the Python extension and every test executable. A link argument reaches every executable and DLL the target links (a proc macro is a DLL), and no static library. Rustdoc reads the `rustdocflags` and not the rustflags, and the second entry gives the doc tests the same runtime (with Cargo 1.97.0, a flag in a target's `rustflags` does not reach a doc test, and one in its `rustdocflags` does). The table names no other target: Linux, macOS and WebAssembly builds do not change.
3. The shim follows the Rust target. `cc` compiles with `/MT` when the target has `crt-static` and with `/MD` when it has not, and `partsmith-occt-sys/build.rs` sets no runtime of its own (kernel.md 3.12 item 5). The `cxx` crate's own `cxx.cc` follows the target the same way. The two link arguments act at the final link and need nothing in `build.rs`.
4. tauri-build keeps its default `build.windows.staticVCRuntime`, true, which `tauri.conf.json` does not set (desktop.md test 33). For the desktop crate's executables alone, tauri-build 2.7.2 (`src/static_vcruntime.rs`) adds `/NODEFAULTLIB` for `libucrt.lib`, `libucrtd.lib`, `msvcrt.lib`, `msvcrtd.lib`, `vcruntime.lib`, `vcruntimed.lib`, `libvcruntimed.lib` and `libcmtd.lib`, and `/DEFAULTLIB` for `libcmt.lib`, `libvcruntime.lib` and `ucrt.lib`. The two arguments of item 2 are among them, and the linker reads an argument given twice once. Seven of the other nine, the other `/NODEFAULTLIB` arguments, keep out the DLL and debug variants of the C runtime, which no object of the build names. The last two, `/DEFAULTLIB:libcmt.lib` and `/DEFAULTLIB:libvcruntime.lib`, name the static Visual C++ runtime that the build links anyway: every `/MT` object names `LIBCMT`, and so does the `libc` crate under `crt-static` (item 2). The deprecated environment variable `STATIC_VCRUNTIME` overrides that setting, and no workflow sets it. With it false, the app would still take the Universal C Runtime from Windows through item 2. Before the owner's decision of 2026-11-05, the setting alone gave the desktop crate's executables the Universal C Runtime of Windows. The examples runner (the CLI) and the test executables of `partsmith-occt-sys` and `partsmith-engine` linked the UCRT of the runner's Windows SDK statically, and a math result of the app on Windows could differ from the one the platforms run checked (the C runtime's `cos` decided a Windows result once, open issue 1 (d), kernel.md open issue 14). Since that decision (section 8, option (c)), every program calls the `ucrtbase.dll` of the Windows it runs on, as before wave F. `rust-platforms` tests on the UCRT of `windows-2025`, and the app uses the user's, which on an older Windows can still give another result.

The runtimes must match at link time. Next to a shim built with `/MT`, an OCCT install built with `/MD` (any install the script built before this change) stops the link with LNK2038, `mismatch detected for 'RuntimeLibrary'`. Such a local install is rebuilt from an empty build folder (open issue 3): delete `$OCCT_WORK_DIR/build-native` (by default `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/occt-7.9.3/build-native`), then run `scripts/build-occt.sh native <prefix>`. The `CMakeCache.txt` of the old folder keeps the `/MD` of its first configure in `CMAKE_C_FLAGS_RELEASE` and `CMAKE_CXX_FLAGS_RELEASE`, and a build there compiles with both `/MD` and `/MT`. CI's runners start empty. A `RUSTFLAGS` or `CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS` variable replaces the rustflags of `.cargo/config.toml`, and a `RUSTDOCFLAGS` or `CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTDOCFLAGS` variable replaces its rustdocflags. A Windows build with one adds the six flags of item 2 to it; no workflow sets one. Without the two link arguments, the CLI and the test executables would link the Universal C Runtime statically again, and the CLI's import check would fail (step 4). Without the rustdocflags, a doc test in a crate that links OCCT (`partsmith-occt-sys` and every crate that depends on it) would link the DLL runtime next to OCCT built with `/MT` and fail with LNK2038. With them, a code block in the documentation of these crates needs no rule of its own. On Windows, `rust-platforms`' `cargo test` builds the doc tests of `partsmith-occt-sys`, `partsmith-engine` and `partsmith-desktop` (none of them has one on 2026-11-04).

**The check of the imports.** `rust-platforms` (2.14) has the step `Check the imports of the app` and, right after it, the step `Check the imports of the CLI`. The Windows row of `bundle` (step 5) has the step `Check the imports of the app`. The three steps run one script, `scripts/check-windows-imports.sh <executable>`, each with its program as the one argument: `target/debug/partsmith-desktop.exe`, which the `crates` step builds; `target/debug/partsmith.exe`, which Cargo builds for the examples runner (the `CARGO_BIN_EXE_partsmith` of `crates/partsmith-cli/tests/examples.rs`); and `target/release/partsmith-desktop.exe`, which the installer holds. No step runs `dumpbin` itself. tauri-build gives the app the two link arguments of item 2 by itself (item 4). The CLI's link gets them from `.cargo/config.toml` alone. The CLI's check is the one that shows the configuration at work (the owner's rule of 2026-11-05, ADR 0038's amendment of that day). The test executables take the same rustflags as the CLI and are not checked by path: Cargo names them with a hash, such as `target/debug/deps/integration-<hash>.exe`, which changes with the build. `cargo test --no-run --message-format=json` would name each one the run built, at the cost of a step that reads Cargo's JSON before the check (ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-05, choice 1). The script runs with `set -euo pipefail` and needs `bash` and the GNU tools of Git Bash. With other than one argument, it prints the usage line of section 4 on standard error and exits 2. Otherwise it:

1. runs `dumpbin -nologo -dependents <executable>` in the MSVC environment that `ilammy/msvc-dev-cmd` set up earlier in the job (options with `-`, because Git Bash rewrites an argument that starts with `/` into a path), drops the carriage returns of its output and prints the listing. dumpbin's exit status is not used; the listing decides. When `dumpbin` is not on `PATH`, the script fails with `Imports not checked`;
2. fails with the message `Imports not checked` of section 4 when the listing has no line that, without its leading spaces, is `Image has the following dependencies:` (the executable is missing, dumpbin could not read it, or dumpbin is not on `PATH`);
3. fails with the message `Visual C++ runtime imported` of section 4 when a line of the listing, without its leading spaces and ignoring case, matches `^(msvcp|msvcr|vcruntime|concrt|vcomp|vccorlib|vcamp|mfc|mfcm)[0-9][^ ]*\.dll$`, among the dependencies or the delay load dependencies; the message names each such DLL in the listing's order. These are the DLLs of the Visual C++ Redistributable, `MSVCR120.dll` of older ones included. The digit after the prefix keeps `msvcp_win.dll` and `msvcrt.dll`, both part of Windows, from matching;
4. fails with the message `Universal C Runtime not imported` of section 4 when no line of the listing, without its leading spaces and ignoring case, matches `^api-ms-win-crt-[a-z0-9-]+\.dll$`. A program linked with `ucrt.lib` imports at least `api-ms-win-crt-runtime-l1-1-0.dll`, which holds the functions that start and end the C runtime. The debug app imported it and nine more `api-ms-win-crt` DLLs in platforms run 14 (41926898084, job 126946230717). A program that links the Universal C Runtime statically imports none;
5. otherwise exits 0 (tests 100, 101, 123 and 124).

Each message of steps 2 to 4 is the last line of standard output, and the script then exits 1.

The import table shows neither the DLLs that the app or its webview load while they run nor how the static runtime behaves. The Windows Sandbox check of desktop.md 6 covers both by hand.

**Caches after the static runtime** (2026-11-04). The key formulas of 3.2 stay. Every OCCT key hashes `scripts/build-occt.sh`. That change made every OCCT cache new, on each OS and for WebAssembly, although only the Windows build differs. The first CI run after it builds the Linux and WebAssembly OCCT cold (56 and 88 minutes on 2026-10-13, inside the 150 minutes of `occt-native` and `occt-wasm`). The caches that a pull request's run saves belong to that pull request. When the change reaches `main` through one, the first run on `main` builds the Linux and WebAssembly OCCT cold a second time. The first platforms run builds the Windows one (about 48 minutes, open issue 16) and the macOS one (about 21) and saves them on `main`, where the next release dry run restores them. No Windows install built with `/MD` is ever restored next to the new rustflags. `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2.9.2` hashes `.cargo/config.toml` into its key. Every Rust cache key changes once, in each job that uses the action (in `ci.yml`, `nightly.yml`, `platforms.yml` and `release.yml`) and on every OS. Each such job restores the previous cache by its prefix and saves a new one once. Only the Windows builds compile again: every crate once more, since the rustflags are part of each crate's fingerprint (test 102). The old OCCT and Rust entries stay until GitHub drops them after 7 days unused, and open issue 16 records the size of all the caches after the first runs.

**Caches after one Universal C Runtime** (2026-11-05). `scripts/build-occt.sh` does not change. No OCCT key changes, and no OCCT is built again on any OS or for WebAssembly (test 127). `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2.9.2` hashes `.cargo/config.toml` into its key, and every Rust cache key changes once more, on every OS, in each job that uses the action. Each such job restores the previous cache by its prefix and saves a new one once. Only the Windows builds compile again, every crate once, since the rustflags are part of each crate's fingerprint. The Linux, macOS and WebAssembly builds reuse what they restore.

**Where signing will plug in** (later, 1.2): the `bundle` job's environment gets the Apple variables of desktop.md 1.2 on macOS and the Windows certificate variables on Windows; the Tauri bundler signs and notarizes when they are present. The updater adds `bundle.createUpdaterArtifacts` and the signing key variables, and `draft` uploads `latest.json`. Each needs the secrets and a human decision first.

### 3.7 Examples in CI

The `rust` job runs the Rust runner as part of `cargo test --workspace` (with the debug CLI binary), which also exports every example that builds to STL, 3MF, OBJ and STEP and imports each STEP file back (2.8, runner test 5) and checks what the corpus covers (runner tests 3 and 6 to 9); the `python` job runs the twin checks and the round trip of the documents the UI writes as part of `pytest python/tests`, and the `e2e` job runs E-160, which makes those documents again in the UI (2.8); the `core-wasm` job opens every example in the web core it built and compares each response with a native session's (engine.md test 154), so a result that only WebAssembly gets wrong fails with its file, request and JSON Pointer. A failing example names its file and the first differing JSON Pointer, so the reviewer sees exactly what changed.

| `_headers` missing | `check:dist` | `{directory}/_headers is missing. Keep ui/public/_headers, which sets the response headers of docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.7.` |
| `_headers` different | `check:dist` | `{directory}/_headers does not match the response headers of docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.7. Make ui/public/_headers equal EXPECTED_HEADERS in scripts/check-dist.mjs, then run pnpm build again.` |
| Notices missing from the build (2.6) | `check:dist`, exit 1 | `{directory}/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt is missing. Keep ui/public/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt, which pnpm notices writes (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.9).` |
| Notices of the build not the web app's (2.6) | `check:dist`, exit 1 | `{directory}/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt does not start with "Third-party notices for the Partsmith web app" (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.9).` |

Run scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh in a clone of the Partsmith repository, whose checked-out commit it archives (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` (`{root}` the real path of the folder above the script's own) |
| Wrong arguments | `scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh`, standard error, exit 2 | `usage: scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh <directory>` |
| Wrong arguments | `scripts/check-source-archive.sh`, standard error, exit 2 | `usage: scripts/check-source-archive.sh <archive> <commit>` |
| Wrong arguments | `scripts/check-windows-imports.sh`, standard error, exit 2 | `usage: scripts/check-windows-imports.sh <executable>` |
| Wrong arguments (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 2 | `usage: node scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs web\|desktop <directory>` |
| The output folder does not exist (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `{directory} does not exist. Make it before writing the dependency source archives into it (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` |
| `tar` is not GNU tar (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `tar --version does not name GNU tar, which the dependency source archives need for the same bytes on every run. Put GNU tar first on PATH (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` |
| A command the script runs fails (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs` and `ui/scripts/native-notices.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `{command} {arguments} failed with exit status {status}: {standard error}` (the message of the notices generator, 2.9) |
| A crate built for the build machine of the web core is not in the web crate set (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `Rust crate {name} {version} is built for the machine that builds the web core, and the web crate set of docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.9 input 1 does not hold it, so rust-crates-web.tar.gz would lack it. Nothing was written.` |
| A lock file entry has no integrity (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `npm package {key} has no integrity in ui/pnpm-lock.yaml, so its tarball cannot be checked (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` (`{key}` the entry's key, such as `clsx@2.1.1`) |
| A tarball cannot be downloaded (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `npm package {name} {version}: {url} answered {status}.` (`{status}` the HTTP status of the last attempt, or the message of the error when no answer came; a network error and an answer of 408, 429 or 500 and above are tried twice more first) |
| A tarball's integrity differs from the lock file's (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `npm package {name} {version}: {url} sent a tarball whose integrity is {actual}, not {expected}, which ui/pnpm-lock.yaml records. Nothing was written.` (`{actual}` in the form of `{expected}`, with the same algorithm) |
| A package of the scope that holds prebuilt native code belongs to no entry of `NATIVE_PACKAGES` (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1: for a package with an `os`, `cpu` or `libc` list before any `cargo` or `git` command or request, and for a tarball that holds a prebuilt file once the tarballs are downloaded and checked | `npm package {name} {version} holds prebuilt native code, and NATIVE_PACKAGES of scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs records no check of its source at that version. Check its source for code under MPL-2.0 or a copyleft license that it compiles in, and record the result there and in docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13.` |
| A package of the Cargo lock file of `tauri-cli` or of a native package's source comes from a source other than crates.io (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1, before `cargo vendor` | `Rust crate {name} {version} in the Cargo.lock of {subject} comes from {source}, not from crates.io. The config.toml of the dependency source archives replaces only crates.io, and an offline build would not find it. Nothing was written (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` (`{subject}` the entry's or `TAURI_CLI`'s name and version, such as `tauri-cli 2.12.2` or `lightningcss 1.33.0`; `{source}` the package's `source` as the lock file writes it) |
| The notices file of a native package is missing (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `ui/scripts/notices/native/{stem}-{version}.txt is missing. Write it from the source of {name} {version} with node scripts/native-notices.mjs in ui/ (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` |
| Wrong arguments of the notices walk (2.13) | `ui/scripts/native-notices.mjs`, standard error, exit 2 | `usage: node scripts/native-notices.mjs` |
| A crate of a native package's notices comes from a source other than crates.io and the source's workspace (2.13) | `ui/scripts/native-notices.mjs`, standard error, exit 1, before any text is read | `Rust crate {name} {version} of the source of {entry name} {entry version} comes from {source}, neither crates.io nor the source's own workspace. The notices name a crate on crates.io or in the tag's tree (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` |
| A crate from crates.io without texts whose repository has no committed copies (2.13) | `ui/scripts/native-notices.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `Rust crate {name} {version} ships no license text, and ui/scripts/notices/repositories/{folder}/ is missing. Commit a copy of the license files at the root of {repository} there (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` (`{folder}` as `repositoryFolder` gives it, `{repository}` the URL its manifest names) |
| A crate from crates.io without texts names no repository with an owner and a name (2.13) | `ui/scripts/native-notices.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `Rust crate {name} {version} ships no license text, takes no standard text and names no repository with an owner and a name (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` |
| A crate of the source's workspace without texts, and no license file at the root of the tag (2.13) | `ui/scripts/native-notices.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `Rust crate {name} {version} of {source} ships no license text, and the root of the tag {tag} holds none (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` |
| The temporary folder is inside the repository (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs` for `desktop`, standard error, exit 1, before any `cargo` command or request | `The temporary folder {folder} is inside {root}, where Cargo would take tauri-cli for a member of Partsmith's workspace. Set TMPDIR to a folder outside the repository (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` (`{folder}` and `{root}` the real paths of Node's `os.tmpdir()` and of the repository root) |
| The tag of a native package's source names another commit than `NATIVE_PACKAGES` pins (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs` for `commit`, and `ui/scripts/native-notices.mjs` for `notices.commit`; standard error, exit 1 | `The tag {tag} of {source} names the commit {commit}, not {pinned}, which NATIVE_PACKAGES of scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs pins for {name} {version}. Upstream may have moved the tag; nothing was written (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.13).` |
| A crate cannot be downloaded (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `Rust crate {name} {version}: {url} answered {status}.` (`{status}` as for a tarball, after the same retries) |
| A crate's SHA-256 differs from the one the script records (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1 | `Rust crate {name} {version}: {url} sent a file whose SHA-256 is {actual}, not {expected}, which scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs records. Nothing was written.` |
| An npm package of a dependency archive is under a license the list does not allow (2.13) | `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs`, standard error, exit 1, one line per package in the lock file's order | The message of 2.10 for each such package, with `{license}` the `license` string of the `package.json` in its tarball, or `Unknown` when it has none |
| Example file in a wrong place | examples runner | `examples/{path}: not an allowed example path (docs/spec/delivery.md section 2.8).` |
| Orphan companion file | examples runner | `examples/{path}: has no .ptsm next to it.` |
| Example sets the rollback marker | examples runner | `examples/{path}: examples must not set the rollback marker.` |
| Example not canonical | examples runner | `examples/{path}: not canonical. Run: partsmith fmt examples/{path}` |
| Feature type without an example | examples runner | `Feature type {type} has no example. Add one under examples/{type}/.` |
| Sketch record form without an example (runner test 6) | examples runner | `Sketch {kind} {type} ({keys}) has no example outside examples/bench/. Add one under examples/sketch/.` (`{kind}` is `constraint` or `entity`; `{keys}` the form's `required` keys other than `id` and `type`, in the schema's order, joined by `, `) |
| Option value or reference kind without an example (runner test 7) | examples runner | `Feature type {type}: {field} {value} has no example outside examples/bench/. Add one under examples/{type}/.` (`{field}` is the manifest hint's path for an option, the path followed by `.kind` for a plane or axis reference kind) |
| Check without a finding on a technology (runner test 8) | examples runner | `No example outside examples/bench/ on a printer of technology {technology} has a finding of {check}. Add one.` (`{technology}` and `{check}` as the protocol schema writes them, `resin`, `small_features`) |
| Asset that no example imports (runner test 9) | examples runner | `examples/{folder}/assets/{name}: no example in examples/{folder}/ imports it. Import it there or delete it.` |
| Expected file missing | examples runner | `examples/{path}.expected.json is missing. Run: PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 cargo test -p partsmith-cli --test integration examples::` |
| Mismatch | examples runner | `examples/{path}.ptsm: {pointer} is {actual}, expected {expected}` (for a missing or extra key: `{pointer} is missing` or `{pointer} is unexpected`) |
| Rebuild failed to run | examples runner | `examples/{path}.ptsm: partsmith rebuild exited with {status}: {reason}` (`{status}` is neither 0 nor 1; `{reason}` is the standard error, or, when that is empty, the `error` member of the JSON on standard output as `error[{code}]: {message}`, since with `--json` a file that cannot be read exits with 3 and reports only there (cli.md 2.2), and empty when there is no `error` member either, whatever else the JSON holds; a run that cannot start or prints no JSON reads `could not start: {error}` or `printed invalid JSON: {error}` after `partsmith rebuild`) |
| Export failed (runner test 5) | examples runner | `examples/{path}.ptsm: partsmith export to {format} exited with {status}: {reason}` (`{format}` is `stl`, `3mf`, `obj` or `step`; `{status}` is not 0; `{reason}`, and the words of a run that cannot start or prints no JSON, are as for a rebuild, with `partsmith export to {format}` in place of `partsmith rebuild`) |
| Export report differs (runner test 5) | examples runner | `examples/{path}.ptsm: partsmith export to {format}: {pointer} is {actual}, expected {expected}` (`{pointer}` is `/files`, a pointer under it, or another member of the report such as `/error`; for a missing or extra key or file: `{pointer} is missing` or `{pointer} is unexpected`) |
| Exported file missing (runner test 5) | examples runner | `examples/{path}.ptsm: partsmith export to {format} wrote no file: {error}` (`{error}` the system's reason the file at the output path cannot be read) |
| Exported STEP file does not import (runner test 5, 2.8 STEP round trip) | examples runner | `examples/{path}.ptsm: partsmith export to step wrote a file that does not import: {message}` (`{message}` the message of the engine's error from `new_document`, `import_step` or `measure`, such as `Import failed. The file is not a valid STEP file.`; an import whose feature fails reads `Body "{name}" has no geometry yet.`, the error of `measure`) |
| Imported STEP volume differs (runner test 5, 2.8 STEP round trip) | examples runner | `examples/{path}.ptsm: partsmith export to step: the imported volume is {actual}, expected {expected}` (`{actual}` the volume `measure` gives for the imported body, `{expected}` the sum of the volumes of the expected bodies the export takes, each written as a mismatch writes a JSON number, such as `6000.01` and `6000.0`) |
| Twin missing | examples runner | `examples/{path}.ptsm: qualifies for a Python twin but {stem}.py is missing. Run: PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 pytest python/tests/test_examples.py` |
| Twin not allowed | examples runner | `examples/{path}.py: the example has an import feature or a failing feature, so it cannot have a Python twin.` |
| Twin builds a different model | `test_examples.py` | `examples/{path}.py does not rebuild examples/{path}.ptsm byte for byte.` with a unified diff |
| Twin writes a generic call the generated script does not (2.8, check 4) | `test_examples.py` | `examples/{path}.py writes more generic feature(...) calls than its generated script ({written} against {generated}). Run: PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 pytest python/tests/test_examples.py` |
| A document the UI writes is missing (ui.md E-160, 2.8) | `round-trip.spec.ts`, `test_gui_documents.py` | `ui/e2e/fixtures/gui/{file} is missing. Run: PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 pnpm -C ui e2e round-trip.spec.ts` |
| A document the UI writes differs from what the UI writes now (ui.md E-160) | `round-trip.spec.ts` | `ui/e2e/fixtures/gui/{file} is not what the UI writes now: {where} is {actual} in the download and {expected} in the committed file. Run: PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 pnpm -C ui e2e round-trip.spec.ts` (the first difference in document order; `{where}` is its JSON Pointer, or `the document` at the root; `{actual}` and `{expected}` are, for two objects whose keys differ, `the keys` followed by each key list as JSON, for two arrays of different lengths `{n} items`, and otherwise each value as JSON) |
| A feature of a document the UI writes is not ok (2.8, check 1) | `test_gui_documents.py` | `ui/e2e/fixtures/gui/{file}: {feature id} has status {status}.`, one line per feature whose status is not `ok`, in timeline order |
| A document the UI writes generates a generic call (2.8, check 3) | `test_gui_documents.py` | `ui/e2e/fixtures/gui/{file}: the generated script has {n} generic feature(...) calls.` |
| Bless in CI | both examples runners, engine test 160 and ui.md E-160 | `PARTSMITH_BLESS is set in CI. Bless expected results locally and commit them.` |

## 5. Budgets

On GitHub's standard runners, with warm caches:

| Measure | Budget |
| --- | --- |
| CI wall time from push to `deploy` finished | 25 minutes (CI run 30, 42222611157, the first CI run on `main` after the move of 2.14, took 15 min 7 s; each of its three browser installs missed its apt cache, and apt's download in each took under 10 s; its time replaces the estimate of about 17 minutes on a fast mirror, test 129, open issue 16; CI run 28, 42156836018, before the move, took 24 min 9 s, 7 min 42 s of it in `e2e`'s Chromium install on a slow apt mirror; an `e2e-engines` row on such a mirror fails at its install timeout when its apt cache misses, and that run does not count (2.14, The move, choice 4); the CI run on wave H2's branch, 42218307369, took 32 min 10 s of wall time (12:31:21 to 13:03:31), 26 min 26 s of it in `rust`'s Tauri install, and does not count (below); its last job would have ended 14 min 14 s after the push without that install, open issue 16) |
| The Rust examples runner over the corpus (debug binary, inside `cargo test`) | 3 minutes |
| `pnpm check:dist` | 2 s |
| `pnpm check:licenses` | 10 s |
| `pnpm check:notices`, crate sources already downloaded | 60 s |
| `deploy` job | 3 minutes |
| `ci.yml`'s `sources` job, from its first step to the end of its upload (2.13) | 3 minutes (measured: on the runner, in CI run 41926888431 of 2026-11-04, the toolchain step and the dependency script took 23 s in `web`, before the native sources; here, the script took 24.5 s for the web scope with an empty Cargo cache on 2026-11-04 and 53 s with the native sources and a warm Cargo cache on 2026-11-05, and Partsmith's archive took 2.2 s; the upload of about 175 MB and the job on the runner are open issue 21 (g)) |
| `release.yml`'s `sources` job, from its first step to the end of its upload (2.13) | 6 minutes (not measured on the runner; here the script took 34 s for the desktop scope on 2026-11-04 and 152 s with the contents of 2026-11-05, with a warm Cargo cache, its temporary folder held about 2.8 GB at its peak, and Partsmith's archive took 2.2 s; the upload of about 385 MB and the job on the runner are open issue 21 (g)) |
| Nightly `properties` job with its Rust cache warm, on a night that runs the tests (2.11) | 5 minutes |
| A platforms run with its OCCT and Rust caches warm, wall time from dispatch to its last job finished (2.14) | 60 minutes (the second run, 40478176115, warm except part of the Windows Rust cache, took 7 min 26 s; the third, 40509561577, warm, 1 h 0 min 22 s, 53 min 17 s of it in WebKit's browser install on a slow mirror; the first run, cold, 1 h 49 min, open issue 16; since the move of 2.14, a run is the copy of the macOS OCCT row and `full-rebuild`, whose job took 2 min 45 s in run 15) |
| Each browser install, `pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps`, in `e2e` and in each row of `e2e-engines` (2.4, 2.14) | 10 minutes, its step's `timeout-minutes` (14 of the first 16 installs of `e2e-engines` took 15 s to 2 min 47 s; the third platforms run's took 21 min 17 s for Firefox and 53 min 17 s for WebKit, almost all of it apt downloading 48.8 and 117 MB of packages at about 39 and 37 kB/s; `e2e`'s took 7 min 42 s in CI run 28, at 48.6 kB/s, open issue 16; since wave H2, on a hit of the apt cache of 3.2 apt downloads only what the entry lacks, after `apt-get update` reads the package lists from the mirror) |
| A release from tag to draft, OCCT caches warm | 60 minutes (cold: 120 minutes) |

A cold OCCT build is excluded from the CI budget and from the platforms budget; it happens only when the OCCT scripts change or a cache was dropped. A CI run whose browser install ended at its timeout is excluded from the CI budget too: it fails, and open issue 16 records its rerun (2.14, The move, choice 4). A CI run whose Tauri install took more than 10 minutes is excluded from the CI budget too. Open issue 16 records it with the install's time and rate (3.2).

## 6. Acceptance tests

Each job's checks are its acceptance tests; they pass on every pull request and push to `main`. Script and runner tests are listed with them.

1. `occt-native`, `occt-wasm`: the install exists at `.occt/native` or `.occt/wasm` (cache hit or built) and contains `lib/libTKernel.a`. After `sources`, the cache of 2.13 with the key `occt-source-...` holds `.occt/source/occt-{OCCT_VERSION}-source.tar.gz`, made by this run or an earlier one.
2. `kernel-wasm`: `cargo test -p partsmith-occt-sys --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten`, `cargo test -p partsmith-sketch --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten` and `cargo test -p partsmith-engine --lib --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten dispatch::` pass.
3. `rust`: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` pass, the latter including `partsmith-desktop` (desktop.md section 6) and the examples runner (2.8).
4. `deny`: `cargo deny check` passes with `deny.toml`.
5. `ui`: `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test` and `pnpm test:scripts` pass, and `pnpm check:licenses` exits 0 (2.10).
6. `schema`: after regeneration, `git status --porcelain -- docs/format ui/src/generated` prints nothing, and `pnpm -C ui check:notices` exits 0 (2.9).
7. `core-wasm`: `scripts/build-wasm.sh` exits 0 and writes `ui/public/core/partsmith_core.mjs` and `partsmith_core.wasm`; `cargo test -p partsmith-wasm --test integration web_module::` passes without skipping (`PARTSMITH_REQUIRE_WEB_MODULE=1` turns a skip into a failure); `cargo test -p partsmith-wasm --test integration web_parity:: -- --ignored` runs engine.md test 154 and passes; the artifact `core-wasm` holds `core/partsmith_core.mjs`, `core/partsmith_core.wasm` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` at its root.
8. `web`: `pnpm build` and `pnpm check:dist` exit 0; `ui/dist/_headers` exists and `ui/dist/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` starts with the web heading (both checked by `check:dist`), `ui/dist/occt-{OCCT_VERSION}-source.tar.gz` and `ui/dist/partsmith-source.tar.gz` exist before `check:dist` runs, which holds each to the 25 MiB limit (2.13), `ui/dist/dependencies/` holds `SHA256SUMS` and the parts it lists, `rust-crates-web.tar.gz.part-00` and `npm-packages-web.tar.part-00` among them, before `check:dist` runs (all three from the artifact `sources-web`, 2.13), and `ui/dist/core/partsmith_core.wasm` and `ui/dist/core/partsmith_core.mjs` exist (checked by the job after `check:dist`).
9. `e2e`: `pnpm e2e` passes against the real core (ui.md 6.5).
10. `python`: `maturin develop --release` succeeds, `pytest python/tests` passes, including `test_examples.py` and `test_gui_documents.py`, `pytest python/tests -m budgets -s` passes, a row over its budget warning without failing, and prints one line per row of python-api.md section 5, `{name}: {value} ms (budget {budget} ms)` (python-api.md test 114 checks that the five tests are collected only with the mark), and `pytest python/tests -m packaging` passes: python-api.md test 76 builds one wheel `partsmith-0.1.0-cp312-abi3-<platform>.whl` and installs and imports it with `python3.12` and with `python3.13`, both found on `PATH` (the test fails, rather than skips, when either is missing).
11. `deploy`: with secrets, the Wrangler step succeeds and the job summary contains a `pages.dev` URL; on a fork pull request, the job succeeds and its log contains the "Deploy skipped" notice.
12. `desktop`: `cargo tauri build --bundles deb` exits 0, exactly one `.deb` exists under `target/release/bundle/deb/`, and its file list holds `usr/bin/partsmith-desktop`, exactly one `.desktop` file under `usr/share/applications/` and `usr/lib/Partsmith/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt`, whose bytes equal the repository's `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` (desktop.md test 49). The file list is `dpkg-deb --fsys-tarfile` piped to `tar -t` with a leading `./` removed from each entry: Tauri's bundler writes entries without the `./` that `dpkg-deb --build` writes, so the check accepts both forms. The notices are extracted by their member name as the list writes it, from the repository root, with `deb` the path of that `.deb`:

    ```sh
    member="$(dpkg-deb --fsys-tarfile "$deb" | tar -t | grep -xE '(\./)?usr/lib/Partsmith/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES\.txt')"
    dpkg-deb --fsys-tarfile "$deb" | tar -xO "$member" | cmp - THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt
    ```

13. `release.yml` dry run (`workflow_dispatch`): the three `bundle` jobs upload `bundle-Linux` (one `.deb` and one `.rpm`), `bundle-Windows` (one `-setup.exe`) and `bundle-macOS` (one `.dmg`); `sources` passes and uploads `sources-desktop` before them, with `occt-{OCCT_VERSION}-source.tar.gz`, `partsmith-source.tar.gz` and the parts of `rust-crates-desktop.tar.gz` and `npm-packages-desktop.tar` (2.13); `verify` and `draft` are skipped. The reviewer of the dry run records, for each bundle, that it holds `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` at the place of desktop.md 2.8, equal to the repository's file: in the `.rpm` (`rpm -qlp` lists `/usr/lib/Partsmith/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt`), in the mounted `.dmg` (`Partsmith.app/Contents/Resources/`) and in the folder the NSIS installer installs to (next to `partsmith-desktop.exe`); and that neither the installer nor the disk image asks the user to accept a license (desktop.md 2.8). On a tag, `draft` creates a draft release holding those files, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` (equal to the tagged commit's file), `partsmith-source.tar.gz` and `occt-{OCCT_VERSION}-source.tar.gz` (2.13), the parts of `rust-crates-desktop.tar.gz` and `npm-packages-desktop.tar` (2.13) and `SHA256SUMS`, whose lines verify with `sha256sum -c` and include the notices, both source archives and every part, and `gzip -dc partsmith-source.tar.gz | git get-tar-commit-id` prints the tagged commit. The person who checks the draft before it is published (the release checklist of 3.6) also joins the parts of each dependency archive in order and extracts it with `tar -x`, which exits 0, and records the result in open issue 21 (c) for the first release.

**Distribution check (`ui/scripts/check-dist.test.mjs`, `node:test`, temporary directories)**

Run by `pnpm test:scripts` in `ui/`. `node --test` takes files or glob patterns, not a folder, so the script passes the quoted glob `"scripts/**/*.test.mjs"`. Every directory that a test of `check-dist.test.mjs` (tests 14 to 19, 26 to 33 and 34) expects to be ready, or to have exactly the problems it lists, also holds a regular file `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` whose first line is `WEB_NOTICES_HEADING` (2.6), unless the test says otherwise.

14. `PAGES_FILE_LIMIT` is 26214400, and `EXPECTED_HEADERS` is the text that 2.6 gives it (written out in the test, which also writes it as the exact `_headers` of the other tests) and equals the bytes of `ui/public/_headers`.
15. `oversizedFiles` of a directory with a 10-byte file and a sparse file of exactly 26,214,400 bytes returns `[]`.
16. A sparse file of 26,214,401 bytes at `core/big.wasm` makes `oversizedFiles` return `[{ path: 'core/big.wasm', size: 26214401 }]`; two oversized files in nested folders come back sorted by path.
17. A symbolic link to an oversized file outside the directory is not reported.
18. `checkDist` returns `[]` for a directory with a small file and the exact `_headers`; the missing-headers message when `_headers` is absent; the mismatch message when it lacks its trailing newline.
19. The command on a ready directory (its `index.html` names no script and it has no worker script) exits 0 and prints the ready line with the file count and the largest file, then `Initial JavaScript: 0 bytes gzipped, 0.0 percent of the limit of 250000 bytes.`; on a directory with an oversized file exits 1 and prints the oversized message naming `{directory}/core/big.wasm`; on a missing directory exits 1 with the missing message; with two arguments exits 2 with the usage line.

**Examples comparison (`crates/partsmith-cli/tests/examples.rs`, on literal JSON)**

20. `{"volume": 24000.0}` matches `{"volume": 24000.02}` (within 1e-6 relative) and does not match `{"volume": 24000.03}`, reported as `/volume is 24000.03, expected 24000.0`.
21. `{"x": 0.0}` matches `{"x": 0.0000009}` (absolute floor) and not `{"x": 0.000002}`.
22. `{"faces": 6}` does not match `{"faces": 7}`; integers are never compared with a tolerance.
23. A missing key and an extra key are reported as `is missing` and `is unexpected` at their pointers; arrays of different lengths do not match.
24. The projection of a report removes `path` and every finding's `triangles` and keeps every other member in order.

**Workflow files (`ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`, `node:test`, run by `pnpm test:scripts`)**

The tests read `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `release.yml`, `nightly.yml` and `platforms.yml` as text, by the two-space indentation those files use: a job is a line `  <id>:` under `jobs:`, with its keys four spaces in and its steps as `      - ` items. A job or step the test cannot find fails it, naming the file.

49. Timeouts (2.4, 2.5, 2.11, 2.14): the jobs of `ci.yml` are `occt-native`, `occt-wasm`, `kernel-wasm`, `rust`, `deny`, `ui`, `schema`, `sources`, `core-wasm`, `web`, `e2e`, `python`, `desktop`, `occt-platforms`, `rust-platforms`, `e2e-engines` and `deploy`, in that order, with `timeout-minutes` 150 for `occt-native`, `occt-wasm` and `occt-platforms` and 60 for every other; the jobs of `release.yml` are `verify`, `sources`, `bundle` and `draft`, with 60, 60, 150 and 60; the one job of `nightly.yml`, `properties`, has 20; and the jobs of `platforms.yml` (2.14) are `occt-platforms` and `full-rebuild`, with 150 and 60.
50. The `schema` job (2.4): its step with `id: install` runs `pnpm -C ui install --frozen-lockfile` and comes before the step that runs `cargo run -p partsmith-cli -- schema --write`, and its last step runs `pnpm -C ui check:notices` with `if: ${{ !cancelled() && steps.install.outcome == 'success' }}`.
51. The nightly skip check (2.11): the `run` script of the step with `id: skip` in `properties`, run with `bash -e` and a stub `gh` first on `PATH` that records its arguments and prints one literal line or fails, with `GITHUB_SHA` a 40-digit commit and `GITHUB_OUTPUT` a file of the test. In every case `gh` receives exactly the arguments of 2.11, once, and the script exits 0. When `gh` prints `GITHUB_SHA`, the file holds `skip=true` and standard output is the skip notice of section 4 for that commit; when it prints another commit, or an empty line (no finished run), the file holds `skip=false` and standard output is empty; when `gh` exits 1, the file holds `skip=false` and standard output is the skip-check warning of section 4 for that commit.

**Source of each build (2.13)**

Tests 52 to 54, 57 and 59 are in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs` and read the workflow files as tests 49 to 51 do; tests 55 and 58 are in `ui/scripts/occt-source-archive.test.mjs`. All seven run in `pnpm test:scripts`, with no network, and use `bash`, `git` and `tar` from `PATH`. Test 56 is run by hand. Tests 52, 55 and 58 run every git command they issue, and the scripts under test, with `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` set to an empty file of the test, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` set to an empty folder of the test (so no global attributes file applies), `GIT_AUTHOR_NAME`, `GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL`, `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME`, `GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL` and fixed `GIT_AUTHOR_DATE` and `GIT_COMMITTER_DATE`, and create repositories with `git init --initial-branch=main`, so the machine's Git configuration (an identity, commit signing, a default branch) neither stops nor changes them. Tests 52, 53 and 57 take the stamp's file name from one constant of `workflows.test.mjs`, and tests 54 and 57 the script's path from another.

52. The source stamp's writer. The `web` job of `ci.yml` has a step named `Write the source stamp` after the step that runs `pnpm build` and before the one that runs `pnpm check:dist`; it sets no `shell`, and its `env` sets `REPOSITORY_URL` to `${{ github.server_url }}/${{ github.repository }}` and `PULL_REQUEST_URL` to `${{ github.event.pull_request.html_url }}`. Its `run` script, run with `bash -e` in a temporary folder holding an empty `dist/`, with `REPOSITORY_URL` `https://github.com/blai30/partsmith`:
    - for a push, with `GITHUB_SHA` forty `a` digits and `PULL_REQUEST_URL` empty, exits 0 and writes `dist/SOURCE.txt` as exactly the five lines of 2.13 for that commit;
    - for a pull request, run in a shallow clone (`git clone --depth 1` from a `file://` URL), which also holds an empty `dist/`, of a repository the test makes, whose tip is a merge, made with `--no-ff`, of a head commit on another branch into a base commit, with a message whose second paragraph is the line `parent` followed by a space and forty `c` digits and the line `tree` followed by a space and forty `d` digits; with `GITHUB_SHA` that merge commit and `PULL_REQUEST_URL` `https://github.com/blai30/partsmith/pull/7`, it exits 0 and writes the five lines followed by `Pull request: https://github.com/blai30/partsmith/pull/7`, `Parents: {base} {head}` and `Tree: {tree}`, with `{tree}` what `git rev-parse {commit}^{tree}` prints in the repository the test made;
    - with `PULL_REQUEST_URL` set and `GITHUB_SHA` a commit the clone does not hold, exits with a status other than 0.
53. The source stamp's check. In the `deploy` job of `ci.yml`, the step named `Check the source stamp`, with `if: steps.secrets.outputs.available == 'true'`, comes after the step that downloads `web-dist` and before the step with `id: pages`. Its `run` script, run with `bash -e` in a temporary folder with `GITHUB_SHA` forty `a` digits, exits 0 and prints nothing when `ui/dist/SOURCE.txt` is the stamp of 2.13 for that commit, with and without the pull request lines. It exits 1 and prints exactly the source-stamp error of section 4 for that commit, one line on standard output, when `ui/dist/SOURCE.txt` is missing, when `ui/dist` is missing, when the stamp names forty `b` digits, and when its commit line has one more character after the commit.

**Vite's servers (2.7; `ui/scripts/vite-servers.test.mjs`, `node:test`, run by `pnpm test:scripts`)**

65. The headers of 2.7 on every response. `preview()` and `createServer()` of `vite`, each with `root` the `ui` folder, `configFile` its `vite.config.ts`, host `127.0.0.1` and port 0 (a free port the system picks): the preview serves a temporary `build.outDir` holding `index.html` and `assets/a.js`, and the test requests both; the dev server serves `ui/` itself, with a temporary `cacheDir` and `optimizeDeps.noDiscovery` (Vite then optimizes only the dependencies its plugins name, before `listen()` returns), and the test requests `/index.html` (Vite's HTML middleware), `/_headers` (a file of `ui/public`) and `/src/main.tsx` (a transformed module, as the core worker's script is). The test reads the five headers from `ui/public/_headers` (after the line `/*`, every line is two spaces, a name, `: ` and a value), so the servers are checked against the file that production serves. A GET of each path returns 200 with the expected headers and no `Vary` header that names `Origin`: from the preview, each of the five with the value of `_headers`; from the dev server, each of the four other than `Content-Security-Policy` with the value of `_headers`, and no `Content-Security-Policy` (2.7). The same GET with `If-None-Match` set to the first response's `ETag` returns 304 with the same headers and values and no `Vary` naming `Origin`. Before the plugin of 2.7, every 304 lacked the isolation headers and every response had `Vary: Origin`, and before ADR 0049 neither server sent `X-Content-Type-Options` or `Referrer-Policy`, and the preview no policy. Both servers close in the test's teardown, and then their temporary folders are removed, so `pnpm test:scripts` leaves no listener and no file behind: the dependency cache of `pnpm dev`, `ui/node_modules/.vite`, stays as it was (with discovery, Vite's optimizer wrote a `deps_temp_*` folder there after the server had closed, on every run).

**WebAssembly test steps (2.4; test 66 in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`)**

66. The steps of `kernel-wasm` and `core-wasm` in `ci.yml`. `kernel-wasm` has, after its step that uses `Swatinem/rust-cache`, three steps whose `run` is, in this order, `cargo test -p partsmith-occt-sys --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten`, `cargo test -p partsmith-sketch --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten` and `cargo test -p partsmith-engine --lib --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten dispatch::`. `core-wasm` has, after its step whose `run` is `scripts/build-wasm.sh` and before its step that uses `actions/upload-artifact`, a step whose `run` is `cargo test -p partsmith-wasm --test integration web_module::` with `env` `PARTSMITH_REQUIRE_WEB_MODULE: "1"`, then a step whose `run` is `cargo test -p partsmith-wasm --test integration web_parity:: -- --ignored`. A `run` written as a block scalar (`|`, which a command holding `:: ` or ending in `::` needs, since a plain YAML value cannot) is read as its one command line. Since a filter that matches no test passes with `running 0 tests`, the test also reads the sources the filters select: `crates/partsmith-wasm/tests/integration.rs` declares `mod web_module;` and `mod web_parity;`, `crates/partsmith-wasm/tests/web_parity.rs` has a `#[test]` with an `#[ignore` attribute next to it, and `crates/partsmith-engine/src/dispatch.rs` defines `fn a_panic_is_an_internal_error_that_poisons_its_document` and `fn a_poisoned_document_saves_its_recipe_from_before_the_failed_command`, the two tests protocol.md test 84 names.

No `.yml` file of `.github/workflows/`, `ci.yml`, `release.yml`, `nightly.yml` and `platforms.yml` among them, contains the text `PARTSMITH_PERF`, in an `env` at any level or in a `run`, so no job runs a `@perf` test, whose budgets hold only on the reference machine (1.2).

**Export examples (2.8; `crates/partsmith-cli/tests/examples/export.rs`, run by `cargo test --workspace`)**

Runner test 5 itself runs over the corpus in the `rust` job (test 3) and, on Windows and macOS, in `rust-platforms` (2.14). Tests 68 to 70 check its parts on literal JSON, files they write and, in test 70, the built binary. Test 107 (at the end of this section) checks its STEP round trip. Literal reports carry `<output>` as a JSON string value, since a Windows temporary path holds backslashes.

68. Which examples build. With a document whose `bodies` are `[{"id": "b-1", "name": "Body 1"}, {"id": "b-2", "name": "Body 2"}]` and on literal expected results, `{"features": [{"id": "f-box1", "type": "box", "status": "ok", "messages": []}, {"id": "f-fil1", "type": "fillet", "status": "warning", "messages": []}], "bodies": [{"id": "b-1", "name": "Body 1", "empty": true}, {"id": "b-2", "name": "Body 2", "volume": 6000.0}]}` builds; the same with the status of `f-fil1` `error` does not, although `b-2` has geometry (as `import/join`); `{"features": [{"id": "f-dp1", "type": "datum_plane", "status": "ok", "messages": []}], "bodies": []}` does not (as `datum_plane/flip`), and neither does `{"features": [{"id": "f-sk1", "type": "sketch", "status": "ok", "messages": []}], "bodies": [{"id": "b-1", "name": "Body 1", "empty": true}]}` (as `sketch/face-plane-empty-body`). The first literal does not build either when `b-2`'s document record is `{"id": "b-2", "name": "Body 2", "visible": false}` or `{"id": "b-2", "name": "Body 2", "role": "support_blocker"}`, and it builds when that record is `{"id": "b-2", "name": "Body 2", "visible": true, "role": "part"}`.
69. The report check, for the example `box/default` and the format `step`, after exit status 0, with `<output>` a file of 1200 bytes that the test writes in a temporary directory. The file is no STEP file. Test 69 stands in for the STEP round trip's import with one that measures 6000.0, the expected volume of `box/default`. Test 107 checks the import itself. The report `{"files": [{"path": "<output>", "media_type": "model/step", "bytes": 1200}], "warnings": [{"severity": "warning", "code": "non_manifold_edges", "message": "Body \"Body 1\" has 1 edge shared by more than two triangles. Slicers may repair it."}]}` gives no problem, since warnings are not compared. With `bytes` 1199 instead, the only problem is exactly `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step: /files/0/bytes is 1199, expected 1200`; with `media_type` `model/stl`, exactly `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step: /files/0/media_type is "model/stl", expected "model/step"`; with a second entry equal to the first, exactly `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step: /files/1 is unexpected`; with `"files": []`, exactly `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step: /files/0 is missing`; with the member `"error": {"severity": "error", "code": "export_failed", "message": "The STEP writer failed."}` added, exactly `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step: /error is unexpected`; and without `files`, exactly `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step: /files is missing`. The first report with no file at `<output>` gives one problem, which starts with `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step wrote no file: `.

The built binary, run as runner test 5 runs it, on `examples/datum_plane/flip.ptsm` to `stl` gives exactly one problem, `examples/datum_plane/flip.ptsm: partsmith export to stl exited with 1: error[nothing_to_export]: There is no visible body with geometry to export.`; exit status 0 with the standard output `not json` and no file at `<output>`, for `box/default` and `obj`, gives one problem, which starts with `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to obj printed invalid JSON: `; and the built binary, run as runner test 5 runs it, on `examples/box/not-an-example.ptsm`, which does not exist, to `stl` gives one problem, which starts with `examples/box/not-an-example.ptsm: partsmith export to stl exited with 3: error[io_error]: cannot read "examples/box/not-an-example.ptsm": `.

**Python round trips (2.8; `python/tests/test_gui_documents.py` and `python/tests/test_examples.py`, run by `pytest python/tests` in the `python` job)**

71. The documents the UI writes. The test is parametrized over `extrude-sides.ptsm`, `picks.ptsm` and `starting-values.ptsm` of `ui/e2e/fixtures/gui/`, and each passes the three checks of 2.8: every feature `ok`, python-api.md 3.9's first form (steps 1 to 5), and a generated script with no generic call. The messages of section 4 are checked on literal values: for `picks.ptsm` with features `f-a` ok, `f-b` error and `f-c` warning, check 1 gives the two lines `ui/e2e/fixtures/gui/picks.ptsm: f-b has status error.` and `ui/e2e/fixtures/gui/picks.ptsm: f-c has status warning.`, and none when every feature is ok; a script with two generic calls gives `ui/e2e/fixtures/gui/picks.ptsm: the generated script has 2 generic feature(...) calls.`; and a missing file gives `ui/e2e/fixtures/gui/picks.ptsm is missing. Run: PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 pnpm -C ui e2e round-trip.spec.ts`.
72. Twin check 4 on literal texts. A twin whose one generic call is `    feature(` followed by `"extrude",` on the next line, where the generated script has `    extrude(sketch_1, 20*mm, opposite_distance=10*mm, id="f-ext1")` and no generic call, fails with `examples/{path}.py writes more generic feature(...) calls than its generated script (1 against 0). Run: PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 pytest python/tests/test_examples.py`; the same twin passes against a generated script with one generic call, and so does a twin with no generic call against a generated script with one (a hand-edited twin). A line `    feature_1 = feature(` counts as a generic call; `feature(` inside a string or after `#` does not.

**The python job (2.4; test 73 in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`)**

73. The steps of `ci.yml`'s `python` job. It has, in this order, a step whose `run` is `pytest python/tests`, a step named `Measure the Python budgets` whose `run` is `pytest python/tests -m budgets -s`, and a step whose `run` is `pytest python/tests -m packaging`; none of the three has `continue-on-error`. So the budgets run on every CI run after the suite, a row that cannot be measured fails the job, and a row over its budget only warns, since the step adds no `-W` (python-api.md section 5).

**Corpus coverage (2.8; `crates/partsmith-cli/tests/examples/coverage.rs`, run by `cargo test --workspace`)**

Runner tests 6 to 9 themselves run over the corpus in the `rust` job (test 3) and, on Windows and macOS, in `rust-platforms` (2.14). Tests 74 to 78 check their parts on literal documents, literal expected results and literal file lists, with the engine's registry, schemas and printer profiles where a test names them. An example is given by its path below `examples/` without extension, as runner test 1 classifies it, and a test that keeps some problems compares them in the order the runner test sorts them.

74. Sketch record forms (runner test 6), with the engine's `.ptsm` schema. The one example `sketch/a`, whose one feature is `{"id": "f-sk1", "type": "sketch", "plane": {"kind": "origin", "plane": "XY"}, "entities": [{"id": "e-1", "type": "line", "start": [0, 0], "end": [10, 0], "construction": true}], "constraints": [{"id": "c-1", "type": "horizontal", "line": "e-1"}, {"id": "c-2", "type": "distance", "point": "e-1.start", "line": "e-1", "expr": "1 mm", "driving": false}]}`, holds the forms `horizontal (line)`, `distance (point, line, expr)` and the entity form `line (start, end)`, and no other: there is one problem for every other alternative of the two `oneOf` lists and none for these three, among them `Sketch constraint horizontal (points) has no example outside examples/bench/. Add one under examples/sketch/.`, `Sketch constraint distance (points, expr) has no example outside examples/bench/. Add one under examples/sketch/.`, `Sketch constraint distance (line, expr) has no example outside examples/bench/. Add one under examples/sketch/.` and `Sketch entity point (position) has no example outside examples/bench/. Add one under examples/sketch/.`. A constraint `{"id": "c-3", "type": "horizontal", "line": "e-1", "points": ["e-1.start", "e-1.end"]}` added to the sketch has no form, since each form of `horizontal` leaves one of its keys out of its `properties`, and changes no problem. The same sketch as the feature of `bench/fifty-features/model` instead holds nothing: there is one problem for every alternative.
75. Option values and reference kinds (runner test 7), with the engine's registry and `.ptsm` schema, keeping the problems that start with `Feature type linear_pattern:` or `Feature type mirror:`. The example `mirror/a` holding `{"id": "f-mir1", "type": "mirror", "body": "b-1", "plane": {"kind": "origin", "plane": "YZ"}}` and the example `linear_pattern/a` holding `{"id": "f-lp1", "type": "linear_pattern", "body": "b-1", "direction": {"axis": {"kind": "edge", "body": "b-1", "edge": {"q": "edge(f-box1/front|f-box1/top)", "fp": "line|n=1,0,0|c=0,-5,10|l=10"}}, "count": "2", "spacing": "20"}, "op": "new", "target": "b-2"}` give exactly these problems, each followed by ` has no example outside examples/bench/. Add one under examples/linear_pattern/.` or `... examples/mirror/.` for its type: `Feature type linear_pattern: direction.axis.kind face`, `Feature type linear_pattern: direction.axis.kind origin`, `Feature type linear_pattern: direction.axis.kind sketch_line`, `Feature type linear_pattern: op join`, `Feature type linear_pattern: second_direction.axis.kind edge`, `Feature type linear_pattern: second_direction.axis.kind face`, `Feature type linear_pattern: second_direction.axis.kind origin`, `Feature type linear_pattern: second_direction.axis.kind sketch_line`, `Feature type mirror: op new`, `Feature type mirror: plane.kind datum` and `Feature type mirror: plane.kind face`. The mirror leaves `op` out, so it holds `join`, the default; the pattern writes `new`, so it holds no `join`, and without a second direction it holds no kind of `second_direction.axis`. With both records in the one example `bench/fifty-features/model` instead, the kept problems are those and also `Feature type linear_pattern: direction.axis.kind edge`, `Feature type linear_pattern: op new`, `Feature type mirror: op join` and `Feature type mirror: plane.kind origin`, each with its ending. With the mirror record in an example `box/c` instead of `mirror/a`, and the pattern in `linear_pattern/a`, every value of `mirror` is a problem: the linear pattern's problems above and `Feature type mirror: op join`, `Feature type mirror: op new`, `Feature type mirror: plane.kind datum`, `Feature type mirror: plane.kind face` and `Feature type mirror: plane.kind origin`, each with its ending, since only a record under `examples/mirror/` counts for `mirror`.

The runner's defaults and kinds (runner test 7), with the engine's registry and `.ptsm` schema. `Registry::validate_and_canonicalize` gives params without `op` for `{"id": "f-mir1", "type": "mirror", "body": "b-1", "plane": {"kind": "origin", "plane": "YZ"}, "op": "join"}`, for `{"id": "f-lp1", "type": "linear_pattern", "body": "b-1", "direction": {"axis": {"kind": "origin", "axis": "X"}, "count": "2", "spacing": "20"}, "op": "join"}` and for `{"id": "f-cp1", "type": "circular_pattern", "body": "b-1", "axis": {"kind": "origin", "axis": "Z"}, "count": "4", "op": "join"}`; params without `hole_type` for `{"id": "f-hole1", "type": "hole", "body": "b-1", "sketch": "f-sk1", "points": ["e-p1"], "diameter": "5", "hole_type": {"kind": "simple"}, "extent": {"kind": "through_all"}}`; and params without `plane` for each of `{"id": "f-box1", "type": "box", "body": "b-1", "size": ["10", "10", "10"], "plane": {"kind": "origin", "plane": "XY"}, "op": "new"}`, `{"id": "f-cyl1", "type": "cylinder", "body": "b-1", "radius": "5", "height": "10", "plane": {"kind": "origin", "plane": "XY"}, "op": "new"}`, `{"id": "f-sph1", "type": "sphere", "body": "b-1", "radius": "10", "plane": {"kind": "origin", "plane": "XY"}, "op": "new"}`, `{"id": "f-cone1", "type": "cone", "body": "b-1", "bottom_radius": "10", "top_radius": "5", "height": "10", "plane": {"kind": "origin", "plane": "XY"}, "op": "new"}` and `{"id": "f-tor1", "type": "torus", "body": "b-1", "major_radius": "20", "minor_radius": "5", "plane": {"kind": "origin", "plane": "XY"}, "op": "new"}`.
