**The browser installs of `e2e` and `e2e-engines` (2.4, 2.14, open issue 16; test 79 in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`)**

79. A slow mirror ends the install, not the job. In `ci.yml`'s `e2e-engines`, the step whose `run` is `pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps ${{ matrix.browser }}` is named `Install ${{ matrix.browser }} and its system packages`, so GitHub's timeout error names the step and the engine, and has `timeout-minutes: 10`, under the job's 60. In `ci.yml`'s `e2e`, the step whose `run` is `pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps chromium` is named `Install chromium and its system packages` and has `timeout-minutes: 10`, under the job's 60. The report upload after each sets no `if-no-files-found`, so after a failed install it adds only the warning of 2.14, not a second error.

**Test roots (ADR 0035; `ui/scripts/test-roots.test.mjs`, `node:test`, run by `pnpm test:scripts`)**

80. Every test file is built. ADR 0035 builds a crate's integration tests as one binary, and a file of `tests/` that no root declares is never compiled, formatted or linted. The test reads the root `Cargo.toml`'s `members`, each `<folder>/*` standing for the folders in that folder, and for every member with a `tests/` folder: its `Cargo.toml` sets `autotests = false` in `[package]`; its roots are its `[[test]]` targets, `tests/<name>.rs` or the target's `path`; every other `.rs` file directly in `tests/`, and every folder of `tests/` that holds a `mod.rs`, is declared by a root; every other `.rs` file of such a folder is declared by its `mod.rs`; and every `.rs` file of a folder `tests/<stem>/` beside a file `tests/<stem>.rs` is declared by that file (`web_parity/` of `partsmith-wasm`, `examples/` of `partsmith-cli`). A file declares a module with a line `mod <name>;` or `pub mod <name>;`, attributes such as `#[cfg(...)]` allowed above it. Folders with neither a `mod.rs` nor a `<stem>.rs` beside them, such as `fixtures/` and `node/`, and folders below the first level are not read. One failure lists every member without `autotests = false` or without a `[[test]]` target, and every file not declared, each with the line that declares it and the file that line goes in. The same rules on literal manifests and files: a module declared under `#[cfg(...)]` counts as declared; a `[[test]]` named `budgets` is a root, which nothing declares; and a manifest without `autotests = false`, a `tests/<stem>.rs` that no root declares, a file of a `mod.rs` folder that its `mod.rs` does not declare, and a file of `tests/<stem>/` that `tests/<stem>.rs` does not declare each give exactly one problem.

**The release and its copies (3.6; tests 81 to 83 in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`)**

81. The release matrix. The `bundle` job of `release.yml` has the three rows of 3.6's table, in this order, each with exactly the keys `runner`, `bundles` and `files`: `ubuntu-24.04` with `deb,rpm` and `target/release/bundle/deb/*.deb target/release/bundle/rpm/*.rpm`; `windows-2025` with `nsis` and `target/release/bundle/nsis/*-setup.exe`; and `macos-15` with `app,dmg` and `target/release/bundle/dmg/*.dmg`. No row builds an AppImage (ADR 0034 choice 1).
82. The copies of 3.6. In `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md`, the numbered items below the heading `## Release checklist` are, word for word and in the same order, the numbered items below the line of 3.6 that starts with `**Release checklist**`. Both lists are numbered from 1 with no gap. The line that starts with `These builds are not signed yet.` appears exactly once in this file, in `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` and in `README.md`. It is the same line in all three (3.6, 2.12 part 3).
83. Item numbers of the checklist named elsewhere. This file and `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` name items of 3.6's release checklist by number outside the checklist: `checklist item` or `(3.6, item` before one number, or `checklist items` before a range such as `4 to 6`. The test lists every such reference of the two files, in the order each file holds them, with words of each item it names. In this file they are the platforms item in 2.5, in steps 3 and 4 of 2.14's promotion and twice in section 8's entry on the platforms workflow, and the source route item in section 8's entry on how OCCT is linked. In `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` they are the platforms item, then the dry run, the tag and the draft's assets in the comments of its Commands block. The item of 3.6 with each named number holds the words listed for it. A change that adds such a reference lists it in the test, and a change that renumbers the checklist updates every reference.

**Wrapped paragraphs (2.9, ui.md open issue 44; test 84 in `ui/scripts/notices.test.mjs`)**

84. Partsmith's own paragraphs are wrapped, and nothing else is. `wrapParagraph` of `Statement.` is `Statement.`. Of 39 `a`, a space, 40 `b`, a space and `c`, it is the first 80 characters, an LF and `c`. Of 40 `a`, a space and 40 `b`, it is the two words on two lines. Of `short`, a space, 90 `u`, a space and `end`, it is `short`, the 90 `u` and `end` on three lines. Of 70 `a`, a space, `run`, a no-break space, `it`, a no-break space, `now`, a space and `end`, it is the 70 `a` and `run it now end` (with spaces) on two lines. Joining the lines of each result with single spaces gives its paragraph back, with a space for each no-break space. `renderNotices` of a literal fixture whose `partsmith` text is one line of 200 `x`, whose OCCT statement is two paragraphs of `word` 30 times with single spaces between, and whose one OCCT entry has a name of 100 `n`: the text holds the statement as `wrapParagraph` gives each paragraph, joined by one blank line, and holds the entry's line and the line of 200 `x` whole. In both committed notices files, every line above `== License texts ==` that is longer than 80 characters is an entry line: it ends with `, texts` followed by one or more ` [{n}]`. Each command that 2.9 (Wrapping) lists for a file lies whole on one line of that committed file.

**The OCCT source in every deployment (2.13, ADR 0034; tests 85 to 87 in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`, test 88 by CI and by hand)**

Tests 85 to 87 read the workflow files as tests 49 to 53 do, and run in `pnpm test:scripts` with no network. Tests 86 and 87 run their git commands with the Git environment of tests 52, 55 and 58, and make their fixture repository with the function that makes test 55's. Below, `{v}` and `{tag}` are the `OCCT_VERSION` and `OCCT_TAG` of `scripts/occt-version.sh`, and the archive's name `occt-{v}-source.tar.gz` comes from the one function that tests 55 and 57 use too. These functions are in `ui/scripts/occt-fixture.mjs`.

85. The archive's cache. In `ci.yml`'s `sources` job (2.13, The source with every artifact), the step after the checkout has `id: occt-source` and uses `actions/cache@v6.1.0` with a `with` of exactly `path: .occt/source` and `key: occt-source-${{ hashFiles('scripts/occt-source-archive.sh', 'scripts/occt-version.sh') }}`. The step after it has `if: steps.occt-source.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'`, no `shell`, and a `run` of exactly the two lines of 2.13 (Made in the `sources` jobs), in that order. In `release.yml`'s `sources` job, no step uses `actions/cache` or `actions/cache/restore`, and the step after the checkout has no `if`, no `shell` and that same `run`. In each of the two, these steps come before the step named `Write the OCCT source archive`. No other job of the three files has a step that names `.occt/source` or `scripts/occt-source-archive.sh`: `occt-native` makes no archive, `web` restores no cache, and `platforms.yml` has no `sources` job (2.14, The move). The `needs` of `web` is exactly `[core-wasm, sources]` (2.4).
86. The archive's writer. In each `sources` job (2.13), the step named `Write the OCCT source archive` comes after the steps of test 85 and before the step named `Write the Partsmith source archive`, and its `run` is the same in the two files. It sets no `shell` and no `working-directory`, and its `env` is exactly `OCCT_SOURCE_SHA256` with the SHA-256 of 2.13's Measured paragraph. That paragraph names the tag `{tag}` and the `OCCT_COMMIT` of `scripts/occt-version.sh`. The test makes the fixture repository of test 87, with its two commits and the annotated tag `{tag}` on the first, and the archive of a commit as test 87 makes it. The step's `run` script runs with `bash -e` in `<root>`, a temporary folder whose `scripts/occt-version.sh` sets `{v}`, `{tag}` and, as `OCCT_COMMIT`, the fixture's tagged commit, which holds a copy of `scripts/check-source-archive.sh` and no `sources/`, with `OCCT_SOURCE_SHA256` in its environment:
    - with `<root>/.occt/source/occt-{v}-source.tar.gz` the archive of the tagged commit and `OCCT_SOURCE_SHA256` its SHA-256, it exits 0, `sources/` then holds only `occt-{v}-source.tar.gz`, with the same bytes, standard output is exactly the line `{sha256}  occt-{v}-source.tar.gz`, with two spaces, as `sha256sum` writes it, and standard error is empty;
    - with the same file and the step's own `OCCT_SOURCE_SHA256`, it exits 0 and copies the file the same way, and standard output is that line and then the warning of section 4 for `{v}` and the two values;
    - it exits 1, prints exactly the cached-archive error of section 4 for `{v}` and the tagged commit, one line on standard output, and prints nothing on standard error, when the file is the archive of the second commit, and when it is the tagged commit's archive without its last 8 bytes (gzip's checksum and length), whose first header still names the tagged commit;
    - with `<root>/.occt/source/` empty, and with it holding only `occt-9.9.9-source.tar.gz`, the archive of the tagged commit, it exits with a status other than 0 and `sources/` holds no file.
87. The archive's check. In the `deploy` job of `ci.yml`, the step named `Check the OCCT source archive`, with `if: steps.secrets.outputs.available == 'true'`, comes after the step named `Check the source stamp` and before the step with `id: pages`. The test makes a fixture repository with two commits and an annotated tag `{tag}` on the first. The tagged commit also holds `big.bin`, 1,048,576 zero bytes. The archive's tar is then larger than a pipe's buffer (64 KiB on Linux). The archive of a commit is `git archive --format=tar --prefix=occt-{v}/ <commit>`, piped to `gzip -9 -n`. The step's `run` script runs with `bash -e` in a temporary folder whose `scripts/occt-version.sh` sets `{v}`, `{tag}` and, as `OCCT_COMMIT`, the fixture's tagged commit, and which holds a copy of `scripts/check-source-archive.sh`.
    - When `ui/dist/occt-{v}-source.tar.gz` is the archive of the tagged commit, it exits 0 and prints nothing on standard output or standard error. It does so twice: once with SIGPIPE at its default, and once with SIGPIPE ignored (`trap '' PIPE` in the shell that starts it), where a `gzip` that writes into a closed pipe prints an error and exits 1. Before the two passing runs, the test checks that the fixture breaks the pipe: `gzip -dc <archive> | git get-tar-commit-id`, run with SIGPIPE ignored, prints `gzip: stdout: Broken pipe` on standard error.
    - It exits 1, prints exactly the OCCT-source error of section 4 for `{v}` and the tagged commit, one line on standard output, and prints nothing on standard error, in each of these cases: `ui/dist/occt-{v}-source.tar.gz` is missing; `ui/dist` is missing; the file is the archive of the second commit; the file is the tagged commit's archive without its last 8 bytes (gzip's checksum and length), whose first header still names the tagged commit; the file is that archive's tar without gzip; the file is empty; the file is `tar -czf` of a folder holding the same files, a whole gzip file whose tar names no commit; and only `ui/dist/occt-9.9.9-source.tar.gz` exists, holding the tagged commit's archive.
88. A deployment holds the archive, by CI and by hand, once after the first preview and the first production deployment with this change. The `deploy` job's log shows the step `Check the OCCT source archive` passing. For each deployment, the SHA-256 is the one that the step `Write the OCCT source archive` printed in the run that deployed it (2.13, Copied into the build). For each of the two deployment URLs (production `https://partsmith.pages.dev` and the preview's alias), `curl -sSfL -H 'Accept-Encoding: gzip, deflate, br, zstd' -o occt-{v}-source.tar.gz {url}/occt-{v}-source.tar.gz` (without `--compressed`) downloads a file whose SHA-256 is that one, and `curl -sSI -H 'Accept-Encoding: gzip, deflate, br, zstd' {url}/occt-{v}-source.tar.gz` shows status 200, `content-type: application/gzip` and no `content-encoding` header. The header asks for the encodings a browser takes: a body that Pages encodes once more fails the SHA-256, and the raw archive sent with `content-encoding: gzip` fails the header check. Open issue 15 records both values, whether they equal each other, and whether they equal 2.13's (the step warns when one does not). When the archive comes with a `content-encoding`, the fix is a rule for its path in `_headers`, which changes `EXPECTED_HEADERS`, 2.7 and test 14: a change of the security headers that needs human review (ADR 0049), recorded in open issue 15. Only CI and a person can make this check: the containers this work runs in cannot reach `pages.dev` (their proxy refused it on 2026-11-04).

**The dependency source archives and the Windows installer's code (2.9, 2.13, ADR 0034 questions 2 and 3; tests 89 to 92 in `ui/scripts/dependency-source-archive.test.mjs`, 93 to 95 in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`, 96 in `ui/scripts/notices.test.mjs`, 97 by hand, 98 by CI and by hand)**

Tests 89 to 96 run in `pnpm test:scripts` with no network. The script's library takes the registry's base URL, the `fetch` it downloads with and the `run` it sends every `cargo` command through as arguments. The tests replace them: a `node:http` server on `127.0.0.1`, port 0, serves a registry folder the test makes, Node's `fetch` reaches it, and `run` answers `cargo vendor` by copying a fixture folder into the folder it names and `cargo metadata` with literal metadata. `tar` and `gzip` are the real ones, as in tests 55 and 87. The tests use only the exports of 2.13 (The script's library) and `scopeCrates` of 2.9. Since 2026-11-05 (tests 108 to 113 and 115), the fixture's repository root holds a copy of the repository's `deny.toml`, each fixture tarball is an npm tarball (a gzip file of a tar whose folder `package/` holds a `package.json` with `license` `MIT`), and the calls pass a `nativePackages` table that records the fixture's packages with an `os` or `cpu` list (`three` 3.0.0 and `win-only` 1.0.0) with nothing to carry (`crates: []`, no `commit`, no `notices`). The desktop cases of test 91 pass a `tauriCli` fixture whose `Cargo.lock` lists only its root package, and a crate registry that serves it. Their vendor command carries `--sync` with that fixture's manifest. Tests 89 to 92 otherwise hold as written.

89. Lock packages and platforms. `lockPackages` of a literal lock text with `lockfileVersion: '9.0'`, an `importers` section, a `packages` section and a `snapshots` section that repeats the keys returns, in the file's order and nothing from `snapshots`, one `{ key, name, version, integrity, os, cpu, libc }` per entry of `packages`: `'@scope/a@1.0.0'` with an integrity, `engines`, `cpu: [x64]` and `os: [linux]`; `b@2.0.0` with an integrity and a `peerDependencies` map whose indented line `c: '*'` is not an entry; `c-musl@1.0.0` with `cpu: [x64]`, `os: [linux]` and `libc: [musl]`; `d@1.0.0` with `os: ['!win32']`; and `e@1.0.0` whose `resolution` is `{tarball: https://example.com/e.tgz}`, with `integrity` null. Fields an entry lacks are null. `platformPackages` of those with (`linux`, `x64`, `glibc`) is `@scope/a`, `b`, `d` and `e`; with (`win32`, `x64`) `b` and `e`; with (`darwin`, `arm64`) `b`, `d` and `e`; with all three the union, in the file's order. `SCOPES` is the object of 2.13: `SCOPES.web` has the root crate `partsmith-wasm`, the triple `wasm32-unknown-emscripten` and the first platform, and `SCOPES.desktop` the root crate `partsmith-desktop`, the triples of `DESKTOP_TARGETS` in their order and all three platforms. `tarballPath('@scope/a', '1.0.0')` is `@scope/a/-/a-1.0.0.tgz`, and `tarballPath('b', '2.0.0')` is `b/-/b-2.0.0.tgz`. Every entry of the repository's `ui/pnpm-lock.yaml` has an integrity.
90. Tarballs and their integrity. `integrityMismatch` of some bytes and their `sha512-` integrity is null, and of their `sha1-` integrity too. Of other bytes it is the integrity of those bytes in the same algorithm. With the test's registry serving three tarballs whose integrities a literal lock text records, the npm archive that `writeDependencySources` writes for `web` holds the folder `npm-packages-web/` with exactly those three files at their `tarballPath`, byte for byte. The server answered one request for each. Each of these rejects with exactly its message of section 4 and leaves the output folder empty: one tarball's bytes changed on the server (the integrity message, naming the changed bytes' integrity); one tarball missing, which the server answers with 404 (the download message with `404`); and a lock entry without integrity (that message, before any request). The server answered one request for the changed tarball and one for the missing one. `DOWNLOAD_RETRY_DELAYS` is `[2000, 10000]`. With `retryDelays` of 1 ms each: when the server answers 429 and then 408 to the first tarball, drops the connection once for the second and answers 503 and then 500 to the third before it serves each, the archive holds the three tarballs as before, and the server answered 3, 2 and 3 requests for them. With `retryDelays` of 30 ms each: when it answers 503, 502 and 504 to one tarball, the call rejects with the download message with `504`, and when it drops the connection three times, with `fetch failed`, Node's message. Each takes three requests and at least the two waits, and leaves the output folder empty.

Bytes, parts and the command. `PART_SIZE` is 25165824. `writeParts` of a file `{name}` of 25 bytes into another folder with a part size of 10 writes `{name}.part-00` and `{name}.part-01` of 10 bytes and `{name}.part-02` of 5 there, and resolves to those three names; joined in order they are the file. A file of 20 bytes gives two parts and no empty third, and a file of 0 bytes one empty part `{name}.part-00`. `archiveNames('web')` is `['rust-crates-web.tar.gz', 'npm-packages-web.tar']`, and `archiveNames('desktop')` the same with `desktop`. Two runs of `writeDependencySources` with the fixtures of tests 90 and 91 write the same parts, byte for byte. The crates' archive is a gzip file whose header holds no file name and a modification time of 0. In the listing that `tar -tvf` gives of each archive in UTC, the entries come in the order of `--sort=name` (folder by folder, each name in byte order), each with owner and group 0 and the time 1970-01-01 00:00. The result is `[{ archive, bytes, sha256, lastPart }]` for the crates and then the packages, where `bytes` and `sha256` are those of the joined parts and `lastPart` names the last part in the folder. `summaryLine` of each result and the output folder is exactly `Wrote {archive} to {directory}: {bytes} bytes, SHA-256 {sha256}, last part {archive}.part-{NN}.`, the line the command prints. The command `node scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs` exits 2 with the usage line of section 4 with no argument, with `web`, with `wrong out` and with `web out extra`. With a missing directory it exits 1 with its message. With a stub `tar` first on `PATH` whose `--version` prints `bsdtar 3.7.4`, it exits 1 with the GNU tar message before it runs any `cargo` command. With a stub `cargo` that prints `error: no network` and exits 1, it exits 1 with the command message of section 4 for the metadata command of 2.9 input 1 for `wasm32-unknown-emscripten`. `writeDependencySources` rejects with the command message of section 4 and removes its temporary folder when `run` rejects the vendor command (status 101), when a stub `tar` that names GNU tar fails to pack the crates, and when a stub `gzip` fails. When the output folder already holds `npm-packages-web.tar.part-00`, it rejects with `EEXIST`, that file keeps its bytes and no other file is left. After each of these, and after each failure of tests 90 and 91, the output folder holds nothing it did not hold before.

**The full-rebuild gate (2.14, technical design section 14, ADR 0054; tests 104 and 105 in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`, 106 by CI)**

Tests 104 and 105 read `platforms.yml` as test 60 does, and test 104 also reads `ci.yml`'s `rust-platforms`. They run in `pnpm test:scripts` with no network. Below, `{operation}` is `run of bench50 from an empty cache`.

107. The STEP round trip, for the example `box/default`, whose expected results hold one body, `b-1`, with the volume 6000.0, which its `.ptsm` leaves visible and a part. The expected volume is checked on literal expected results and a literal document, as test 68 checks which examples build. Every other case runs runner test 5's check of one export of `box/default` to `step` at `<output>`, a file that the test writes in a temporary directory, after exit status 0 and a report that matches the file there, with the expected volume 6000.0, unless the case says otherwise. The volume check stands in for the import with one that measures the volume it names. The other cases import in a headless session, as runner test 5 does:
    - The expected volume. For the first literal of test 68 and its document it is 6000.0. With `{"id": "b-3", "name": "Body 3", "volume": 1000.0}` added to the bodies of those expected results and `{"id": "b-3", "name": "Body 3"}` to the bodies of the document, it is 7000.0. With that record `{"id": "b-3", "name": "Body 3", "visible": false}` or `{"id": "b-3", "name": "Body 3", "role": "support_blocker"}`, it is 6000.0 again.
    - The volume check. With an import that measures 6000.0 or 6000.005 (8.3e-7 relative), there is no problem. One that measures 6000.01 gives exactly `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step: the imported volume is 6000.01, expected 6000.0`, and one that measures 5999.99 gives exactly `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step: the imported volume is 5999.99, expected 6000.0`.
    - A file that does not import. With `<output>` holding the 5 bytes `hello` and the report `{"files": [{"path": "<output>", "media_type": "model/step", "bytes": 5}]}`, the only problem is exactly `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step wrote a file that does not import: Import failed. The file is not a valid STEP file.` (the message of engine.md test 93).
    - The order of the checks. With the same file and `bytes` 6 in that report, the only problem is exactly `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step: /files/0/bytes is 6, expected 5`: a report that does not match is not followed by an import. With the same file, the format `stl` and the report `{"files": [{"path": "<output>", "media_type": "model/stl", "bytes": 5}]}`, there is no problem: only `step` files are imported.
    - The file the binary writes. Runner test 5's own work for one export (the run of the built binary, the check of its report and the import of its file in a headless session) on `examples/box/default.ptsm` to `step` gives no problem.
    - Another expected volume. The same work for the same export, with the expected volume of expected results whose `b-1` has the volume 7000.0 and of the `.ptsm` of `box/default` as committed, gives exactly one problem. It starts with `examples/box/default.ptsm: partsmith export to step: the imported volume is ` and ends with `, expected 7000.0`. The volume compared is the one the import measured.

    Against the runner before this change, the cases of a volume that does not match, of a file that does not import and of another expected volume fail: it reported no problem for any of them.

**What the dependency archives carry (2.9, 2.10, 2.13, the owner's decisions of 2026-11-05 and ADR 0033's and ADR 0034's amendments of that date; tests 108 to 113 in `ui/scripts/dependency-source-archive.test.mjs`, 114 by hand)**

Tests 108 to 113 run in `pnpm test:scripts` with no network, with the fixtures of tests 89 to 92 (their note above). Where a test needs a Git repository, it makes one in a temporary folder with `git init`, commits and tags, and passes its `file://` URL as an entry's `source`. Where it needs crates.io, a second `node:http` server on `127.0.0.1` serves `{name}/{name}-{version}.crate` files of a folder the test makes, and the test passes its URL as `crateRegistry`. A fixture `.crate` is a gzip file of a tar whose folder is `{name}-{version}/`. After each failure the output folder holds nothing it did not hold before.

108. Licenses of the npm packages. `allowedLicenses` of the repository's `deny.toml` is `MIT`, `Apache-2.0`, `Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception`, `BSD-2-Clause`, `BSD-3-Clause`, `ISC`, `Zlib`, `Unicode-3.0`, `CC0-1.0`, `MPL-2.0`, `BSL-1.0`, `MIT-0`, `Python-2.0` and `BlueOak-1.0.0`, in this order. `tarballLicense` is `ISC` for a tarball whose `package/package.json` has `"license": "ISC"`; `MIT` for one whose only folder is `aria-query/` and whose `aria-query/package.json` has `"license": "MIT"`; still `ISC` for the first tarball when it also holds `package/lib/package.json` with `"license": "GPL-3.0-only"`; `MIT` for one that holds `package/package.json` with `MIT` and then `other/package.json` with `GPL-3.0-only`, and `GPL-3.0-only` for the same two files in the reverse order; and `Unknown` for one with no `package.json` in its folder, for one whose `package.json` is not valid JSON, for one whose `package.json` has `"license": {"type": "MIT"}` and for one whose `package.json` has only `"licenses": [{"type": "MIT"}]`. It is `ISC` for a tarball whose only folder has a name of 99 bytes, which GNU tar writes with `--format=ustar` (a name with a prefix), `--format=pax` (a pax `path`) and `--format=gnu` (a GNU long name). It runs no command: with stub `tar` and `gzip` commands that fail first on `PATH`, it gives the same results. With a lock text of five packages whose tarballs' licenses are, in the lock file's order, `MIT-0`, `GPL-3.0-only`, `(MIT OR GPL-3.0-only)`, `{"type": "MIT"}` and `Python-2.0` (the last with the folder `types-x/`), `writeDependencySources` for `web` rejects with exactly two lines: the message of 2.10 for the second package with `GPL-3.0-only`, then for the fourth with `Unknown`. The server answered one request for each tarball, and `run` received the commands of test 91 and no other. Without those two packages it resolves, and the npm archive holds the other three. The license is read from the bytes the archive holds: a tarball with the lock file's integrity whose license is `GPL-3.0-only` fails even when a tarball of the same name and another version under `MIT` is in the lock file too.
109. Native packages. `NATIVE_PACKAGES` is exactly the table of 2.13, in its order. For each scope, every package that `platformPackages` gives from the repository's `ui/pnpm-lock.yaml` and whose entry has an `os`, `cpu` or `libc` list belongs to an entry of `NATIVE_PACKAGES`, and each entry has at least one package of the desktop scope that belongs to it. With a table of `@scope/binding` 1.0.0, `solo` 2.0.0 and `unused` 1.0.0, `nativeEntries` of the packages `@scope/binding-linux-x64-gnu` 1.0.0 (with `cpu`), `@scope/binding` 1.0.0 (no list), `solo` 2.0.0 (with `os`) and `solo-linux-x64` 2.0.0 (with `cpu`) is the first two entries, in the table's order, each once. It throws exactly the native-package message of section 4 for `@scope/binding-linux-x64-gnu` 1.0.1 (with `cpu`), whose version no entry has, and for `@scope/bindingx` 1.0.0 (with `cpu`), whose name is neither `@scope/binding` nor starts with `@scope/binding-`. When two packages belong to no entry, it throws for the first in the order of the packages. `writeDependencySources` for `web` with a lock text that holds such a package rejects with that message, and `run` and the server received nothing. A package with no list whose tarball holds `package/build/addon.node`, and that belongs to no entry, makes the call reject with exactly the native-package message for it once the tarballs are downloaded, even when its license is `GPL-3.0-only`, and leaves the output folder empty. So does one whose tarball holds `package/lib/codec.wasm`, and one whose `.node` file has a path longer than 100 bytes, in a tarball that GNU tar writes with `--format=ustar` (a name with a prefix), with `--format=pax` (a pax `path`) or with `--format=gnu` (a GNU long name). So does each of ten packages whose tarball holds `package/bin/tool`, with no telling name, starting with one of the ten byte sequences of `PREBUILT_MAGIC_NUMBERS` followed by other bytes. With an entry for that package in `nativePackages`, the call resolves. A tarball whose only such names are a folder `package/x.node/` and a file `package/notes.node.txt` holds no prebuilt file, and so does one whose other files are a script starting with `#!`, a file of the one byte `M`, an empty file, and a file with the ELF bytes after its first five bytes.

The test makes a Git repository with the files `node/Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock` and `README.md`, a commit tagged `v1.0.0` and a second commit, which changes `README.md`, tagged `v1.1.0`, and a table with two entries `native-css` 1.0.0 and 1.1.0 whose `source` is its URL, `tag` the two tags, `commit` the tags' commits and `manifest` `node/Cargo.toml`. Each source archive is a gzip file whose header holds no file name and a modification time of 0, whose bytes equal `git archive` of 2.13 for its tag piped to `gzip -9 -n`, whose entries are the files of the tag under `native-css-{version}/`, and for which `git get-tar-commit-id` prints the tag's commit. The crates archive holds `native-css-crates/config.toml`, exactly `vendorConfig('native-css-crates', 'native-css')`, and `native-css-crates/vendor/` with the fixture's vendor folder. `run` received, in the repository root, `cargo vendor --locked --versioned-dirs --manifest-path {temporary folder}/clones/native-css-1.0.0/node/Cargo.toml --sync {temporary folder}/clones/native-css-1.1.0/node/Cargo.toml {vendor}` once. With only the 1.0.0 package in the lock file, the command has no `--sync`, and the folder holds no archive of 1.1.0. `vendorConfig('rust-crates-web', 'Partsmith')` is `cargoConfig('web')`. Two runs write the same parts. When the table's commit of 1.1.0 is the commit of `v1.0.0`, the call rejects with exactly the tag-commit message of section 4 for `v1.1.0`. When the lock file holds no package of an entry with a `commit` or `crates`, the npm archive has no folder `npm-sources-web/`. When the `Cargo.lock` of `v1.0.0` also lists `git-dep` 0.1.0 with a `git+` source, the call with only the 1.0.0 entry rejects with exactly the tool-lock source message of section 4 for it, with `{subject}` `native-css 1.0.0`, `run` received no `cargo vendor` command with `--manifest-path`, and the output folder stays empty. When `source` names a folder that does not exist, the call with `retryDelays` of 30 ms each rejects with the command message of section 4 for `git clone --quiet --depth 1 --branch v1.0.0 {source} {clone}`, after at least the two waits, and the output folder stays empty.
111. Crates under MPL-2.0 of a native package. With a table entry `native-ui` 2.0.0 whose `crates` hold `mpl-crate` 0.1.0 with the SHA-256 of the bytes the crate server serves, and the same crate in a second entry `native-ux` 1.0.0, and a lock text holding `native-ui-linux-x64-gnu` 2.0.0 and `native-ux-linux-x64-gnu` 1.0.0, `npm-sources-web/` holds exactly `mpl-crate-0.1.0.crate`, byte for byte as served, and the crate server answered one request. The crate server serves a fixture `tauri-cli-9.9.9.crate`, a gzip file of a tar whose folder `tauri-cli-9.9.9/` holds `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `src/main.rs` and `templates/app/.gitignore`. Its `Cargo.lock` lists the root `tauri-cli` 9.9.9 without a source, and `stubbed` 2.0.0 and `tool-only` 1.0.0 from crates.io. The fixture vendor folder of test 91 also holds `tool-only-1.0.0`. With `tauriCli` `{ version: '9.9.9', sha256 }` of that file, `writeDependencySources` for `desktop` writes a crate archive whose `rust-crates-desktop/vendor/tauri-cli-9.9.9/` holds the four files of the crate unchanged and a `.cargo-checksum.json` whose text is exactly `{"files":{...},"package":"{sha256}"}` with the SHA-256 of each of the four files, by path in byte order. `stubbed-2.0.0/` and `tool-only-1.0.0/` stay whole, though the desktop crate set lacks them, and `unlisted-3.0.0/` stays a stub. `run` received the vendor command `cargo vendor --locked --versioned-dirs --sync {temporary folder}/tauri-cli-9.9.9/Cargo.toml {vendor}` once, and the crate server one request. For `web`, `run` received the vendor command without `--sync`, and the crate server no request. When its `Cargo.lock` also lists `git-dep` 0.1.0 with a `git+` source, the desktop call rejects with exactly the tool-lock source message of section 4 for it, with `{subject}` `tauri-cli 9.9.9`, before any `cargo vendor` command, and leaves the output folder empty. With `TMPDIR` set to a folder inside the fixture's repository root, the desktop call rejects with exactly the temporary-folder message of section 4, naming the real paths of that folder and of the root, and `run`, the crate server and the npm server received nothing. The web call with the same `TMPDIR` resolves.

First, `node scripts/native-notices.mjs` in `ui/` writes the notices files of `ui/scripts/notices/native/`. The pull request commits what it writes. On Linux with no route to the internet, with the archives that `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs` writes for both scopes joined and extracted into `<sources>`, a full path: `CARGO_HOME=<sources>/rust-crates-desktop cargo install tauri-cli --version 2.12.2 --locked` exits 0 and installs `<sources>/rust-crates-desktop/bin/cargo-tauri`, whose `--version` prints `tauri-cli 2.12.2`. Test 97's desktop build then runs with that `cargo tauri` and compiles `libdbus-sys` without `vendor/dbus/`. For each lightningcss version of `NATIVE_PACKAGES`, with `npm-sources-web/lightningcss-{version}-source.tar.gz` and `lightningcss-crates.tar.gz` extracted, `CARGO_HOME=<full path>/lightningcss-crates cargo build --locked --release -p lightningcss_node` in `lightningcss-{version}/` exits 0, with the toolchain that the tag's `rust-toolchain.toml` names installed beforehand. `bexpand-1.2.0.crate` extracts into `bexpand-1.2.0/` with its `LICENSE`. The script's log shows no license problem. The licenses of the crates of each tool lock file are measured again: the `license` of each package with a source, or its `license_file` when it has none, in `cargo metadata --format-version 1 --locked --all-features --manifest-path` over the extracted `rust-crates-desktop/vendor/tauri-cli-{version}/Cargo.toml` and each `lightningcss-{version}/Cargo.toml`. The pull request records in 2.13 (The crates of the lock files of lightningcss and `tauri-cli`) the crates under MPL-2.0 alone and those outside the list of 2.10. The test fails when a crate is under a copyleft license alone, such as a GPL, LGPL or AGPL license with no permissive alternative. Such a crate then needs the owner's decision. The pull request records the archives' rows of the table of 2.13 (Scope, measured), with their sizes, parts and SHA-256s. The same steps on Windows and macOS are left to whoever can run them (open issue 21).
115. Notices of the native packages. With a table entry `native-ui` 2.0.0 with `crates: []` and `notices`, a lock text holding `native-ui-linux-x64-gnu` 2.0.0 with `cpu: [x64]` and `os: [linux]`, and the fixture's repository root holding `ui/scripts/notices/native/native-ui-2.0.0.txt`, the npm archive that `writeDependencySources` writes for `web` holds `npm-sources-web/` with exactly `native-ui-2.0.0-notices.txt`, byte for byte that file. With the entry named `@scope/native-ui`, a lock text holding `@scope/native-ui-linux-x64-gnu` 2.0.0 and the file `ui/scripts/notices/native/scope-native-ui-2.0.0.txt`, the folder holds exactly `scope-native-ui-2.0.0-notices.txt`. When the file is missing, the call rejects with exactly the native-notices message of section 4 and leaves the output folder empty. Without `notices`, the entry gives no notices file and the npm archive has no folder `npm-sources-web/`. In the repository, every entry of `NATIVE_PACKAGES` with `notices` has its file `ui/scripts/notices/native/{stem}-{version}.txt`, UTF-8 with LF line endings and not empty, whose first line is the entry line of a crate, which starts with `{name} {version}, {license}`. The folder holds no other file. Each file has the layout of 2.13 (The notices of the native packages): its entry lines, read back as entries, are sorted by name and version, each names only texts the file holds, and `renderCrateNotices` of those entries is the file byte for byte, which also holds each text number to one block, the numbers to the order the lines first name them and every text to a line that names it. `renderCrateNotices` of three entries that share a text writes the exact text of the layout. The check reads those entries back from it. A file with a fourth text that no line names fails the check.
116. The notices walk (`ui/scripts/native-notices.mjs`). The test makes a Git repository with a workspace `Cargo.toml`, the root files `COPYRIGHT`, `LICENSE` and `README.md`, the crate `native_ui_binding` with no license file, the crate `helper` with its own `LICENSE`, and the crate `other`, tagged `v2.0.0`, and a registry folder with `shipped` 0.9.0 and 0.10.0 (each with `LICENSE-APACHE` and `LICENSE-MIT`), `slash` 0.2.0 (`MIT/Apache-2.0`, no license file), `bare` 2.0.0 (`MIT`, no license file, the repository `https://github.com/owner/bare.git`) and `dev-only` 1.0.0. A fake `run` answers `cargo metadata` with a graph where the binding reaches `helper` and `shipped` 0.10.0 through normal edges, `slash` through a build edge and `dev-only` through a dev edge only, `helper` reaches `shipped` 0.9.0 and `bare`, and `other` and a crate `unreached` are reached by nothing. `writeNativeNotices` for the entry `@scope/native-ui` 2.0.0 with `notices` `{ crate: 'native_ui_binding', commit }` writes `ui/scripts/notices/native/scope-native-ui-2.0.0.txt`, exactly six entry lines in the order `bare`, `helper`, `native_ui_binding`, `shipped` 0.9.0, `shipped` 0.10.0 and `slash` and eight texts: `bare` from its repository's copies, `helper` with its own text and the tag's tree as URL, `native_ui_binding` with `see texts` and the root's `COPYRIGHT` and `LICENSE` from its repository, both `shipped` with the same two numbers, and `slash` with the standard Apache-2.0 text. `run` received `cargo metadata --format-version 1 --locked --all-features --manifest-path {clone}/Cargo.toml` once, in the repository root, and the clone is gone afterwards. Two runs write the same bytes. `nativeNotices` of a folder holding the same files, not a Git repository, gives the same text. These reject with exactly their message of section 4: a commit in `notices` that the tag does not name (the tag-commit message, and no file is written); a crate of the registry without texts under `ISC` whose repository `https://github.com/owner/missing` has no copies; one that names no repository; the binding when the clone holds no license file at its root; and a crate whose source is `git+https://github.com/owner/git-dep?rev=abc#abc`, before any text is read. `repositoryFolder` gives `github.com/oxc-project/oxc`, `github.com/oxc-project/fast-glob` for a URL ending in `.git`, `github.com/Canop/lazy-regex` for a URL with `/tree/main/src/proc_macros` after the name, and null for `https://example.com/`, `https://example.com/owner`, `not a URL` and null. The command with an argument exits 2 with the usage line of section 4 and prints nothing on standard output.
**Partsmith's source and the source with every artifact (2.13, ADR 0034's amendment of 2026-11-05; test 117 in `ui/scripts/partsmith-source-archive.test.mjs`, 118 to 120 in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`, 121 by CI and by hand)**

Tests 117 to 120 and 122 run in `pnpm test:scripts` with no network and use `bash`, `git`, `gzip` and `tar` from `PATH`. Tests 117, 119 and 120 run every git command they issue, and the scripts and steps under test, with the Git environment of `ui/scripts/isolated-git.mjs` (the note of tests 52 to 59), and make their repositories with `git init --initial-branch=main`. Tests 57 and 117 to 120 take the archive's name and the script's path from `ui/scripts/partsmith-fixture.mjs`. Tests 117 and 120 make the archive of a commit with its one function: `git archive` with the options and folder of 2.13 (Partsmith's source archive, Bytes), run with `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1` and piped to `gzip -9 -n`.

117. The Partsmith archive script (`ui/scripts/partsmith-source-archive.test.mjs`, temporary directories, with the Git environment of `ui/scripts/isolated-git.mjs`). The test makes a fixture repository `<repo>`: a first commit holding `README.md`, `src/main.rs`, `docs/notes.md`, `.gitattributes` (the two lines `* text=auto eol=lf` and `src/main.rs !eol`, the second so that `core.autocrlf` decides the line endings of one file) and a copy of `scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh` at that path, each but the script a short text with LF line endings; a second commit that changes `src/main.rs` and adds `big.bin`, 1,048,576 zero bytes; then an uncommitted change to `README.md` and an untracked file `notes.txt`. `{commit}` is the second commit. The test runs `bash <repo>/scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh <out>` from another folder, with `<out>` an empty folder:
    - it exits 0, prints exactly `Wrote <out>/partsmith-source.tar.gz from commit {commit}: {bytes} bytes, SHA-256 {sha256}.` with the file's size and SHA-256, and `<out>` holds only that file;
    - the file is a gzip file whose header holds no file name and a modification time of 0. `git get-tar-commit-id` prints `{commit}` for its tar. Its entries are the files and folders of `{commit}` under `partsmith-{commit}/`, each file with the bytes of the commit (the committed `README.md`, not the change) and the commit's committer time, and none is `notes.txt`. Its bytes equal those of `git -c core.autocrlf=false -c core.eol=lf -c tar.umask=0002 -c core.attributesFile=/dev/null archive --format=tar --prefix=partsmith-{commit}/ {commit}` in `<repo>`, run with `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1` and piped to `gzip -9 -n`;
    - a second run into another empty folder writes the same bytes. So do a run with `core.autocrlf=true`, one with `core.eol=crlf`, one with `tar.umask=0077` and one with a global attributes file holding `docs/* export-ignore`, each set in the global Git configuration of the test, and a run of the script in a shallow clone of `<repo>` (`git clone --depth 1` from a `file://` URL). Before the four runs with a setting, the test checks that each setting alone changes the bytes of a plain `git archive --format=tar {commit}` in `<repo>`, as test 55 does for OCCT. The fixture then exercises all four;
    - run as `bash scripts/partsmith-source-archive.sh <out>` from `<repo>`, with `CDPATH` set to a folder `<decoy>` that holds a folder `scripts/` of its own, it exits 0 and writes the same bytes;
    - with `PATH` a folder that holds links to `bash`, `dirname`, `git`, `gzip`, `wc`, `tr`, `cut`, `mv` and `rm` and a stub `shasum`, and no `sha256sum`, it exits 0, writes the same bytes and prints the line of the first run. The stub records its arguments and prints the SHA-256 of its standard input followed by two spaces and `-`, as `shasum -a 256` does. It received `-a 256`, once;
    - with a stub `git` first on `PATH` that records the value of `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM` with the arguments of each command and passes every command to the real `git`, it writes the same bytes, and the command whose arguments hold `archive` ran with `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM` set to `1`;
    - with `<out>/partsmith-source.tar.gz` already there, it exits 0 and the file holds the new archive;
