**The apt archives of every apt install (2.4, 2.5, 2.14, 3.2, 3.6, ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-06, choices 7 and 8; tests 130 and 132 to 135 in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`, 131 by CI and by hand)**

Tests 130, 132 and 133 read the workflow files as tests 49 to 51 do. Test 134 runs the copy out with a stub `dpkg-query`. All run in `pnpm test:scripts` with no network.

130. One pattern for every browser install (3.2). In `ci.yml`, `e2e-engines`, with `<browser>` the text `${{ matrix.browser }}`, and `e2e`, with `<browser>` the text `chromium`, each have exactly five steps after the step whose `run` is `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` and before the step that runs the tests (`xvfb-run --auto-servernum pnpm e2e --project ${{ matrix.browser }}` in `e2e-engines`, `pnpm e2e` in `e2e`). They are the five steps of 3.2, in that order, each with exactly these keys and values:
    - `name: Restore the apt archives of <browser>`, `id: apt-archives` and `uses: actions/cache/restore@v6.1.0`, with a `with` of exactly `path: ~/apt-archives`, `key: apt-archives-<runner>-<browser>-${{ github.run_id }}` and `restore-keys: apt-archives-<runner>-<browser>-`, where `<runner>` is the job's `runs-on`, `ubuntu-24.04`;
    - `name: Copy the apt archives into apt's cache`, `if: steps.apt-archives.outputs.cache-matched-key != ''` and `run: sudo cp ~/apt-archives/*.deb /var/cache/apt/archives/`;
    - `name: Install <browser> and its system packages`, `timeout-minutes: 10` and `run: pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps <browser>` (test 79);
    - `name: Copy the apt archives out of apt's cache`, `id: copy-out` and `if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'`, with a block `run` of exactly the twelve lines of 3.2, from `set -o pipefail` to `fi`;
    - `name: Save the apt archives of <browser>`, `if: steps.copy-out.outputs.downloaded == 'true'` and `uses: actions/cache/save@v6.1.0`, with a `with` of exactly `path: ~/apt-archives` and `key: ${{ steps.apt-archives.outputs.cache-primary-key }}`.

    The five steps of `e2e-engines` are the YAML block of 3.2 line for line. Those of `e2e` are the same with each `${{ matrix.browser }}` replaced by `chromium`. Comment lines and blank lines are not compared, as in test 128. No job of `ci.yml`, `release.yml`, `nightly.yml` or `platforms.yml` but `e2e` and `e2e-engines` has a step whose `run` holds `playwright install`. No job but those two, `rust`, `desktop` and `release.yml`'s `bundle` has a line that holds `apt-archives`. No step of any workflow has `id: playwright`. The fourth step's `run` passes `bash -n`. Against `ci.yml` before this change, the test fails: each job has the step `Read the Playwright version` before its restore, and the restore has no `restore-keys`.
131. The first runs with the apt cache of every install, by CI and by hand. Wave H2's first CI run on `main`, CI run 30 (42222611157), made the first saves under wave H2's browser keys, `apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-<browser>-playwright-1.64.0`. Open issue 16 records its result. Below, `<name>` is `chromium`, `firefox`, `webkit` or `tauri` and `<prefix>` is `apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-<name>-` (3.2). The CI run on wave H3's branch, before `main` moves: in `rust` and `desktop`, `Restore the apt archives of the Tauri Linux packages` logs `Cache not found for input keys: <prefix><run id>, <prefix>`, `Copy the apt archives into apt's cache` is skipped, the install passes, and the copy out and the save are skipped. In `e2e` and in both rows of `e2e-engines`, the restore logs the size that run 30 saved for its browser, `Cache Size: ~20 MB (21102075 B)` for `chromium`, `Cache Size: ~47 MB (48803004 B)` for `firefox` and `Cache Size: ~109 MB (113992514 B)` for `webkit`, and then `Cache restored from key: apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-<browser>-playwright-1.64.0`. The copy into apt's cache passes. The install's line `Need to get <part>/<total> of archives.` names a part smaller than its total (`0 B` when Ubuntu updated none of the entry's packages since run 30). The copy out and the save are skipped. Every install passes, and so does every job. The first CI run on `main` with wave H3: in `rust` or `desktop`, `Copy the apt archives out of apt's cache` passes and `Save the apt archives of the Tauri Linux packages` logs `Cache saved with key: apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-tauri-<run id>` after its `Sent <sent> of <size> (<percent>%), <rate> MBs/sec` lines. `<size>`, the size of the entry in bytes, is within 10 percent of the size in the install's `Need to get` line (about 61 MB, where apt's MB is 1,000,000 bytes). The other job's save logs `Failed to save: Unable to reserve cache with key apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-tauri-<run id>, another job may be creating this cache.` and passes, or, when its restore already found that entry by its exact key, its copy out writes no output and its save is skipped. A browser job restores its wave H2 entry as on the branch and saves only when its copy out writes `downloaded=true`: when Ubuntu updated one of its packages since run 30. The runner image then keeps apt's downloads in `/var/cache/apt/archives`, and the runner's user can read them. The next CI run, on `main` or on a pull request: in `rust` and `desktop` the restore logs `Cache Size: ~<n> MB (<size> B)` with the `<size>` of the save and then `Cache restored from key: apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-tauri-<run id of the save>`. `Copy the apt archives into apt's cache` passes. The install's line `Need to get <part>/<total> of archives.` names a part smaller than its total (`0 B` when Ubuntu updated none of the entry's packages since the save). apt then takes the files copied with `sudo` as its own after `apt-get update`. Each `Get:` line after the `Need to get` line names a package whose `.deb` the entry lacks or holds at another version. On `main`, a copy out after an install with no such `Get:` line writes no output, and the save is skipped. The first run on `main` whose install downloads an update on a hit, in any of the four sets: the copy out writes `downloaded=true`, the save logs `Cache saved with key: <prefix><run id>`, and the next run restores that key. The save's `<size>` is at least 90 percent of the `<total>` of that install's `Need to get <part>/<total>` line: the entry holds every archive the install took. It is larger than `<total>` only by the files it carried forward of packages that the runner image already holds at those versions (3.2, The copy out). It is smaller than the restored entry's `<size>` plus `<part>` by about the size of the versions that the downloaded packages replaced: the entry left those out. The first release dry run with wave H3: its Linux row's restore logs `Cache restored from key: apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-tauri-<run id>` with the run of CI's newest save on `main`, the copy passes, the install's `Need to get` line names a part smaller than its total, and `Install Ninja` is skipped when the OCCT cache hit. Open issue 16 records, for each set, the entry's `<size>`, the `Need to get` lines of the miss and of the first hit and each install's time, and each later save with its run and the packages its install's `Get:` lines named. A save that warns `Path Validation Error`, a hit whose part equals its total, a copy step that fails, a save after an install that downloaded nothing, an entry saved after a miss whose `<size>` is more than 10 percent off its install's total, an entry saved after a hit whose `<size>` is under 90 percent of its install's total, or an entry that kept a version an update replaced is a defect of this change. It is recorded in open issue 16 and fixed in the workflow. By hand, once, in a container of the runner's Ubuntu release with its apt (Ubuntu 24.04.5, apt 2.8.3), with `Dir::Cache` and `Dir::State::Lists` in a new folder: `apt-get install --download-only` of a package downloads its `.deb`; run again, it prints `Need to get 0 B/<size> of archives.`, also after the file was copied out and back with `cp` as `root`; with the file cut short, it downloads it again; with one byte changed and the size kept, it prints `Need to get 0 B/<size> of archives.` (apt does not hash it); and with the file renamed to another version, it downloads the version the lists name and leaves the renamed file. `apt-get install --reinstall --download-only libxdo-dev libxdo3` writes `libxdo-dev_1%3a3.20160805.1-5build1_amd64.deb` and `libxdo3_1%3a3.20160805.1-5build1_amd64.deb`, the names that `dpkg-query -W -f '${Package}_${Version}_${Architecture}.deb\n'` with each colon written `%3a` gives for the two installed packages. Open issue 16 records those runs of 2026-11-06. Only CI can make the rest of this check: this repository's container is Docker's Ubuntu, whose `docker-clean` removes apt's downloads, and has Chromium alone.
132. The Tauri installs of `rust` and `desktop` keep their packages in the apt cache (3.2). In `ci.yml`, `rust` and `desktop` each have exactly one step whose `run` is `sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libxdo-dev libssl-dev librsvg2-dev`. It is named `Install the Tauri Linux packages` and sets no `timeout-minutes`, no `if` and no `continue-on-error`. The two steps right before it and the two right after it are those of `e2e-engines` around its install (test 130), with each `${{ matrix.browser }}` in the restore's `key` and `restore-keys` replaced by `tauri` and in the names of the restore and the save replaced by `the Tauri Linux packages`, comment and blank lines aside: `Restore the apt archives of the Tauri Linux packages` (with `key: apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-tauri-${{ github.run_id }}` and `restore-keys: apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-tauri-`), `Copy the apt archives into apt's cache`, then `Copy the apt archives out of apt's cache` and `Save the apt archives of the Tauri Linux packages`. `ubuntu-24.04` in the keys is each job's `runs-on`. In `rust` the five steps come right after the step named `Free disk space` and right before the step whose `run` is `rustup show active-toolchain || rustup toolchain install`. In `desktop` they come right after the step that restores `.occt/native` with `actions/cache/restore@v6.1.0` and right before that toolchain step. The five steps of the two jobs are the same, line for line, comment and blank lines aside (as in test 130). Against `ci.yml` before this change, the test fails: neither job has a step before its install that restores the apt archives.
133. The Linux release bundle restores CI's Tauri entry and installs Ninja only for an OCCT build (2.5, 3.2, 3.6). In `release.yml`'s `bundle`, the three steps right after checkout are `Restore the apt archives of the Tauri Linux packages`, `Copy the apt archives into apt's cache` and `Install the Tauri Linux packages`. Each has the keys and values of the step of the same name in `rust` (test 132), with an `if` added or extended: `runner.os == 'Linux'` for the restore and the install, and `runner.os == 'Linux' && steps.apt-archives.outputs.cache-matched-key != ''` for the copy. No step of `bundle` is named `Copy the apt archives out of apt's cache` or `Save the apt archives of the Tauri Linux packages`, has `id: copy-out` or uses `actions/cache/save`. The step `Install Ninja` comes right after the step with `id: occt` and right before the step whose `run` is `scripts/build-occt.sh native "$OCCT_ROOT"`, with `if: runner.os == 'Linux' && steps.occt.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'` and `run: sudo apt-get install -y ninja-build`. These two install steps are the only steps of `bundle` whose `run` holds `apt-get`, and `Install Ninja` is the only one whose `run` holds `ninja-build`. The `runner` of the matrix row with `bundles: deb,rpm` is `ubuntu-24.04`, the runner the keys name (test 81). Against `release.yml` before this change, the test fails: its Linux install step is `Install the Tauri Linux packages and Ninja`, with `ninja-build` in its command and no restore before it.
134. The copy out keeps the installed versions and says when the install downloaded one (3.2). The test writes the `run` of `e2e-engines`' step `Copy the apt archives out of apt's cache` to a file with each `/var/cache/apt/archives` replaced by a temporary folder (apt's cache). It runs the file with `bash -e`, as GitHub runs a step with no `shell` (`bash -e {0}`), with `HOME`, `RUNNER_TEMP` and `GITHUB_OUTPUT` in a temporary folder and a stub `dpkg-query` first on `PATH`. The stub appends its arguments to a file and prints three lines `<package>_<version>_<architecture>.deb`, one of them with an epoch (`libxdo3_1:3.20160805.1-5build1_amd64.deb`), out of byte order: a script that does not sort them fails at `comm`. In one case below it prints a fourth line. In each of the first six cases the stub exits 0 and the script exits with status 0:
    - a miss: apt's cache holds the `.deb` file of each listed package version, the epoch's colon written `%3a`, a file of another version of one of them, a file `lock` and a folder `partial/`, and `~/apt-archives` does not exist. `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` then holds exactly the line `downloaded=true`, and `~/apt-archives` holds exactly the three files of the listed versions, each with the bytes of apt's cache;
    - a hit with nothing new: `~/apt-archives` and apt's cache hold the same three files. `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` stays empty, and `~/apt-archives` keeps its three files;
    - a hit after an update: `~/apt-archives` holds one listed package at another version and the other two at the listed versions, and apt's cache holds those three files and the listed version of the first package. `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` holds `downloaded=true`, and `~/apt-archives` holds the three listed versions alone;
    - a hit after the same update whose entry also holds the file of a package that the runner image holds at that version, which apt skipped: the stub prints that version as a fourth line, and apt's cache holds the file as the copy into it left it. `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` holds `downloaded=true`, and `~/apt-archives` holds that file with the three listed versions (3.2, The copy out);
    - nothing downloaded and no entry: apt's cache holds no `.deb` file. `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` stays empty, and `~/apt-archives` is an empty folder;
    - a hit whose entry holds only a version that nothing installed: apt's cache holds that file alone. `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` stays empty;
    - a failing `dpkg-query`: apt's cache is that of the miss, `~/apt-archives` does not exist, and the stub prints nothing and exits 1. The script exits with a status other than 0, `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` stays empty, and `~/apt-archives` is not made.

    In each case the stub was called once, with exactly the three arguments `-W`, `-f` and `${Package}_${Version}_${Architecture}.deb\n` (a backslash and an `n` at the end). No line of the script holds `sudo`. Against `ci.yml` before this change, the test fails: the step writes no output and copies every `.deb` file of apt's cache.
135. The workflows AGENTS.md gives each failed install its rerun (3.2, 2.13). For each sentence of `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` that 3.2 gives as `"<old>" becomes "<new>"`, the file holds `<new>` and not `<old>`. In its promotion rule's exception, `12 hours` comes once, inside the parentheses that start `(for` and name `e2e` and `e2e-engines`, the jobs that upload OCCT's compiled code (2.13, Kept a day longer). Those parentheses name neither `rust` nor `desktop`. Against the exception of wave H3's first change, which gave the 12 hours to the Tauri installs of `rust` and `desktop` too, the test fails.

## 7. Open issues

1. **OCCT, the kernel and the engine on Windows and macOS.** `scripts/build-occt.sh`, `partsmith-occt-sys`, the engine and the desktop host have been built and tested only on Linux (and the first two for Emscripten). CI's `occt-platforms` and `rust-platforms` (2.14), jobs of the platforms workflow until wave H, build and test them with MSVC on `windows-2025` and with Apple Clang on `macos-15`, with the setup of the release's `bundle` job; until a run passes, its findings are recorded here, each with the job, the test and what it found. The first run (40315773128, scheduled, 2026-10-26, commit 2a202ac) built OCCT on both runners and passed the macOS crate tests and clippy; its findings are in (b), (d) and (g). The second run (40478176115, dispatched on `main`, 2026-10-27, commit 5153d61) passed the crate tests and clippy on both runners, the `partsmith-occt-sys` tests on Windows for the first time, and failed only in the examples runner, on the thin walls of the cone examples (d). Reading the code points at six places, (a) to (f), a first run may stop or go wrong, each fixed where it shows and not before, the more so where the fix changes `scripts/build-occt.sh`, as for (a) and the first option of (e), since that makes every OCCT cache, CI's included, build again (3.2): (a) on Windows, `OCCT_ROOT` reaches CMake as the install prefix with the backslashes of `${{ github.workspace }}` (`D:\a\...`), which CMake can read as escapes where it writes the prefix into a script it reads later, and `cygpath -m "$prefix"` in `build-occt.sh` gives the form with forward slashes; (b) OCCT compiles its static toolkits on Windows with `-DOCCT_STATIC_BUILD` (`adm/cmake/occt_toolkit.cmake` line 43), while `partsmith-occt-sys/build.rs` compiles the shim without it, so for the shim `Standard_Macro.hxx` (line 252) makes `Standard_EXPORT` `__declspec(dllexport)` and `Standard_IMPORT` `__declspec(dllimport)`. That gives link warnings LNK4217 and LNK4286 and exported symbols in every binary, and may not fail a run at all, so the first Windows log is checked for those two warnings. The fix is `build.define("OCCT_STATIC_BUILD", None)` for every target: `Standard_Macro.hxx` is the only header that reads the macro, so the define does nothing outside Windows. It is not a cache change. Resolved (kernel.md 3.12 item 5): `build.rs` defines `OCCT_STATIC_BUILD`. The first Windows log (job 121568157546) showed no LNK4217 or LNK4286, but `Creating library ... .lib and object ... .exp` for each of the eight executables it linked (the seven test executables and the desktop binary), the sign of exported symbols; the next run checks that the line is gone for the `partsmith-occt-sys` and `partsmith-engine` executables, and any that remain name another exporter. The second run's Windows log (job 122101627964) has no `Creating library` line and no LNK4217 or LNK4286 warning, neither for the same eight executables, linked with the same Rust 1.97.0 whose `linker_messages` warning printed those lines in the first run, nor for the CLI's that the examples runner links, so (b) is closed; (c) the system libraries `build.rs` links (`advapi32`, `gdi32`, `user32`, `wsock32` and `psapi` on Windows, none beyond the C++ library on macOS) hold only while no OCCT object that calls AppKit or IOKit (in `TKService`, which `TKV3d` and the STEP toolkits pull in) is linked, which the linker reports by name if it is. Both runs linked every executable they built on both runners with these libraries; (d) the examples runner compares the rebuilds of MSVC and of Apple Clang, which contracts floating-point operations into fused ones on arm64, with expected files blessed on Linux, and a result outside the tolerance of 2.8 is recorded here with the example and its numbers, for a decision between a kernel fix and the tolerance rule, never per-platform expected files. The first run (job 121568157520, `macos-15`) found seven examples outside it, in 96 lines, all under `/checks/` (every volume, area, bounding box and count matched): `bench/fifty-features`, whose body 0 had 3 open edges at z = 28 on face 50 (`1 error, 29 warnings` instead of `29 warnings`) and whose body 1 had its overhangs in another order; `cone/pointed` and `cone/apex-below-tolerance`, 5 warnings instead of 4; `cone/frustum` and `cone/inverted`, their thin walls in another order with values up to 0.6 percent off; and `chamfer/pipe-tee` and `fillet/pipe-tee`, their two mirror overhangs swapped. Two causes: OCCT's Delabella triangulates in `long double`, whose precision is the compiler's (a 64-bit mantissa with GCC on x86_64, 53 bits on arm64 macOS and with MSVC), so the diagonals of cocircular nodes came from rounding, and at 53 bits it lost triangles and crashed on revolved faces in a Linux reproduction; and print regions were ordered by exact area, which mirror images share only up to the last bits. The fixes are kernel.md 3.7 item 2 (the shim's own copy of Delabella in double-double arithmetic, ADR 0039) and print.md 3.7 item 7 (areas the comparison of 2.8 cannot tell apart are ordered by their triangles). The cause of the holes and lost triangles was `long double` precision, not FMA alone; pipe-tee's swap needed neither. What remains, mesh nodes that Apple Clang's fused operations and Apple's libm can move in the last bits, is kernel.md open issue 14: if a macOS run still differs once kernel.md test 137 passes there, the answer is chosen there (section 8). The second run (jobs 122101627953 and 122101627964) passed kernel.md tests 137 and 138 on both runners, and `bench/fifty-features`, both pipe-tees, the four revolve examples whose triangulation crashed at 53 bits in ADR 0039's reproduction, and `move_body/move-then-fillet` matched; only the thin walls of the four cone examples differed. On `macos-15`: `cone/pointed` and `cone/apex-below-tolerance` 5 warnings instead of 4 (`checks/5/findings/2` other, `findings/4` unexpected), `cone/frustum` two of its five regions other, `cone/inverted` 9 warnings instead of 8 (`findings/0`, `/1` and `/3` other, `findings/6` unexpected). On `windows-2025` one line: `cone/inverted`'s `checks/5/findings/1/value` 0.006294717528121916 against 0.006299536308159865. Cause: thin walls measured rays into the acute edge where a cone's flat end meets its side, so a finding was where a centroid fell, and the ends' 144 rim points lie on one circle, so their last bits picked the diagonals: on Windows the C runtime's `cos` (raising glibc's cosine of one rim node by one ulp gives the Windows line bit for bit, kernel.md open issue 14), on macOS not attributed and not needed. Fix: print.md 3.9.6 item 2 (ADR 0041), a hit counts only on a face within 30° of opposite, with the four cone expected files blessed again on Linux, since the spec changed their results, and `shell/thin-wall` added so the platforms compare a real thin wall; no expected file is per platform; (e) on Windows, before wave F, two C runtimes met in one binary: tauri-build linked every `partsmith-desktop` target, its tests included, with the static Visual C++ runtime (`libcmt` and `libvcruntime`, with `/NODEFAULTLIB:msvcrt.lib`), because Tauri's `build.windows.staticVCRuntime` is true by default (tauri-utils 2.10.1), while OCCT (CMake's default `/MD`; `build-occt.sh` set no runtime) and the shim (`cc`'s default `/MD`, with no `crt-static`) used the DLL runtime. The link was then expected to warn LNK4217 and LNK4286, and the app still imported `MSVCP140.dll` and `VCRUNTIME140.dll`, which the `windows-2025` image has installed with the Visual C++ Redistributable, so no runner showed what a user without it gets; desktop.md 6 checks the installed app in Windows Sandbox for that. The two options were the static runtime everywhere (on Windows, `build-occt.sh` passes `-DCMAKE_MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY=MultiThreaded -DCMAKE_POLICY_DEFAULT_CMP0091=NEW`, which works with the CMake 3.10 OCCT requires, and `.cargo/config.toml` sets `-C target-feature=+crt-static` for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, so the shim and the Rust code follow; this changes every OCCT cache key) and the DLL runtime (`staticVCRuntime: false` in `tauri.conf.json`, with the redistributable shipped by the installer); a check of the built executable's imports (`dumpbin /dependents`) joins `rust-platforms` with the choice, since what it must show depends on it. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: the static runtime everywhere (ADR 0038 option (i)). Wave F's windows-crt track builds it as 3.6 (The C runtime on Windows) says, with the check of the imports in `rust-platforms` and in the Windows `bundle` job (tests 99 to 103). The first platforms run after it builds the Windows OCCT cold, about 48 minutes, and is the first to run the check. (e) stays open until that run and the next release dry run pass (test 102). Platforms run 14 (41926898084, on 0ae7337, 2026-11-04) was that run: `occt-platforms` found no Windows OCCT under the new key, built it in 1 h 10 min 21 s and saved it. `rust-platforms` passed on `windows-2025`, its step `Check the imports of the app` listing 28 DLLs for the debug app, ten of them `api-ms-win-crt` DLLs and none of the Visual C++ Redistributable (test 123 holds the list). No release dry run has run yet. Since the owner's decision of 2026-11-05, every Windows program takes the Universal C Runtime from Windows (3.6). Test 127 adds the CLI's check and the zlib installer to what the next runs show. The first runs after it, on 2026-11-06, were CI run 28 (42156836018, the push of a3df7f5 to `main`) and platforms run 15 (42156838426, on a3df7f5). Both passed. In CI run 28, `occt-native` and `occt-wasm` restored their caches and built nothing. In run 15, `occt-platforms` restored the Windows and macOS OCCT and built nothing. `rust-platforms` passed on `windows-2025` with MSVC 14.51.36231 (Visual Studio 2026 Enterprise) and the Windows SDK 10.0.26100.0. Its steps `Check the imports of the app` and `Check the imports of the CLI` passed. The listing of `target/debug/partsmith-desktop.exe` named the same 28 DLLs as run 14's listing (test 123), ten of them `api-ms-win-crt` DLLs. Only the order of the fifth to ninth differed: `api-ms-win-core-synch-l1-2-0.dll`, `user32.dll`, `ole32.dll`, `shlwapi.dll` and `comctl32.dll`. The listing of `target/debug/partsmith.exe` named 17: `kernel32.dll`, `bcryptprimitives.dll`, `api-ms-win-core-synch-l1-2-0.dll`, `ADVAPI32.dll`, `USER32.dll`, `WSOCK32.dll`, `ntdll.dll` and the same ten `api-ms-win-crt` DLLs. Neither listing named `VCRUNTIME`, `MSVCP`, `ucrtbase` or another DLL of the Visual C++ Redistributable. (e) stays open for the owner's release dry run: the Windows `bundle` job's check of `target/release/partsmith-desktop.exe`, the first setup executable compressed with zlib and the 7-Zip check by hand of open issue 10 all wait for it; (f) the examples runner rebuilds through the `partsmith` binary, whose engine work runs on its main thread, and a Windows main thread has 1 MiB of stack (MSVC's default `/STACK`) against 8 MiB on Linux and macOS and 2 MiB on the threads of Rust's test harness; deep OCCT calls need far more than a small stack, as the web core's 4 MB shows (`crates/partsmith-wasm/build.rs`), so a rebuild that fits elsewhere can exit with `0xC00000FD` (stack overflow) on Windows, and the fix, when a run shows it, is to run the CLI's work on a thread made with `std::thread::Builder::new().stack_size(8 << 20)`. The second run rebuilt every example on Windows with no stack overflow; (g) the first run's Windows tests hung (job 121568157546). Cargo ran `partsmith-desktop`'s tests first (6, 0 and 107, all passed) and `partsmith-engine`'s lib tests (251 passed); in the engine's integration binary, t107 (a too large fillet) FAILED at 12:24:34, t093 (unreadable STEP data) passed right after, and t149 (which opens a document with a fillet), t092, t105 and t112 (STEP imports) had been running for over 60 seconds at 12:25:34; nothing more was printed until the job's 60 minutes canceled it at 13:20:44. t107's failure text never printed, since libtest prints failures when its binary ends, which it never did. The cause, read from the code: cc 1.7.0 and cxx-build 1.0.205 compile the shim without `/EHsc`, so cl.exe destroys no automatic object in the frames an exception leaves, and a failed fillet left `blend_mutex` locked and an unreadable STEP file `exchange_mutex` (kernel.md 3.10); the same thread's next lock then failed (MSVC's `std::mutex` throws "resource deadlock would occur" when its owner locks it again, inferred, since the text never printed) and the other threads blocked forever. The fixes are kernel.md 3.9 item 9 and 3.12 item 5 (`/EHsc`, and a build error without C++ unwinding), kernel.md test 136, and the 20-minute timeout of the `crates` step (2.14), so a hang ends that step and the examples runner and clippy still report. The `partsmith-occt-sys` tests, the examples runner and clippy did not run on Windows in the first run. The second run's crate tests ended in 1 min 57 s with every test passing (`partsmith-occt-sys` lib 134 and integration 424, kernel.md test 136 among them, `partsmith-engine` lib 251 and integration 428, t107 among them, and the desktop's 107, 6 and 0), and the examples runner and clippy ran after them, so (g) is closed. Known residual: the `cxx` crate's own `cxx.cc` is compiled by cxx's build script without `/EHsc`; it takes no locks. Already handled: the Windows atomic replace and the macOS socket paths of the desktop tests (desktop.md 3.4 step 4.5, tests 56 and 59), the Windows application manifest without which no desktop test binary could start on Windows (desktop.md 2.2, test 71), and clippy for the code that only Windows or macOS compiles (`rust-platforms`, 2.14). Confirmed by the second run: (b), the hang of (g) and the two causes of (d) of the first run. Fixed since and left for the next run to confirm: the cone thin walls of (d).
2. **Core size.** The linked core is 19,710,890 bytes (18.8 MiB) on 2026-10-23, about 6.2 MiB under the limit (17.2 MiB on 2026-10-12, 3.5), so splitting it into lazily loaded modules is not needed now. If it grows past the limit, the `web` job fails, ADR 0017 is revisited, and the plan of 3.5 starts with an ADR; R2 (step 3) is a fallback only, for a file the first two steps cannot bring under the limit.
3. **Local OCCT installs.** A local OCCT WebAssembly install built before the `OCC_CONVERT_SIGNALS` fix of ADR 0003 makes optimized links fail in `wasm-opt` (in `ShapeUpgrade_ShapeDivide::Perform`). CI's cache key includes the script hash, so CI rebuilds; developers must rebuild local installs after the script changes. A local Windows install built before the static runtime (3.6, The C runtime on Windows) is rebuilt from an empty build folder: delete `$OCCT_WORK_DIR/build-native` (by default `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/occt-7.9.3/build-native`), then run `scripts/build-occt.sh native <prefix>`. Its `CMakeCache.txt` keeps the `/MD` of the first configure.
4. **Examples and debug builds.** The runner uses the debug CLI binary inside `cargo test --workspace`. If the corpus grows past the 3-minute budget, it moves to its own job with `--release`. Runner test 5 adds four `partsmith export` runs, each a rebuild, for every example that builds: on 2026-10-28, on 4 cores with the debug binary, the `examples::` module took 24 s before it, exporting all 256 examples to the four formats took 41 s on its own, and the module took 55 s with runner test 5 in it. Runner tests 6 to 9 read files and run no binary. On 2026-11-04, on the same machine with 294 examples, 270 of which build, the module took 33.8 to 34.2 s in six runs after the build before 2.8's STEP round trip, and runner test 5 alone 19.8 and 21 s in two. The imports and measures that the round trip adds took 7.7 s in one session in the test binary, built with the dev profile as the runner is, 1.4 s of it for the benchmark model. They took 7.3 s over four sessions on four threads after the exports, whose STEP reads take turns under the kernel's mutex (kernel.md 3.10 item 4). Two scratch versions of runner test 5 measured where the imports run: with each worker importing right after its export, the module took 35.3 to 36.9 s in four runs, and with one session after the exports 41.9 and 43.7 s in two. Runner test 5 imports in each worker. As built, the module took 35.8 to 37.0 s in nine runs here, 2.5 s more than before on average, and runner test 5 alone 22.9 to 23.7 s in five.
5. **Import examples** (resolved by 2.8 and features/import.md tests 9 and 11). `import/new-body`, `import/join` and `import/cut-through-plate` reference the placeholder asset name of features/import.md (64 zeros), whose bytes its tests write at run time, so through the CLI they rebuild with `asset_missing` errors, recorded in their expected results. `import/step-block`, `import/join-step-block`, `import/cut-step-block` and `import/intersect-step-block` import a real STEP file, the 10 x 20 x 30 mm block in `examples/import/assets/` (fixture `S` of features/import.md), and record the results of its tests 1, 2, 7 and 9 (volumes 6000, 6750, 14000 and 250), which its tests 9 and 11 check on the same documents and asset bytes, test 11 also checking the asset's name against its hash; `sketch/imported-face-ambiguous` imports a 10 mm cube from `examples/sketch/assets/`, written by the engine's STEP export. No example with an `import` feature has a Python twin (2.8).
6. **Source maps.** `vite.config.ts` emits source maps, which deploy with the app (about 1 MB today). That is fine for an open-source app and helps bug reports; dropping them from production is a one-line change if wanted.
7. **features.md and cli.md open issues 3.** Both point at the examples layout, which section 2.8 now defines; their text can be updated to point here when those specs are next edited.
8. **Initial JavaScript headroom.** The app was one chunk, and its initial JavaScript grew to 96.8 percent of the 250 KB limit on 2026-10-16 (242,039 bytes gzipped; 94.1 percent on 2026-10-13, 2.6) and past it on 2026-10-23 (252,096 bytes, with phase 4 of the reference stability work). Sketch mode's overlay then became a chunk of its own, loaded with a dynamic `import()` that the shell's first render starts (ui.md 3.1; 48,341 bytes, 16,015 gzipped), which brought the initial JavaScript to 238,669 bytes gzipped, 95.5 percent of the limit and 11,331 under it (`check:dist` on a build of the tree, 2026-10-23). Declaring the drawing tools in `src/sketch/tools.ts` then took `src/sketch/drawing.ts` and `src/sketch/inference.ts` out of the main chunk and into that one, which brought it to 234,595 bytes gzipped, 93.8 percent of the limit (the phase 4 cleanup pass, 2026-10-23). When more is needed, the next candidates are the Print context and export, each with the loading state it needs specified in ui.md. Raising the limit changes the budget of ui.md 5 and needs human review.
9. **One integration test binary per crate** (resolved by 3.2 and ADR 0035). Each file of a crate's `tests/` was its own binary: 177 binaries (174 without the `stability-study` feature), 105 of them linking OCCT, 7.5 GB of integration test executables. 3.2 and ADR 0035 gathered them into one binary per crate, beside the targets of their own that 3.2 names: `tests/integration.rs` declares the files as modules; they stay where they were, so every path that names a test file still holds. A test's id starts with its file's name (`examples::examples_rebuild_as_expected`), and `cargo test -p <crate> --test integration <file>::` runs the tests of one file (and of any file whose name ends in `_<file>`, such as `error_messages.rs` for `messages::`), except the files of the separate targets, which select nothing there: `budgets.rs` runs with `--test budgets` (in `partsmith-recompute`, `--test budgets feature_box::` runs one feature's budgets). The reference stability corpus, first a target of its own behind the `stability-study` feature, joined `partsmith-recompute`'s `integration` when every scenario passed (recompute.md test 69), so a third runs with `--test integration reference_stability_N::`. Tests of different files of a crate now share one process, so their OCCT calls overlap on the test threads. The integration test executables went from 7.5 to 1.03 GB (18 binaries, 17 without the feature, and 17 since the corpus joined `integration`). Linking those of `partsmith-recompute` (`integration` and `budgets`) from scratch takes 7.8 to 8.0 s instead of 21.5 to 26.3 s, and building them in release about 2 minutes instead of about 21. The locks of kernel.md 3.10 cover the overlap: 495 debug runs of the merged binaries that link OCCT, 278 of them at 8 or 16 threads, passed (2026-10-22). In release, `partsmith-recompute`'s 22 budgets are its test target `budgets` (`tests/budgets.rs`, a module per feature file), as `partsmith-features`' are, so they are never timed beside the tests of the other files, where `feature_shell::t012_performance` missed its 250 ms median with twice as many test threads as cores (`RUST_TEST_THREADS=8` on 4 cores). In their own target the budgets still slowed each other at 8 threads (`feature_boolean::t016_join_builds_within_15_ms` missed its 15 ms in 8 of 8 runs), so `median_duration` of `tests/feature_support` times one median at a time; every budget but features/hole.md test 33 (features.md open issue 5) then holds at any thread count on an otherwise idle machine (ADR 0035).
10. **What the notices do not cover** (resolved for the NSIS installer by 2.9 input 6, the owner's strictest reading of 2026-11-04; resolved for NSIS's LZMA module and the compiler's runtime by 2.9 input 6 and desktop.md 2.8, the owner's decisions of 2026-11-05, section 8). The NSIS installer carries NSIS's own code and Tauri's `nsis_tauri_utils` plugin. None of these is a Cargo or npm package. ADR 0034's question 3 asked whether they need entries. The owner answered it with the strictest reading and no lawyer: they do. The desktop notices list NSIS 3.11, `nsis_tauri_utils` 0.5.3 and tauri-bundler 2.10.2's installer script, for Windows only, each with its license texts. The versions follow the `tauri-cli` pin (test 96). Nothing else the bundle formats add is third-party code the notices miss (2.9 input 6). Before the owner's decisions of 2026-11-05, the installer also carried NSIS's LZMA decompressor under CPL-1.0, whose source the notices named only through the NSIS release folder on SourceForge. The static Microsoft C and C++ runtime in the Windows executable had no entry. On 2026-11-05 the owner decided both (section 8): the installer compresses with zlib and leaves the LZMA module out, and the runtime gets a Windows-only entry. Wave G's windows-runtime track builds both: `bundle.windows.nsis.compression` set to `zlib`, with the NSIS entry under `Zlib` and its `COPYING` without the bzip2 and LZMA parts, and the entry `Microsoft Visual C++ runtime (libcmt, libvcruntime and libcpmt), in the Windows app` with Partsmith's text on the Distributable Code terms (2.9 input 6, tests 125 and 126). That text, and the claim that `libcmt.lib`, `libvcruntime.lib` and `libcpmt.lib` are Distributable Code, were written without Microsoft's terms: neither `visualstudio.microsoft.com` nor `learn.microsoft.com` could be reached from the container that wrote them. CPython's `PC/crtlicense.txt`, read on 2026-11-05, names two of the four conditions of the text's second paragraph (terms that protect the code at least as much as Microsoft's own, and Microsoft's trademarks). Before the first tag, a person checks the text against Microsoft's terms and the 7-Zip listing of the first zlib setup executable. This issue records both results (test 127). No AppImage is built. It would bundle WebKitGTK, GTK and their dependencies from the Ubuntu 24.04 build machine, many under the LGPL, and the AppImage runtime, which reportedly links libfuse (LGPL) statically. ADR 0034 set out the choice (drop it from M1, or add its libraries' copyright files and source packages to each release). The owner dropped it on 2026-11-04 (choice 1, desktop.md 3.8). The CLI and the Python wheel need notices of their own when they are distributed (1.2).
11. **No way to the notices inside the app** (resolved by ui.md 3.5 and desktop.md 2.8, ADR 0034's amendment of 2026-11-03, which the owner accepted with option (a) on 2026-11-04). LGPL 2.1 section 6 asks for a prominent notice with each copy. It also asks that a work which shows copyright notices while it runs show OCCT's among them, with a reference to the license. The bundles and the web app carried the file, but nothing in the UI named it. Now the palette command Third-party notices (`app.notices`, under View next to Keyboard and mouse) points at it on both hosts. On the web it opens `/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` of the deployment in a new tab. On the desktop it shows a notification that names where the bundle installed the file: `/usr/lib/Partsmith/` for the `.deb` and `.rpm`, `Partsmith.app/Contents/Resources/` on macOS, and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Partsmith\` on Windows (the folder next to `partsmith-desktop.exe` when the user installed it elsewhere). The UI words that place from the operating system it already detects. The desktop host gains no command, capability or dependency. `bundle.copyright` (desktop.md 2.8) now reads `Copyright the Partsmith contributors. Includes Open CASCADE Technology, copyright OPEN CASCADE SAS, under the GNU LGPL 2.1; see THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt.` The About Partsmith panel of Tauri's default macOS menu shows it, and the Windows executable carries it as its legal copyright. The owner accepted both texts as worded and the notification in place of a host command (option (a) of the amendment). Two more things concern the web tab. Under the deployment's policy Chromium 141's text view does not wrap the long lines. CI's Chromium 156 reports no violation there and wraps them. ui.md open issue 44 is resolved with option (iii), which the owner chose on 2026-11-04: the generator wraps the paragraphs Partsmith writes itself (2.9, test 84). `pnpm preview` sends the file as `text/plain` without a charset, and Chromium then reads it as windows-1252 and garbles its 27 lines with characters outside ASCII (measured 2026-11-03). Pages is expected to send `text/plain; charset=utf-8` for text files. A request to a deployment confirms that, a human check of open issue 19 (this container cannot reach one). E-164 reads only ASCII lines.
12. **The web notices name no commit** (resolved by 2.13). One committed file serves every deployment, production and previews alike, so the web statement cannot name the commit itself; it names `/SOURCE.txt`, the stamp the `web` job writes into each deployment, previews included, and `deploy` requires (ADR 0034 option A, item 4). Deployments made before the stamp landed have none.
13. **Two bundle builds per release.** The checklist (3.6) runs a dry run before the tag, and the tag run builds every bundle again from the same commit, so each release runs the Windows and macOS jobs twice (section 8). The tag run could take the dry run's `bundle-*` artifacts of that commit instead (`actions/download-artifact` with the dry run's `run-id`), which halves the cost; that needs `verify` to find the dry run and a change to 2.5, and is left for the implementer of `release.yml` to propose with the first measured figures.
14. **`sketch/face-ambiguous` shows a lost face.** Since phase 1 of [the reference stability study](../design/reference-stability-and-errors.md), the resolver never takes a face by its fingerprint alone, and a face reference is ambiguous only between imported faces, whose fallback names are confirmed by their shape (naming.md section 3.9, step 1). The stored face of `sketch/face-ambiguous`, `{q: f-box1/nothing, fp: plane|a=100}`, is therefore missing, and its expected results equal those of `sketch/face-lost`. `sketch/imported-face-ambiguous` shows the ambiguous case of `plane_fallback` (features/sketch.md section 3.5) on an imported cube instead. `sketch/face-ambiguous` is to be removed, or renamed after what it shows (a lost face whose stored size fits every face of the box stays lost and is never replaced by a lookalike); either way its three files go. That waited for the review of section 8, where the owner accepted the examples as built on 2026-11-04. The removal or rename stays with this issue.
15. **The web's OCCT source** (resolved: for the deployments by 2.13, the owner's decision of 2026-11-04, and for CI's artifacts by 2.13's The source with every artifact, the owner's decision of 2026-11-05, option (b) widened). ADR 0034 reads sections 4 and 6(d) as asking for OCCT's source from the place that distributes the build, and its question 1 for a lawyer assumes a link to the upstream tag is not enough. Under that reading the web fell short: production, every preview and CI's public artifacts carried OCCT's object code, while a deployment's commit reached OCCT's source only through `scripts/occt-source-archive.sh` and `scripts/build-occt.sh`, which clone the upstream tag. On 2026-11-04 the owner chose to write the archive into every deployment (17.5 MB, under the 25 MiB limit of 3.5), over a release carrying each pin's archive before `main` deploys that pin and over the upstream tag if a lawyer allowed it. Later that day the owner chose to ask no lawyer and to take the strictest reading of sections 4 and 6 for all four questions of ADR 0034, which answers question 1 the same way: OCCT's source comes from Partsmith's own place, never from the upstream tag alone. 2.13 (The archive in every deployment) builds it: `occt-native` made the archive once per pin into a cache (the `sources` jobs since wave G), every build gets it next to `SOURCE.txt`, and `deploy` refuses a build without the archive of the pinned OCCT commit (tests 85 to 88). Deployments made before that change hold no archive. The first `main` run that made the cache entry, CI run 41926888431 (`0ae7337`, 2026-11-04), cloned the tag with git 2.56.0. `web` printed `bb71bf7473f9a278648f3ff96e28bd50afe49bf8d6e53ab55e883b54d0220155`, the SHA-256 of 2.13: git 2.56.0 and gzip 1.12 wrote the bytes that git 2.43.0 wrote here. No pull request has run CI yet. The first one that does records here the SHA-256 that its `sources` job prints. Test 88's request to the first deployments that hold the archive is still a check at the gate, which only CI and a person can make. CI run 41926888431 made the first production deployment with the archive (https://6db57554.partsmith.pages.dev). On 2026-11-06 CI run 28 (42156836018) deployed a3df7f5 to production (https://4d377c5e.partsmith.pages.dev), the first deployment with the OCCT, dependency and Partsmith source archives together (2.13). Its `deploy` passed `Check the OCCT source archive` and its other three checks. Each deployment's own address keeps serving its commit after production moves on. Test 88's requests have not run on either. When it finds the archive sent with a `content-encoding`, this issue records it with the `_headers` rule that test 88 names, which needs human review. CI's artifacts other than `web-dist` carried OCCT's object code without its source: `core-wasm` and the `playwright-report` of a failed `e2e` run in `ci.yml`, `core-wasm` and `playwright-report-{browser}` in `platforms.yml`, and the `bundle-*` artifacts of release runs, dry runs included, each kept 7 days and open to any signed-in GitHub user (ADR 0034, CI's public artifacts). The owner's options were (a) keep them as they were, (b) upload the archive as an artifact of its own in every run that uploads one of them, and (c) keep them 1 day instead of 7. On 2026-11-05 the owner chose (b), widened with the dependency source archives of the artifact's scope and Partsmith's own source archive, each kept as long as the artifact it goes with (section 8). 2.13 (The source with every artifact) builds it, with the source artifact kept 8 days against the artifacts' 7 by the owner's rule (Kept a day longer). A job `sources` in each workflow uploads `sources-web` or `sources-desktop` before any job of the run uploads an artifact with OCCT's compiled code, and `web` and `draft` reuse it (tests 117 to 121).
16. **What the platform jobs and the platforms workflow cost.** Section 5's 60 minutes of wall time for a warm platforms run and 2.14's runner time, the sum of its job times, were estimates until a warm run measured them (the second run, below). The runs record, here and in section 5, each job's time with its caches cold and warm, the free disk space that `occt-platforms` prints after the OCCT build and `rust-platforms` after the tests on each runner (the macOS arm64 runners have the least, and a cold OCCT build uses the most), the size of each cache the workflow saves (the Windows and macOS OCCT installs and the Rust caches of `rust-platforms`), and every scheduled run that found a Windows or macOS OCCT cache gone (3.2). GitHub keeps at most 10 GB of caches per repository and drops the least recently used beyond it (3.2): CI restores its caches on every push, the Windows and macOS ones among them since wave H. The ones dropped first are `full-rebuild`'s Rust cache and the `bundle` job's Rust caches. The risk is a cold Rust build in `full-rebuild` and in the release's tag runs. A Windows OCCT cache unused for 7 days costs CI one cold build (2.14, The move). If the caches do not fit, `full-rebuild` and `bundle` stop saving their Rust caches (`save-if: false`), which costs each of their runs its dependency build. The first run (40315773128, scheduled, 2026-10-26), cold on Windows and macOS: 1 h 49 min of wall time (6,567 s, 11:32:02 to 13:21:29 UTC), with both OCCT builds and the Windows cancel of open issue 1 (g). `occt-platforms` took 20 min 36 s on `macos-15` (the build step 20 min 20 s; cache 37,615,653 bytes; 42 GiB free after the build) and 48 min 11 s on `windows-2025` (47 min 43 s; 114,537,223 bytes; 220 GB free on `D:`). `occt-wasm` restored CI's cache in 10 s, and `core-wasm` took 4 min 45 s (`build-wasm.sh` 3 min 39 s). `rust-platforms` on `macos-15`, with no Rust cache, took 4 min 51 s: OCCT restored in 4 s, the crate tests 2 min 34 s with a 2 min 19 s build (the 428 engine integration tests ran in 8.06 s, the 413 `partsmith-occt-sys` integration tests in 4.47 s), the examples runner 58 s and clippy 33 s; it saved a Rust cache of 411,301,836 bytes and had 40 GiB free after the tests. On `windows-2025`, also with no Rust cache, OCCT restored in 12 s and the test build took 3 min 23 s before the hang; the Rust cache, 393,296,819 bytes, was saved after the cancel, with 144 GB free on `D:`. `e2e-engines` took 9 min 18 s for `firefox` (the tests 6.1 min, 21 of them at their 30 s timeout; the browser install 2 min 47 s) and 2 min 59 s for `webkit` (the tests 2.0 min). The four new caches hold 956,751,531 bytes of the 10 GB, and no cache was found gone. The second run (40478176115, dispatched on `main`, 2026-10-27, commit 5153d61), the first warm one except part of the Windows Rust cache: 7 min 26 s of wall time (09:05:38 to 09:13:04 UTC) and 18 min 42 s of runner time. `occt-platforms` restored both OCCT caches, in jobs of 15 s on `macos-15` (43 GiB free after the restore) and 17 s on `windows-2025` (147 GB free on `D:`), and `occt-wasm` restored CI's in 10 s; `core-wasm` took 4 min 55 s (`build-wasm.sh` 3 min 48 s). `rust-platforms` took 3 min 33 s on `macos-15` (OCCT restored in 5 s, the Rust cache in 19 s; the crate tests 1 min 28 s with a 1 min 8 s build, the examples runner 53 s with a 34 s build and 18.34 s of tests, clippy 28 s; 40 GiB free after the tests; Xcode 16.4) and 5 min 29 s on `windows-2025` (OCCT in 4 s, the Rust cache in 22 s; the crate tests 1 min 57 s with a 1 min 33 s build, the examples runner 1 min 23 s with a 1 min 2 s build and 21.36 s of tests, clippy 1 min 13 s; 142 GB free on `D:`; MSVC 14.51.36231). `e2e-engines` took 1 min 51 s for `firefox` (the browser install 17 s, the tests 1 min 20 s) and 2 min 12 s for `webkit` (32 s and 1 min 20 s). Every cache key matched, so no cache was saved, and none was found gone. The Windows Rust cache is only partly warm: the first run saved it after its cancel, before the examples runner and clippy had built anything, so in this run they built 47 and 174 crates (35 and 160 of them dependencies, such as `serde_core`, `syn`, `clap`, `schemars` and the Tauri tree), where `macos-15` built only its 12 and 14 workspace crates (34 s and 28 s against 1 min 2 s and 1 min 13 s); rust-cache saves only when its key misses, so each Windows run pays those builds until the key changes (either row's key changes with `Cargo.lock`, a `Cargo.toml`, `.cargo/config.toml`, the toolchain or a runner image with another installed Rust toolchain, and the Windows row's also with another MSVC, whose linker path its key hashes) and the next run on `main` saves a full cache. The wall time does not depend on it: the Windows job ended at 09:11:29, and the run's last job, `e2e-engines` for WebKit, at 09:13:03. Section 5's budget stays 60 minutes until the owner sets another (section 8). The third run (40509561577, dispatched on `main`, 2026-10-27, commit e0f36a0), warm, took 1 h 0 min 22 s of wall time (13:09:20 to 14:09:42 UTC), 22 s over section 5's budget, and 1 h 33 min 18 s of runner time, because `pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps` took 21 min 17 s in the `firefox` row and 53 min 17 s in the `webkit` row (17 s and 32 s in the second run); the `webkit` job took 54 min 54 s of its 60. The two logs show where the time went. `apt-get update` took about 4 s (8.2 and 12.6 MB of package lists at about 10 MB/s), unpacking and setting up the packages 8 s and 10 s, and the browser downloads from `cdn.playwright.dev` 3 s (Firefox 157.0) and 2 s (WebKit 27.2), with FFmpeg under 1 s. The rest was apt downloading the packages from `azure.archive.ubuntu.com`, the first mirror of the runner's apt list: for Firefox, 40 new packages (fonts, and FFmpeg's libraries with their codecs), 48.8 MB to get, in 20 min 59 s, about 39 kB/s; for WebKit, 181 new and 6 upgraded (GStreamer and its plugins, the same fonts and FFmpeg libraries, and the libraries WebKit links), 117 MB in 52 min 57 s, about 37 kB/s. These rates, like the threshold below, use the size of apt's `Need to get` line; its closing `Fetched` line counts fewer bytes, 44.1 MB (35.0 kB/s) and 85.3 MB (26.9 kB/s). In the second run the same mirror sent Firefox's 51.6 MB in 1 s (38.4 MB/s) and WebKit's 117 MB in 8 s (14.1 MB/s). Packages that stalled until apt gave up on them (`Ign`, 1 for Firefox and 7 for WebKit) were fetched again at the end, and the one that stalled twice, `libavfilter9`, came from the list's next mirror, `archive.ubuntu.com`: 4,235 kB in 0.3 s. Both slow rows ran in the Azure region `westus3`, on two runner images (20261025.353.1 and 20261012.334.1), where the second run's rows ran in `eastus2` and `centralus`; the first run's `firefox` install, 2 min 47 s, had fetched its 51.6 MB at 341 kB/s in `westus`. So the cost was the mirror's throughput for that region and day: caching the browsers would have saved 3 s, and a smaller package set would still have come from the same mirror. The change is a bound (2.14, test 79): the install is a step named `Install ${{ matrix.browser }} and its system packages` with `timeout-minutes: 10`. Over the first eight runs (40315773128 to 41018391709), 14 of the 16 installs took 15 s to 2 min 47 s; 10 minutes still passes a mirror of about 200 kB/s for WebKit's 117 MB, and ends one as slow as the third run's after 10 minutes, the row at about 11 and the run at about 16, failed, where the third run's `webkit` row came within 5 minutes of its job's 60. Each such failure is recorded here with the run, the row and the rate its log shows. Its rows are rerun, with `deploy` after them, only while the run is the newest of its concurrency group (2.14). The bound only ends a slow install, and a person reruns the row. Only caching apt's downloaded archives takes the package downloads off the mirror. `apt-get update` still reads the package lists from it. This record proposed it with `actions/cache@v6.1.0` (already pinned, 2.2): the cache would keep the `.deb` files that `--with-deps` downloads, keyed by the browser and the exact Playwright version, copied with `sudo` into `/var/cache/apt/archives` before the install and out of it after. On a hit apt would download only the packages updated since the cache was saved. It would cost a step before and after the install, about 50 MB (Firefox) and 120 MB (WebKit) of the 10 GB of 3.2, and checks a first run must make (that the runner image keeps apt's archives, their permissions, and a stale or partial cache). A second install timeout recorded here was the trigger to add it, with 2.14, 3.2 and test 79 changed with it. CI's `e2e` installed Chromium the same way (2.4) and was not changed then. The trigger came on 2026-11-06. Wave H2 built the cache (the record of the apt cache, below). Only CI could confirm the timeout's effect. The Firefox experiment's six timeouts were the first installs to reach it. The static C runtime on Windows (3.6, Caches) makes every OCCT and Rust cache key new once. The old entries wait 7 days unused next to the new ones. The first CI, platforms and release runs after it record the size of all the repository's caches here. The `full-rebuild` job (2.14, ADR 0054) adds a job on `macos-15` and a Rust cache of release builds. The first platforms runs with it record the job's times and that cache's size here (test 106). One Universal C Runtime (3.6, Caches after one Universal C Runtime) makes every Rust cache key new once more and leaves the OCCT keys as they are. The first platforms run after it records the Windows build's time here, and the first release dry run the size of the setup executable compressed with zlib, which no run has measured with LZMA either (test 127). Platforms run 15 (42156838426, dispatched on `main`, 2026-11-06, a3df7f5) was that platforms run: 9 min 44 s of wall time from dispatch to its last job, with every job and row passing and no rerun. `occt-platforms` restored both OCCT caches and built nothing, and `occt-wasm` restored its cache. `sources` took 1 min 17 s and `core-wasm` 5 min 13 s. `rust-platforms` took 6 min 6 s on `windows-2025` and 4 min 15 s on `macos-15`, where the job first waited 5 min for a runner. `full-rebuild` took 2 min 45 s. `e2e-engines` took 1 min 53 s for `firefox` (the browser install 25 s) and 2 min 16 s for `webkit` (32 s), against 17 s and 32 s for the installs of the second run. CI's `e2e` installed Chromium with no `timeout-minutes` of its own until wave H2 (the job's 60 minutes bound it). In CI run 28 (42156836018, on the same commit) that install took 7 min 42 s, against 19 s in run 41785469932 of 2026-11-04. Its log shows that apt fetched the same 21.1 MB of packages from `azure.archive.ubuntu.com` in 7 min 14 s (48.6 kB/s, in the Azure region `westus2`), where run 41785469932 fetched them in under a second (58.8 MB/s, `eastus`). Open issue 21 (g) records what it did to CI's wall time. This record does not hold the Windows build's own time, the build time and Rust cache size of `full-rebuild` (test 106) or the size of the caches. The setup executable waits for the owner's release dry run. Since wave H, `occt-platforms`, `rust-platforms` and `e2e-engines` are CI's (2.14, The move). CI restores the Windows and macOS OCCT installs and the Rust caches of `rust-platforms` on every run (3.2). The first CI run on `main` after the move records here each of their times, the waits for a macOS runner, each browser install's time and the run's wall time against section 5 (test 129). A browser install that ends at its 10-minute timeout now fails a CI run and skips its `deploy` until the rows are rerun. They are rerun only while the run is the newest of its concurrency group (2.14). Such a run does not count against section 5's budget (2.14, The move, choice 4). A newer push that cancels `rust-platforms` while it builds after a key miss saves a partial Rust cache on `main`, as the first run's Windows row did after its timeout (2.14, The move, choice 1). Each such case is recorded here with the run and the key. `gh cache delete <key>` removes the entry (`gh cache list --key v0-rust-rust-platforms-`). The next passing run on `main` then saves a full one. The next platforms run records its wall time with its two jobs. A runner image with another installed Rust toolchain or another MSVC changes a row's key, as `Cargo.lock`, a `Cargo.toml`, `.cargo/config.toml` and the toolchain do (2.14, The move). The row then builds its dependencies and saves a new cache on `main`. Each such case is recorded here with the run, the row and the old and new keys. The first was on `macos-15`, between platforms runs 7 (40935502508, 2026-10-29, 722e2a1) and 8 (41018391709, 2026-10-30, 9665323). The runner image went from 20260907.0337.1 to 20261028.0489.1. Its own Rust toolchain went from 1.98.1 to 1.99.1 (the pinned 1.97.0 stayed). The key went from `v0-rust-rust-platforms-Darwin-arm64-96dae21d-44cb5354` to `v0-rust-rust-platforms-Darwin-arm64-2943b290-44cb5354`: the environment hash changed and the hash of the files did not. rust-cache logged `No cache found.` in run 8. The row's crate tests took 2 min 35 s with the dependency build (1 min 14 s in run 7). The row saved a new cache of 411,185,158 bytes. The `windows-2025` row's environment hash, `88fbcfa7`, stayed the same in runs 1 to 15, over three runner images. The record of the apt cache (3.2, 2.14, ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-06, choice 7): the Firefox experiment (run 42188760747, 2026-11-06, commit b12d298, a temporary workflow whose jobs set up as `e2e-engines` does) recorded six install timeouts, the trigger above. 6 of its 57 jobs (`force-2`, `force-8` and 4 `cold-start` runners) ended at the 10-minute timeout of `Install firefox and its system packages` while apt stalled downloading the 48.8 MB of packages from `azure.archive.ubuntu.com`. The other 51 installs took 15 to 47 s, at about 46.6 MB/s. With the moved rows in `deploy.needs`, two rows that timed out as often as those jobs would fail about one CI run on `main` in five and hold its deploy. Wave H2 built the cache for the three browser installs of `ci.yml`: `e2e`'s Chromium and the two rows of `e2e-engines`. The Tauri Linux packages of `rust`, `desktop` and `release.yml`'s Linux `bundle` job still came from the same mirror, with no cache and no step timeout of their own. An install of theirs over 10 minutes was to be recorded here, and the second such record was to be the trigger to give them the pattern of 3.2 (the record of the Tauri installs, below). `e2e`'s install also took `e2e-engines`' step name and its 10 minutes. A stalled install then ends at 10 minutes instead of the job's 60. Chromium's 21.1 MB pass 10 minutes at any rate above about 36 kB/s (CI run 28's mirror sent 48.6 kB/s). At platforms run 3's rates a Chromium install that misses ends at about its 10 minutes. Such a run fails, is rerun, and does not count (2.14, The move, choice 4). Misses are rare once the cache holds the packages. Checked here on 2026-11-06, in this repository's container (Ubuntu 24.04.5, apt 2.8.3, with Docker's `docker-clean` in place) with `Dir::Cache` and `Dir::State::Lists` in a folder of `target/tmp`: `apt-get install --download-only sl` downloaded `sl_5.02-1_amd64.deb` (12.7 kB), owned by `root` with mode 0644. Run again, it printed `Need to get 0 B/12.7 kB of archives.`, also after the file was copied out and back with `cp` as `root`. Cut to 6,000 bytes, the file was downloaded again. With one byte changed and its size kept, apt printed `Need to get 0 B/12.7 kB of archives.`: apt 2.8 does not hash a file it already has (`pkgAcqArchive::QueueNext` in apt 2.8's source). Renamed to `sl_5.01-1_amd64.deb`, the file was left in place and `sl_5.02-1_amd64.deb` was downloaded. The runner image's build scripts (actions/runner-images at be74a77) end with `apt-get clean` and set nothing that removes downloaded archives. Only CI can check that on the runner. The same scripts set how apt gives up on a mirror. `configure-apt.sh` writes `Acquire::Retries "1"` and http and https timeouts of 15 s to `/etc/apt/apt.conf.d/zz-retries` (runner-images #14643 and #14726, merged in September 2026, before the oldest `ubuntu-24.04` image named here, 20261012.334.1). `configure-apt-sources.sh` lists `azure.archive.ubuntu.com`, `archive.ubuntu.com` and `security.ubuntu.com`, in that order, in `/etc/apt/apt-mirrors.txt`. apt moves a download to the next mirror after two tries that each get nothing for 15 s. Platforms run 3's `libavfilter9`, which stalled twice and then came from `archive.ubuntu.com`, is that failover at work. It helps only when a mirror sends nothing for 15 s. A mirror that keeps sending a few kB/s, as run 3's did at about 39 kB/s, never trips the 15 s. Only the cache takes those downloads off it. The first runs record, for each of the three installs, the entry's size (the `<size>` of the save's `Sent <sent> of <size>` lines), the `Need to get` line of the miss and of the first hit, the `Fetched` line of its `apt-get update` and each install's time, and the time of the Tauri packages' install in `rust` and `desktop` (test 131). The CI run on wave H2's branch (42218307369, 2026-11-06, commit d09fb1a) ran the three installs first. Each restore logged `Cache not found for input keys: apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-<browser>-playwright-1.64.0`. The copy into apt's cache was skipped, and so were the copy out and the save, which run only on `main`. `apt-get update` fetched 12.9 MB in 1 s in each job. The installs printed `Need to get 21.1 MB of archives.` for Chromium, 48.8 MB for Firefox and 114 MB for WebKit, fetched in 1 s, 2 s and 10 s, in install steps of 20 s, 18 s and 40 s. CI run 30 (42222611157, on 80ae66f, 2026-11-06), the first run on `main` with the browser cache, passed all 20 jobs, `deploy` included, in 15 min 7 s of wall time (13:04:58 to 13:20:05). Each restore missed with the branch run's line. `apt-get update` fetched 12.9 MB in 1 s in each job. The installs printed the same `Need to get` lines (21.1, 48.8 and 114 MB), fetched in 9 s, 1 s and 9 s, in install steps of 27 s, 25 s and 29 s. In each job the copy out, which runs without `sudo`, and the save passed. The copy into apt's cache was skipped on each miss. The saves logged `Sent 21102075 of 21102075`, `Sent 48803004 of 48803004` and `Sent 113992514 of 113992514` before `Cache saved with key: apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-<browser>-playwright-1.64.0`. Each entry is within 1 percent of its install's `Need to get` size. The runner image keeps apt's downloads in `/var/cache/apt/archives`, and the runner's user can read them. Run 30 showed none of the defects that test 131 names. No save warned `Path Validation Error`, no copy step failed, and no entry saved after a miss is more than 10 percent off its install's total. The Tauri installs took 45 s in `rust` (`Fetched 61.2 MB in 15s`) and 31 s in `desktop` (`Fetched 61.2 MB in 12s`). The `rust` job took 6 min 38 s in all. An install on a hit that fails because apt or dpkg rejects a file under `/var/cache/apt/archives` is recorded here with its log lines, and every entry of its set is deleted (3.2, What apt checks). An install on a hit that ends at a timeout is the mirror's. It is recorded here and rerun, and no entry is deleted. Until wave H3, an entry whose hit showed a `Need to get` part over half its total was deleted too. Since wave H3 a run on `main` saves such an update in a new entry (3.2). The record of the Tauri installs (3.2, ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-06, choice 8): the CI run on wave H2's branch (42218307369, 2026-11-06, commit d09fb1a: the tree of 80ae66f with a temporary push trigger for the branch and the experiment's files) gave the first Tauri install over 10 minutes. All 19 jobs passed, and `deploy` was skipped. The run took 32 min 10 s of wall time (12:31:21 to 13:03:31). The Rust job took 31 min 53 s. Its step `Install the Tauri Linux packages` took 26 min 26 s (12:33:24 to 12:59:50). `apt-get update` fetched 12.9 MB in 1 s. The install then printed `0 upgraded, 173 newly installed`, `Need to get 61.2 MB of archives.` and `Fetched 46.6 MB in 26min 4s (29.8 kB/s)`, every package from `azure.archive.ubuntu.com` (107 from `noble` and 66 from `noble-updates`). `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-0` 2.52.6-0ubuntu0.24.04.1 (25.5 MB, `noble-updates`) took 391 s, then `Ign:70`, then a second `Get` from the same mirror for 294 s. `libjavascriptcoregtk-4.1-0` (8.2 MB) took 216 s. The runner image's failover (above) never moved to `archive.ubuntu.com`: the mirror kept sending. In the same run `desktop` installed the same 173 packages on another runner in 7 s (`Fetched 61.2 MB in 7s (8660 kB/s)`, the step 30 s). Without the Rust job's install, the run's last job would have ended at 12:45:35, 14 min 14 s after the push. Under the owner's rule, wave H3 did not wait for a second record. The rust job is in `deploy.needs`, runs on every push and pull request, and has a 60-minute job timeout with no step timeout on this install. The first record took more than half of that with a warm Rust cache. The same mirror gave installs of 21 min 17 s and 53 min 17 s in platforms run 3 (40509561577), and 6 install timeouts in 57 jobs of the Firefox experiment. A 53-minute install with a cold Rust build would end the rust job at its timeout and hold `deploy`. Waiting for a second record meant waiting for such a run. Wave H3 gives the Tauri installs of `rust` and `desktop` the apt cache of 3.2. The Linux `bundle` row restores their entry and saves none. The browser caches move to the key of 3.2, which rolls with each save on `main` after a download. The first runs record what test 131 names. A Tauri install that takes more than 10 minutes, or runs into its job's 60, is recorded here with its run, job, `Need to get` line and rate (3.2). Checked here on 2026-11-06: in this repository's container, `apt-get install --reinstall --download-only libxdo-dev libxdo3`, with `Dir::Cache` and `Dir::State::Lists` in a folder of `target/tmp`, wrote `libxdo-dev_1%3a3.20160805.1-5build1_amd64.deb` and `libxdo3_1%3a3.20160805.1-5build1_amd64.deb`. `dpkg-query -W -f '${Package}_${Version}_${Architecture}.deb\n'` with each colon written `%3a` gives the same two names. The copy out of 3.2, run with `bash -e` as GitHub runs it, against scratch folders in place of apt's cache and `~/apt-archives`, with the real `dpkg-query` and those two files, wrote `downloaded=true` on a miss and after an update, and the new entry held only the installed versions. It wrote nothing on a hit with nothing new, with nothing downloaded and no entry, and with an entry that held only a version nothing installed. With a stand-in `dpkg-query` that exits 1, the step failed and wrote nothing. Without its first line, `set -o pipefail`, the same step passed with no output. Run 30 passed, in attempt 1, every check that test 129 makes of the first CI run on `main`. The run's 15 min 7 s (above) is within section 5's 25 minutes. It started 2 s after the push. Each browser install's download took under 10 s on its miss.
