There `OCCT (macos-15)` waited 7 min 8 s for a runner (12:31:22 to 12:38:30), and both rows of `rust-platforms` started after it, at 12:38:43 and 12:38:44. They restored the same two keys with `full match: true` and took 6 min 5 s on `windows-2025` and 4 min 8 s on `macos-15`. In that run `e2e-engines` took 1 min 36 s for `firefox` and 2 min 21 s for `webkit`. The branch run's last job was `rust`, which ended at 13:03:30 (the record of the Tauri installs, above).
17. **Headers of 304 responses in production (a human check).** Vite's servers answered revalidations with a 304 without COOP and COEP and sent `Vary: Origin` on every response until the plugin of 2.7, and in WebKit the core failed after a reload and the preloaded font was fetched twice, most likely because of them (ui.md open issue 41). Whether Cloudflare Pages behaves the same is not known, since its hosts cannot be reached from the development containers. To check by hand: whether a Pages 304 carries the `_headers` values (`curl -sI https://<pages host>/assets/core.worker-<hash>.js` for the `ETag`, then `curl -sI -H 'If-None-Match: <etag>'` on the same URL), and whether the font response varies on `Origin` (its `Vary` header); and whether Safari 17 keeps the core after a reload, after "Restart core" (ui.md 4.4, which creates the worker again in the same page) and in a Private Browsing window (an ephemeral session, like Playwright's). If a Pages 304 lacks the headers, the hardening option is `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` for `/assets/*` in `ui/public/_headers`, so hashed files are never revalidated; it changes `EXPECTED_HEADERS`, `check:dist` and its tests (2.6, 2.7) and waits for the owner (section 8).
18. **What an exported file holds** (resolved by 2.8's STEP round trip and test 107, [ADR 0046](../adr/0046-examples-exported-to-every-format.md) option (c), the owner's choice of 2026-11-04). Runner test 5 checked that every example that builds exports to each format and that each report names the file written. It read none of the files. Technical design section 16's STEP round trip, the volume kept within 1e-6 relative through an export and an import, was tested on `box.ptsm` alone (cli.md test 41, engine.md test 89), whose faces are planar. Runner test 5 now imports each example's STEP file back with `Session::import_step`, in the headless session of the worker that ran the export, right after that export. It compares the measured volume with the sum of the volumes of the expected bodies the export takes, within 2.8's tolerance for numbers that are not both integers. Measured by hand on 2026-10-28 over the 232 examples that built then: the worst volume difference was 9.0e-13 relative, the worst area difference 3.1e-13, and every face count was unchanged. Measured again on 2026-11-04 over the 270 examples that build, with the built CLI's STEP files and a scratch test in the test binary, built with the dev profile as the runner is: every import succeeded, and the worst volume difference was 9.0e-13 relative (`cone/pointed` and `cone/apex-below-tolerance`). Open issue 4 has the time the imports take. The bytes of mesh files stay io.md's tests' and, for two 3MF exports, engine.md test 160's (2.8).
19. **The policy outside Chromium and on Pages (a human check).** The policy of 2.7 was measured in Chromium only: on 2026-10-30, the 225 E-tests then in the repository that do not check Playwright's own projects passed in Chromium against a preview that sent the five headers (E-156 was left to the repository's configuration), and a scratch run of E-161's steps saw no violation in the page or the core worker. That run left out what `pnpm e2e` leaves out, the `@visual` and `@perf` tests (ui.md 6.6, 6.7), and the test pages of `viewport.spec.ts`, P-12 and the engine page came from a dev server, which sends no policy (2.7), so those ran without one; the `bypassCSP` of the visual tests (ui.md 6.6) was measured in Chromium against a scratch page that sent the policy: without it a screenshot's `stylePath` was refused (one `style-src-elem` violation, the style not applied), with it the style applied and nothing was refused. The `firefox` and `webkit` projects run the `@engines` E-tests against the same preview in CI's `e2e-engines` (2.14), so a policy that refused the core, the sketch overlay's chunk or a stylesheet in either engine fails those tests there; a refusal that breaks nothing would show only in that engine's console, since E-161 and the page check every E-test ends with (ui.md 6.3) run in Chromium alone. Pages' hosts cannot be reached from the development containers, so whether the deployed headers are those of `_headers` is not known either. To check by hand on the production host: `curl -sI` of `/`, `/assets/core.worker-<hash>.js` and `/core/partsmith_core.wasm` shows each of the five headers once, with the values of 2.7 (`X-Content-Type-Options` and `Referrer-Policy` not joined with Pages' defaults); the page source of `/` holds no script that Cloudflare injected (Web Analytics' beacon from `static.cloudflareinsights.com`, Rocket Loader, Zaraz or Email Obfuscation, 2.7); and the app, opened in Chrome, Firefox and Safari 17 with the developer console shown, logs no content security policy message while a model opens, a sketch is entered and left, a print check runs and an STL is exported. `curl -sI` of `/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` shows `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8` (open issue 11). In Firefox and Safari 17 the reviewer runs Third-party notices from the palette and records with ui.md open issue 44 whether the tab wraps its long lines. The performance tests (ui.md 6.7) run once under the policy on the reference machine, by the commands of `ui/e2e/README.md`, and their numbers are compared with the last run without it.
20. **What the coverage checks leave out.** Runner tests 6 to 9 check what a file can show by its structure (2.8, ADR 0051), and leave out: the role of each sketch point a field picks (a point, a line's start or end, a center; the hole examples use all four, which features/hole.md's tests pin), the reserved references `origin`, `x_axis` and `y_axis` in each constraint form, each finding code of each check (`fits_rotated` and `footprint_too_large` are both in the corpus; `open_edges`, `misoriented_edges` and `inside_out` are not, and no solid the kernel builds is expected to give them), the drawing tools other than the seven of features/sketch.md tests 44 to 50 (ui.md U-247; the slot and tangent arc tools come after M1), the option values of the benchmark document, and the boolean mode flags, which no manifest hint lists with options (`reverse` of `extrude`, `hole`, `revolve` and `circular_pattern`, `flip` of `datum_plane` and `split_body`, `keep_tools` of `boolean`, `direction.reverse` and `second_direction.reverse` of `linear_pattern`, and `distance.flip` of `chamfer`, each `true` in an example of its type on 2026-10-30). Each would be a new runner test with its examples, and none is asked for by technical design section 16's list.
21. **The dependency source archives outside this container.** 2.13 measured the archives and built the web core, the web UI and the Linux desktop crates from them here. Seven parts wait for CI, a person or the first tag. (a) No build from the desktop archives has run on Windows or macOS. The desktop crate set is the union of the three graphs that the notices list and holds each platform's crates. Test 97 records the first such build. (b) The first deployment that holds `/dependencies/` (test 98): its upload count and time, and whether Pages sends a part without a `content-encoding`. That was the deployment of CI run 41926888431 (0ae7337, 2026-11-04, https://6db57554.partsmith.pages.dev). Its `deploy` took 46 s and passed `Check the dependency source archives`. Its log read `Uploaded 15 files (8 already uploaded) (18.17 sec)`. On 2026-11-06 CI run 28 (42156836018) deployed a3df7f5 to production in 42 s, with `Uploaded 14 files (12 already uploaded) (12.87 sec)`. Test 98's requests have not run on either deployment. (c) The first release draft with the parts, whose assets a person downloads and checks with `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` before publishing it (3.6). (d) The bytes depend on the runner's cargo, GNU tar and gzip. `sources` does not compare them with 2.13's SHA-256s, unlike the OCCT archive, because they change with every lock file change. `deploy` checks only that the parts match their `SHA256SUMS` and join into whole archives. (e) The `desktop` scope runs in CI in `release.yml`'s job `sources`, in every dry run and tag run (2.13). Its first run in CI is the first dry run after this change, which this issue records with the job's time. Tests 91 and 92 run its logic on fixtures. A `draft` that fails runs again with `gh run rerun <id> --failed` (3.6). (f) Since 2026-11-05 the archives also hold `tauri-cli` and its crates, lightningcss's source with its crates, and `bexpand`'s crate (2.13). `tauri-cli`'s offline install in release and the lightningcss builds with the toolchains their tags pin ran here on Linux from the script's archives (test 114, 2.13, Built from the archives alone). The same on Windows and macOS wait for whoever can run them. The bytes also depend on the runner's git. The time the archives take on the runner is part of (g). (g) The source with every artifact (2.13, the owner's decision of 2026-11-05 on CI's artifacts). The first runs after this change record, for each workflow, the time of its job `sources` against section 5 (3 minutes in `ci.yml` and `platforms.yml`, and 6 in `release.yml`, whose job has no Cargo cache, vendors about 1,500 crates and records its peak disk too), the size and time of the upload of `sources-web` or `sources-desktop`, the time `web` and `draft` take to download it, and each run's wall time against section 5 (CI's 25 minutes, which 2.13 expects to grow by about 0.5 to 1 minute). Test 121 lists the checks. The first runs, on 2026-11-06, both on a3df7f5: in CI run 28 (42156836018), `sources` (`Source archives (web)`) took 1 min 22 s from start to end, its upload 4 s, and in platforms run 15 (42156838426) 1 min 17 s, its upload 2 s, both within 3 minutes. CI run 28 passed all 14 jobs. `web` took 42 s in all, its download of `sources-web` included. `deploy` passed `Check the Partsmith source archive`. The run took 24 min 9 s from push to `deploy` finished. That is 51 s under section 5's 25 minutes and 7 min 11 s more than run 41785469932's 16 min 58 s, where 2.13 expected 0.5 to 1 minute more. Its critical path was `sources`, `core-wasm`, `e2e` and `deploy`. `core-wasm` started 1 min 28 s after the push, 3 s after `sources` ended (the OCCT restores had ended within 13 s). That start was 1 min 14 s later than in run 41785469932. The job took 5 min 50 s against 7 min 14 s and ended 10 s earlier. `e2e` took 16 min 4 s against 8 min 54 s. Its `pnpm exec playwright install --with-deps chromium` took 7 min 42 s against 19 s (open issue 16), and `pnpm e2e` 7 min 56 s against 8 min 21 s. The install grew by 7 min 23 s, more than the whole run did. `deploy` took 42 s. Platforms run 15 took 9 min 44 s against its 60 minutes. The size of each upload is not recorded here. `release.yml`'s `sources` and `draft`'s download wait for the owner's release dry run. Test 121's request to production waits for the owner.

## 8. Needs human review

- **Cloudflare setup** (one time): create the Pages project with production branch `main`, create an API token with only "Account, Cloudflare Pages, Edit", add `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` as repository secrets, and optionally `CLOUDFLARE_PAGES_PROJECT`. Decide whether the account belongs to a person or a project organization (technical design section 19). A project connected to the repository in the dashboard has its automatic builds paused (3.3 item 6). Status 2026-10-16: done. `main` is the default branch, the Git-connected Pages project has its own builds paused and receives CI's Wrangler deploys, and both secrets are set. The owner made the repository public and accepted ADR 0034 option A, B-2's notices and the notices rule of `check:dist` are on `main`, and no guard holds deployments back: the preview access policy and the production hold-back that ADR 0034's recommendation named as interim guards for the private period are not set up. After four red runs, CI passed on `main` in run 38939853875 (commit 55dde8a), whose `deploy` job made the first production deployment on 2026-10-16, before the source route of 2.13 was in place, which the recommendation's hold-back waited for. That deployment, like every one before the source stamp of 2.13 lands, has no `/SOURCE.txt`; the repository's deployments page lists the commit of each production deployment. Until 2.13 is on `main`, a pull request that does not carry it deploys a preview with the web statement now on `main`, which says a preview is built from the head commit of its pull request (it is built from GitHub's merge commit, 2.13), and with no stamp; so the change that lands 2.13 is best the first pull request to run CI, or else the owner turns on the preview access policy (Settings, General) until 2.13 is on `main`. README.md gives the production address once the owner confirms the subdomain (2.12 part 3). Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: the production address is `https://partsmith.pages.dev`, which the owner confirmed from the Cloudflare dashboard. README.md names it (2.12 part 3). Decided by the owner on 2026-11-05: the Pages project stays on the owner's own Cloudflare account.
- **Branch protection** on `main` requiring the CI jobs of 2.4. Status 2026-10-16: not set (`main` is unprotected). `main` passed CI in run 38939853875, so the jobs can be required now without blocking every merge. Protection also keeps force pushes and deletion of `main` off (GitHub's default for a protected branch), which the stamps of production deployments rely on; a tag ruleset that blocks deleting or moving `v*` tags does the same for releases. Status 2026-11-04: still not set (the branches API reports `protected: false`), an owner action. The required checks include `Source archives (web)` since wave G, and since wave H the six checks of the jobs that moved from the platforms workflow: `OCCT (windows-2025)`, `OCCT (macos-15)`, `Rust tests (windows-2025)`, `Rust tests (macos-15)`, `End-to-end tests (firefox)` and `End-to-end tests (webkit)` (2.14, The move). The owner adds them. A failed `sources` skips `core-wasm`, `web`, `e2e`, `e2e-engines` and `deploy`. GitHub counts a skipped required check as passed (3.1).
- **Third-party notices** (2.9, ADR 0033): what they list and leave out; the two OCCT statements; the four notices of code OCCT includes from other projects (flex; DELABELLA, also copied into partsmith-occt-sys by ADR 0039, under the same entry; David M. Gay's strtod; Unicode's ConvertUTF); the texts given to the 22 crates that ship none (a standard Apache-2.0 or MPL-2.0 text, or a committed copy from the crate's repository); the WebView2 SDK loader that `webview2-com-sys` links statically on Windows, whose license the owner confirms against the NuGet package that crate version ships (checked 2026-10-16: the `x64/WebView2LoaderStatic.lib` of `webview2-com-sys` 0.39.1 is byte for byte the one of `Microsoft.Web.WebView2` 1.0.3800.47, the SDK version its repository's `update-bindings` names at the packaged commit, and that package's `LICENSE.txt`, a BSD-3-Clause text with Microsoft's copyright line, is the committed `ui/scripts/notices/webview2-com-sys-0.39.1/LICENSE.txt`; the package's `NOTICE.txt`, for Antlr3.Runtime and StringTemplate4 under BSD-3-Clause, covers its `tools/wv2winrt` code generator, not the loader, so the notices leave it out); the text of `block2`, `objc2`, `objc2-encode` and `objc2-foundation`, whose repository's only license file, `LICENSE.md`, names MIT and links to it but holds neither the MIT text nor a copyright line, so their entries carry that file as it is (the owner may add, in each of the four `ui/scripts/notices/crates/<name>/` folders, the MIT text that `LICENSE.md` links to, `https://opensource.org/license/MIT`, which still has no copyright line); the same `LICENSE.md` says the objc2 crates are derived from Apple SDKs and that it is unclear whether the Xcode license allows distributing such derived works, which concerns every crate of that repository the macOS bundle compiles in (`block2`, `dispatch2`, `objc2`, `objc2-encode`, `objc2-exception-helper`, `objc2-foundation` and the six `objc2-*` framework crates): a question that was to go to the owner's lawyer with those of ADR 0034, until the owner accepted the caveat as built and chose to ask no lawyer on 2026-11-04; the Unicode data license of `regex-syntax`'s tables (Unicode-DFS-2016, which `deny.toml` does not allow and `cargo-deny` never sees, since the crate declares `MIT OR Apache-2.0`): allow it with an ADR, or accept it as data rather than code; whether the NSIS installer code (NSIS, its LZMA module, `nsis_tauri_utils`) gets entries (open issue 10); the shipping places (bundle resource, release asset, web build), with no license page or agreement in any installer; and the texts reproduced as their packages ship them. The ICU4X crates' Unicode-3.0 texts hold em dashes, which the root AGENTS.md forbids in any file; ADR 0033 sets out three ways (amend the rule with the wording `Never write the em dash character in any file, except third-party license texts copied verbatim: the notices that pnpm -C ui notices writes and the copies it reads under ui/scripts/notices/ (delivery.md 2.9).`, which it proposes; write U+2014 as `--`, which alters license texts; or hold the notices back, which blocks B-2) for the owner to choose. And the line `pnpm -C ui notices` in the Commands block of the root AGENTS.md (2.9, "Kept current"), which the owner adds when this work merges `main`. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: accepted as built (ADR 0033). The em dash rule takes way 1, which exempts third-party license texts copied verbatim. On 2026-11-05 the owner decided that the root AGENTS.md's rule names the exception in ADR 0033's wording, and gave wave G's workflows-docs track leave to write it there (ADR 0033). That track wrote it on 2026-11-06. The four objc2 entries keep `LICENSE.md` as it is, with no MIT text added, and their Apple SDK caveat stands as built. The Unicode data license of `regex-syntax`'s tables is accepted as built, as data, with `deny.toml` unchanged. Whether the NSIS installer code needs entries was question 3 of ADR 0034. Later on 2026-11-04 the owner answered it with the strictest reading and no lawyer: it does. The desktop notices list it for Windows (2.9 input 6, open issue 10). Question 3 also named the AppImage runtime, which no bundle carries since the AppImage left M1 (ADR 0034 choice 1). The root AGENTS.md has had the `pnpm -C ui notices` line since 2026-11-04, added with the owner's leave.

This spec carries it out with the source stamp of every web deployment and the OCCT source archive of every release (2.13, tests 52 to 58), the statements of 2.9 that name them, the web notices in the `core-wasm` artifact (2.4) and 7-day artifacts for the bundles (2.5), which test 59 checks; until the archive step is in `draft`, a release stays a draft (3.6, checklist item 1). - **CI's artifacts and OCCT's source** (open issue 15, ADR 0034's amendment of 2026-11-04 on OCCT's source in every deployment). Status: closed. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-05: option (b), widened. Every CI, platforms and release run that uploads an artifact with OCCT's compiled code inside (`core-wasm`, `web-dist`, `playwright-report` and `playwright-report-{browser}`, `bundle-*`, dry runs included) also uploads, in the same run and with the same retention, OCCT's source archive, the crate and npm source archives of that artifact's scope, and Partsmith's own source archive. 2.13 (The source with every artifact) specifies it, with four choices by the owner's rule of 2026-11-05 (ADR 0034's amendment of that date): a job `sources` in each workflow, which every job that uploads such an artifact needs and which makes the OCCT archive's cache itself; one source artifact per run and scope, `sources-web` or `sources-desktop`, which `web` and `draft` download instead of making the archives again; `partsmith-source.tar.gz` in every release too; and the source artifact kept 8 days, one more than the artifacts (GitHub counts each artifact's days from its own upload), with failed jobs rerun alone only within 12 hours of the `sources` job (tests 117 to 121). The options were (a) keep the artifacts as they were, (b) add the source as an artifact of its own to every run that uploads one of them, and (c) keep them 1 day instead of 7 (open issue 15).
- **`.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` and the archives** (2.4, 2.5, 2.13, 3.6). Status: closed. `deploy` "fails without deploying unless `ui/dist/SOURCE.txt` names the run's commit, `ui/dist/occt-<version>-source.tar.gz` holds the source of the pinned OCCT commit, `ui/dist/dependencies/` holds the parts its `SHA256SUMS` lists and `ui/dist/partsmith-source.tar.gz` holds the source of the run's commit (delivery.md 2.13)"; in the `release.yml` line, `sources` (the artifact `sources-desktop`: OCCT's source archive, `partsmith-source.tar.gz` and the parts of the desktop dependency source archives, kept 8 days, delivery.md 2.13) before `bundle`, and `draft` "(a draft GitHub Release with the bundles, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt`, the files of `sources-desktop` and `SHA256SUMS`, published by a human)", and "A manual run (`workflow_dispatch`) is a dry run that builds the bundles and uploads them with `sources-desktop`, with no release."; in the `platforms.yml` line, "Artifacts: `sources-web`, kept 8 days, and, kept 7 days, `core-wasm` and `playwright-report-<browser>` on an end-to-end failure."; in the rules, "Every job that compiles with cargo runs `rustup show active-toolchain || rustup toolchain install` and then `Swatinem/rust-cache`; the `sources` jobs run only `cargo vendor` and `cargo metadata` and take the toolchain step alone." and two new rules, "Every job that uploads an artifact holding OCCT's compiled code needs the job `sources`, directly or through the jobs it needs. A run then never holds that code without its source (delivery.md 2.13, ADR 0034)." and "A job that uploads OCCT's compiled code is rerun with `gh run rerun <id> --failed` only within 12 hours of the end of the run's `sources` job. - **The source of NSIS's LZMA module** (2.9 input 6, open issue 10, ADR 0034's amendment of 2026-11-04 on the strictest reading). Status: closed. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-05: option (c). The Windows installer compresses with zlib (`bundle.windows.nsis.compression` in `tauri.conf.json`, desktop.md 2.8 and test 33). No CPL-1.0 code ships. makensis builds the setup executable and its uninstaller on NSIS's zlib stub. None of the plugins the installer calls holds the LZMA module. The NSIS entry of the desktop notices names the zlib decompressor under `Zlib`, and its copy of `COPYING` leaves out the bzip2 and LZMA parts, the CPL-1.0 text among them (2.9 input 6, test 126). No other code that ships needs that text. Wave G's windows-runtime track builds it. The options were (a) as built, with the NSIS release folder on SourceForge as the place of the LZMA module's source, (b) every release also attaching `nsis-3.11-src.tar.bz2`, checked against a recorded SHA-256, and (c) zlib compression, which leaves the CPL-1.0 code out.

- **The compiler's runtime in the Windows executable** (2.9 input 6, open issue 10, ADR 0034's amendment of 2026-11-04 on the strictest reading, ADR 0038). Status: closed. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-05: option (b). The desktop notices gain the Windows-only entry `Microsoft Visual C++ runtime (libcmt, libvcruntime and libcpmt), in the Windows app`, right after the WebView2 loader, with no version, `see texts`, the address of Microsoft's license terms for Visual Studio and a text of Partsmith's own on the Distributable Code terms (2.9 input 6, test 125). It does not name the UCRT, which the app takes from Windows (below). The owner's rule settled the entry's text (ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-05, choice 2). Microsoft ships the static runtime with no notice text. Its terms are not an open source license with a text to copy. The options were (i) a copy of the Visual Studio license terms, long, under Microsoft's copyright and different for each edition; (ii) the address alone, with no text, which the layout of 2.9 does not allow; and (iii) a short text of Partsmith's own that names the code, its owner and its terms and the conditions those terms set on whoever passes the code on, as CPython's Windows build does in its license file. (iii) is the industry practice and the safest: a reader learns the conditions, and Partsmith copies no text it may not copy. The terms ask whoever passes the code on to have the people they give it to agree to terms that protect it. By the owner's rule, the notices file that every bundle installs carries those terms to them, with no license page to accept, as CPython's Windows build does with its license file (ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-05, choice 4). The options were the notices file alone, a license page to accept (`bundle.licenseFile`), which reverses ADR 0033 and desktop.md 2.8, and leaving that condition unanswered. Wave G's windows-runtime track builds it. The owner's options were (a) the runtime read as part of the compiler, with no entry, (b) a Windows-only entry in the desktop notices, and (c) the DLL runtime of ADR 0038 option (ii).

Decided by the owner on 2026-11-05: the schedule (daily at 04:23 UTC), the runner time and the skip rule, accepted as built.
- **The platforms workflow** (2.14, ADR 0038): its weekly schedule and runner time (the sum of its job times, 18 min 42 s for the second run, warm except part of the Windows Rust cache, with 7 min 26 s of wall time, and 1 h 33 min 18 s for the third, warm, with 1 h 0 min 22 s of wall time, 1 h 14 min 34 s of it in its two browser installs on a slow mirror, open issue 16, against the one and a half to two hours a week ADR 0038 estimated, not billed on this public repository) and its caches, which share GitHub's 10 GB with CI's (open issue 16); the two browser builds `e2e-engines` installs as test tools from `@playwright/test` 1.64.0, Firefox 157.0 (MPL 2.0) and WebKit 27.2 (LGPL 2.1 and BSD), which run only on the runners and are never distributed, as Playwright's Chromium today; the promotion rule, under which a job joins `deploy.needs` and the owner adds it to the required checks, and a row that failed at its browser install's timeout counts only once rerun (2.14); the release checklist's new requirement of a passing platforms run (3.6, item 2); the supported browsers and minimum versions of ui.md 1.1 as a promise to users; and the C runtime the Windows app links (open issue 1 (e)), whose two options change either every OCCT cache key and `.cargo/config.toml` or `tauri.conf.json` and the installer. From the runs' findings (open issue 1 (d)): the portable triangulation of ADR 0039 (MIT code copied into the repository and changed, its notices entry of 2.9, and the shim's glue to OCCT's mesher), and the thin-wall rule of ADR 0041, whose 30 degree angle print.md section 8 lists; OCCT built without fused operations is not needed: the thin-wall rule makes the cone results independent of their rim nodes, whatever moves them, and the flag could not have fixed Windows, which fuses none and differed through its C runtime's `cos` (kernel.md open issue 14); the wall-time budget of section 5, which the second run met in 7 min 26 s and the third, warm, missed by 22 s on a slow mirror; with the browser install's 10-minute bound (2.14, open issue 16), such a run fails at about 16 minutes, and a warm run that passes stays under about 18 (about 7.5 minutes plus at most 10 of install), so the owner may lower it to 20 minutes, which also leaves room for a cold Rust cache while the installs stay quick; and the production checks of open issue 17, with their hardening option. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: accepted as proposed (ADR 0038): the weekly and on-demand runs outside the required gate, their runner time and caches, releases waiting for a passing run, the promotion rule, the browser minimums with macOS 13, the 60-minute budget, and Firefox 157.0 and WebKit 27.2 as test tools. The Windows app links the C and C++ runtime statically everywhere (open issue 1 (e), ADR 0038 option (i)). Technical design section 14's full-rebuild gate is judged on the workflow's macOS arm64 runner, by a timed `bench50` run from an empty cache (`full-rebuild`, 2.14, ADR 0054). A result over 1 s goes to the owner with an ADR that raises the budget or schedules speed work. Wave F builds both. ADR 0039's Delabella copy is kept as built, and ADR 0041's 30 degree angle is accepted. The production checks of open issue 17 stay checks at the gate. Every job of the workflow but `full-rebuild`, which no run has had yet and which is never promoted, passed its two latest runs on `main` (41456376800, and 41751363019 in attempt 2, after a rerun of its failed `firefox` job, ui.md open issue 41; 2.14's promotion rule does not count that rerun). No wave F track moves them into `ci.yml` (2.14, promotion): the move edits checklist item 2's copy in `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md`, which waits for the owner's leave, and section 5's CI budget changes only with human review (gap list B-8). Decided by the owner on 2026-11-05: the first median of `full-rebuild` on `macos-15`, 1.477 s (platforms run 14, 41926898084, on 0ae7337), is over 1 s, and a new ADR sets the budget the runner holds from it (ADR 0054 decision 6, option (a)), with speed work recorded as later work. Jobs move into `ci.yml` under the promotion rule once they pass two platforms runs after wave F (run 14 is the first). Wave G moves none. Built in wave H (2.14, The move, ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-06): every job and row but `full-rebuild` passed platforms runs 14 and 15. The jobs moved into `ci.yml` with both rows and joined `deploy.needs`. CI's wall time is estimated at about 17 to 24 minutes, within its 25. Its budget stays. Four choices are the owner's rule's: CI keeps `cancel-in-progress: true`, a CI run is rerun only while it is the newest of its concurrency group, the copy of the macOS OCCT row has a name of its own, and section 5 does not count a run whose browser install ended at its timeout (2.14, The move, choices 1 to 4).
- **`deny.toml` advisory exceptions** RUSTSEC-2024-0429 and RUSTSEC-2024-0370 for Tauri's GTK3 stack (3.2), a change to the dependency policy. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: accepted with Tauri 2 (ADR 0014).
- **Release policy**: tags `vX.Y.Z` on commits with green CI, a dry run from `main` before each tag, draft releases published by a human, unsigned bundles until signing is set up, macOS arm64 only. Its cost: each release builds the bundles twice, in the dry run and in the tag run, with bundle jobs of about 20 minutes each (an estimate the first dry run replaces with measured figures), and a cold OCCT build adds up to the cold budget of section 5. The repository is public, so its standard GitHub-hosted runners, Windows and macOS included, bill nothing (ADR 0034), and the cost is the release's wall time; open issue 13 would halve the bundle time. A hung job ends at its `timeout-minutes` (2.4, 2.5), 150 minutes for a bundle job. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: accepted as written.
- **Build tools pinned here** (`tauri-cli` 2.12.2, Wrangler 4.149.0, maturin 1.15.0, pytest 9.1.1) and the new actions (`upload-artifact`, `download-artifact`, `setup-python`, `wrangler-action`, `msvc-dev-cmd`). Decided by the owner on 2026-11-05: accepted as built.
- **Examples rules**: the expected-results format and the 1e-6 tolerance, the bless workflow, and that an asset is committed once and never rewritten, other bytes for the same model being a new asset under their own hash (2.8); and whether runner test 5 also imports each STEP file back and compares its volume (open issue 18, ADR 0046 option (c)). Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: runner test 5 also imports each STEP file back and checks its volume within technical design section 16's tolerance, which 2.8 (STEP round trip) specifies and test 107 checks. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-05: the rest is accepted as built (the expected-results format with the 1e-6 tolerance, a bless only with `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1`, and assets never rewritten).
- **Examples blessed in phase 1 of the reference stability study** (its section 10.3 item 11, which asks for the sign-off of technical design section 16; [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-resolve-references-by-meaning.md), [ADR 0022](../adr/0022-split-regions-keep-their-area.md) and [ADR 0024](../adr/0024-diagnostics-with-actions-causes-and-ghosts.md)). Thirteen expected results were blessed again for the new messages (cli.md open issue 10): `chamfer/edge-lost`, `fillet/one-edge-lost` and `shell/one-face-lost` (lost references with their reason, candidates and fixes), `extrude/region-by-fingerprint` and `extrude/region-missing` (region texts and fixes), `import/cut-through-plate`, `import/join` and `import/new-body` (the asset name in `detail`), `sketch/face-ambiguous`, `sketch/face-lost`, `sketch/face-not-flat` and `sketch/face-plane-empty-body` (the Pick action, and the lost face of open issue 14), and `extrude/region-by-outer-boundary`; besides messages, they change only the last digits of volumes, areas and centers of mass. `extrude/region-by-outer-boundary` alone changes geometry: its redrawn hole circle now fills the hole (features/extrude.md test 31, features.md open issue 10), so its volume goes from 36858.407 to 40000, its faces from 7 to 6, its edges from 14 to 12, and its status from warning (`region_remapped`) to ok with the notice `region_split`. Its name describes the old match by outer boundary; it is renamed once features.md open issue 10 is decided. `sketch/face-ambiguous`, whose results now equal those of `sketch/face-lost`, is to be removed or renamed (open issue 14). New in the same step: the location `examples/references/` (2.8) with `line-redrawn`, `slot-inserted` and `notch-replaces-line`, each with its twin; `sketch/imported-face-ambiguous` with its STEP asset (open issues 5 and 14); and the `outline` of every stored region (`Region::reference()` of the unchanged sketch, [ADR 0025](../adr/0025-file-format-version-2.md)) in the 33 extrude and revolve examples whose regions are picks of their current sketch, so that their twins write regions as a host stores them: the short form `extrude(sketch_1, 30*mm, id="f-ext1")` where the profile is every region of the sketch, and the stored outline where the profile is a chosen region (`Region(..., outline=...)` in `extrude/region-with-hole`) or the extent has no dedicated form (the generic `feature("extrude", ...)` of `extrude/two-sides`, `two-sides-reverse` and `two-sides-one-zero`); their results are unchanged. Regions without outlines are left only where the stale key is the example's point (`extrude/region-by-fingerprint`, `extrude/region-missing` and `extrude/region-by-outer-boundary`) and in `bench/fifty-features`, which `ui/e2e/fixtures/part-50.ptsm` must equal byte for byte (ui.md 6.2), so its twin spells out its 13 regions until both files are updated together. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: accepted as built, with ADRs 0021, 0022 and 0024 signed off as built.
- **Examples blessed in phase 2 of the reference stability study** (its section 10.4 item 3, [ADR 0023](../adr/0023-frames-live-on-the-model.md)): `sketch/face-ambiguous`, `sketch/face-lost` and `sketch/imported-face-ambiguous` were blessed again because their `plane_fallback` warnings now offer `Attach to {description}` for each candidate before `Pick a face again` (engine.md section 3.22, features/sketch.md section 3.5); nothing else in them changes. New in its section 10.4 item 6: `references/moved-face-sketch` (R32 (a)), `references/sketch-after-cut` (R33) and `linear_pattern/leaning-edge` (R38), each with its twin and the references its setup picked (2.8). `sketch-after-cut` records `f-ext2` as ok with no message, where features/sketch.md test 22 expects `separate_solid`, which phase 3 step 4 adds; its expected results are blessed again in that step. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: accepted as built, with ADR 0023 signed off as built.
- **Examples blessed in phase 3 of the reference stability study** (its section 10.5 items 3 to 5 and 8, [ADR 0024](../adr/0024-diagnostics-with-actions-causes-and-ghosts.md)): twenty-one expected results were blessed again for the messages of features.md sections 3.15 and 4 and sketch.md section 4 and the fixes of engine.md section 3.22. `no_effect` says how nothing changed in `boolean/cut-misses` and `extrude/cut-misses` (`Beside`), `circular_pattern/count-one` and `linear_pattern/count-one` (`NoCopies`), `mirror/symmetric` (`Inside`) and `move_body/nothing-moves` (`NotMoved`); a lone smooth edge is skipped at its entry with Remove from Edges and no second `no_effect` in `chamfer/pipe-tee`, `chamfer/smooth-edge`, `fillet/pipe-tee`, `fillet/smooth-edge` and `fillet/smooth-edge-next-to-torus`; a hole that misses is named by its sketch coordinates, with Turn off Reverse where the other way reaches the body and no second `no_effect` for one point, in `hole/all-points-off-body`, `hole/point-off-body`, `hole/reverse` and `hole/swallowed-solid`; a join that floats warns `separate_solid` in `hole/swallowed-solid`, `shell/two-solids` and `references/sketch-after-cut` (Attach and the root `f-sk2`, features/sketch.md test 22); the three `import/` examples get the new missing-file text with the asset's path in `detail`; and `sketch/redundant-dimension` offers Make a reference and Delete. Besides messages and the statuses their warnings set (`f-box2` of `hole/swallowed-solid` and `shell/two-solids` and `f-ext2` of `references/sketch-after-cut` go from ok to warning), they change only the last digits of volumes, areas and centers of mass. New in its section 10.5 item 8: `hole/deleted-point` (R43 (b)), whose twin writes the hole in the generic form because no point of the sketch stands for the deleted `e-p2`. Like every twin, it equals the generated script: the generator gives no variable to a sketch or point that a feature looked up before falling back to the generic form (python-api.md section 3.8, rule 7), which also drops the unused `as sketch_1` from the twins of `extrude/two-sides`, `two-sides-reverse` and `two-sides-one-zero`. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: accepted as built, with ADR 0024 signed off as built.
- **Examples blessed for the thin-wall rule** ([ADR 0041](../adr/0041-thin-walls-measure-faces-that-face-each-other.md), print.md 3.9.6): `cone/pointed`, `cone/apex-below-tolerance`, `cone/frustum` and `cone/inverted` were blessed again because their thin wall findings at the rim, which measured the triangulation and differed on macOS and Windows, are gone (`cone/pointed` and `cone/apex-below-tolerance` from `4 warnings` to `Ready to print`, keeping their `degenerate_triangles` info; `cone/frustum` from `5 warnings` to `Ready to print`; `cone/inverted` from `8 warnings` to `2 warnings`, `no_bed_contact` and `small_detail`, keeping its info); nothing else in them changes. New: `shell/thin-wall`, `shell/open-top` with a 0.6 mm wall, whose two thin wall findings of 0.6 mm (faces `[0, 1, 2, 3, 4]` and `[5, 6, 7, 8, 9]`, every other check `ok`) the platforms now compare. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: accepted as built, with ADR 0041's 30 degree angle.
- **The 25 MiB plan** (3.5): each step's ADR when the gate first fails.
- **The web app's response headers** (2.7, [ADR 0049](../adr/0049-web-content-security-policy-and-accessibility-checks.md)), a security review: the content security policy adapted from the desktop's, with `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` for the core and `worker-src 'self'` for its worker; `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` in place of Pages' defaults; no policy on the dev server, which also serves the desktop's dev window; the Cloudflare features that inject scripts kept off; the headers ADR 0049 leaves out (a `Permissions-Policy`, Trusted Types, `X-Frame-Options`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`, removing Pages' `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`); and the checks of open issue 19. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-04: accepted as built, option (a) of ADR 0049 decision 1, the five headers enforced. The checks of open issue 19 stay checks at the gate.
